# Build tracker

**Not a specification.** `docs/` says what to build and why; this says what is done. Where the two disagree, `docs/` is right. [Plan.md](Plan.md) says what remains and in what order; where *those* two disagree, this one is right, because it is the record and that one goes stale.

**Rewritten 4 August 2026** from `b2cf367`, and **the build log below was compacted 9 August 2026** — 31 dated narrative entries covering 4–9 August became the changelog that follows. Nothing was dropped that is still load-bearing: what each item *did* is in [§4](#4-every-open-gap)'s closed table and in [Plan.md](Plan.md)'s own item sections, what each item *taught* is in [§5](#5-the-traps), and the full prose is in git history. The entries were a running log written forward; past a certain length that stops being a record and starts being a place where live items hide.

## 0. Build log

**5 August** — CI's integration workflow configured and verified end to end (`e7efa71`), the first PR run that actually tested anything · a second validation pass over A2/A3/B2/B3 before building them, finding GAP-37 and GAP-38 (`2822193`) · **A2** partner/banking/cash reads, 52 tests, and GAP-39 found as a live gap the endpoint works around · **A3** period/write-off/payment reads · **A4** customer-scoped reads · **A5** driver history reads · **B8** real Asgardeo auth — the item this plan had costed at "ten minutes of console work" while its whole client half was unbuilt · **B0** the `/more` hub and sign-out · **A9a** migration `0008` · **deployed**: `fleetsettle.com` and `qa.fleetsettle.com` both live · A6–A10 re-validated against the code ahead of building them.

**6 August** — **GAP-3 closed out of sequence**: `confirmDay` had been silently discarding real confirmations in production since P13, found by an independent review rather than by this tracker · **A6** the trip receivable · **A0/GAP-42**: every signup had been landing in the one role with no screen · Track B validated before building, three items corrected · GAP-43 given a specified mechanism (W-57) · `UI-UX-REVIEW.md` validated → GAP-44…GAP-48 · **the first real browser round trip against QA**, which found GAP-49, GAP-50 and GAP-51 in twenty minutes and added four traps to §5.

**7 August** — **B11** (GAP-49/GAP-50, both worse than reported) · the A11 access model settled · **B10** (GAP-51) arrangement B can be started at last · **A11** a second person can join a business · merged to `develop`, QA redeployed, GAP-54 found · **A12** (GAP-56/GAP-57), the only new money defect either review produced.

**8 August** — A12 merged; a flow-inventory audit against both trackers found nine unrecorded gaps (GAP-61…GAP-69) · a live QA browser pass (`QA-BROWSER-TEST-FINDINGS`) · **B14, B0b, B12, B13 and B3's core** all shipped in one session · a triage pass over the queue itself, correcting four scheduling errors · a QA rerun, mostly re-confirmations plus GAP-83 · **the queue re-sequenced around B4**, with six facts checked before the order was written · **F1** (GAP-84) · **B4 Wave 1** — the Review shell, the catalogue and all six phase-1 reports.

**9 August** — **12a**, the DM §15 / UI §11.1 `doc-change` for GAP-70/GAP-71, found complete but uncommitted in the working tree and committed rather than left at risk · **B16 Phase 1**, a visual-semantics pass from an independent review, with two of its recommendations declined against source (the 8px card radius contradicts UI §5.3; the proposed status words contradict U-6's locked vocabulary — both re-raised on 9 Aug and re-declined, §6 item 22) · **GAP-70/71's four new DM §15 queries verified against real Postgres**, which found `golden.py` silently broken since migration `0004` (§5) · a CI regression from F1 fixed in two test files never re-run after it (§5) · **GAP-85** · **the ninth validation pass** over four external review documents, adding GAP-88…GAP-99 · **F2 (server half)**, closing the revoked-member 500 (GAP-90) · **A17**, two route-def validation holes (GAP-92, GAP-93) · **A16**, a vehicle's trip history (GAP-77) · **A15**, changing a daily lease's driver (GAP-62) · **A13**, changing a vehicle's arrangement (GAP-54) — moving off B auto-closes the open daily lease via A15's own mechanism, since `generate-day-cards` never reads `vehicle_arrangement` · **A10a**, the management fee that never fires (GAP-39), migration `0011`, **G-1 re-verified at 134,000 through the real close endpoint**. Track A's queue is now clear down to A10b, A8, A14, A7 and A9b.

**Later the same day — five standing decisions asked and answered, then built.** GAP-88, GAP-10, GAP-87, GAP-60 and GAP-1 had each stalled on a decision only the owner could make (§2's own rows on all five). Asked directly, with a recommended option on each; all five came back "recommended." Two needed a `doc-change` first and got one, in the order this tracker has used since GAP-70/71: **D-9** (DM §4.1, §17) settles GAP-88 — `startDailyLease` and `changeDailyLeaseDriver` now materialise their own rolling 90-day horizon synchronously, inside the same transaction, using the identical write `generate-day-cards` already runs; the cron's role changes from originating the fact to extending it, which is what CLAUDE.md's "no cron is a prerequisite for a user action" required all along. `user-flows.md` F-3.4 (v1.1.5) settles GAP-10 — **A10b** shipped: `recordCustomerContribution` now opens an `Obligation` a customer can actually pay against (migration `0012` adds the `incident_recovery.obligation_id` FK the schema had left toothless since `0001`), and `recordRecoveryReceived` was extended to settle that obligation and post the real `payment`/`payment_allocation` pair alongside it — found necessary only once the obligation existed: without it, the recovery's own receipt and the obligation's `settledMinor` would have been two independently-updatable facts about the same money, exactly the split-brain CLAUDE.md's "earned and received never collapse" rule exists to prevent. **GAP-87** closed the same pass, no doc-change needed: `bookTrip` now mirrors the client's own `canBookTrip` gate (arrangement B or C, never A, never unset) — the identical hole GAP-84 closed on the other two start screens, found while fixing those and deliberately left for its own turn. Its test blast radius was large (35 vehicle-creation call sites across four integration files had never set a standing arrangement) and was fixed by a subagent, then independently re-run end to end before being trusted — all four files green, G-1's 134,000 untouched. **GAP-60** and **GAP-1** were decided but not built this pass — see their own rows in §2/§4 for what was decided and why building stays for A9b and its own slot respectively. `npm run check` clean throughout; golden fixtures re-verified at 39/39 (134,000 / 15,000 / 7,500 all unmoved).

**Still open from B16, and carried by the user separately:** **Phase 2** (`Section`/`EmptyState`/`NotAvailable` variants) and **Phase 3** (reports-catalogue grouping, Review-shell money-direction consistency). Both independent and pickable any time; Phase 3 wants the outstanding `develop` merge first so it is visible on hosted QA to verify against. **Superseded 14 August 2026 — neither is carried separately any more:** GAP-124's M-31 pass restates both, so **Phase 2 is absorbed into GAP-124a** (Wave 5 · Step 0) and **Phase 3 into GAP-124b** (Wave 7). Left standing rather than deleted so the next reader finds where the work went, not a category that silently vanished.

**10 August — B4 Wave 2, the half of B4 that stayed open since Wave 1.** GAP-70 and GAP-71 built against the queries the 9 Aug `doc-change` (12a) already verified against real Postgres. **GAP-70**: `listBankedByDestination` and `listAdvancesOutstandingByDriver` (`queries/reports.ts`) give the two subtrahends `heldMinor` already nets their own rows — `cashPositionResponseSchema` gains `banked[]`/`driverAdvances[]`, kept arithmetically consistent with `heldMinor`'s own simplification (a `part_settled` advance counts at its full amount in both places, deliberately). `CashPositionReportScreen` retitles to "Where is our cash" and shows both breakdowns as their own tables, with an honest "nothing yet" message rather than an empty table. **GAP-71**: `listLostDaysByMonth`/`listLostDaysByReason` join `listLostDays` as siblings, not replacements — `lostDaysResponseSchema` restructures from a flat weekday array into `{ byWeekday, byMonth, byReason }`, a breaking wire change absorbed in one pass since the only consumers were this branch's own screen and its tests. `LostDaysReportScreen`'s primary chart is now genuinely "column per month" (UI §11.1), with weekday distribution and the reason breakdown as its two second-tier charts; the per-driver table is unchanged. `LOST_REASON_LABEL`/`LOST_REASON_OPTIONS` extracted to `web/src/lib/lostReasonLabel.ts` so the reason chart and `ConfirmDayCard`'s own picker share one source rather than drifting, the same pattern `ARRANGEMENT_LABEL` already set. 8 new API integration tests (both endpoints), full suite **32 files / 463 tests green**, including a concurrency-adjacent reconciliation check (an advance's `issued_by_user_id` must be set for `heldMinor` and `driverAdvances` to agree — the two queries are scoped differently on purpose, DM §15's own stated reason). Web: 97 files / 418 tests green. `npm run check` clean throughout; golden fixtures unmoved. **B4 is now fully done, both waves.**

**10 August, later the same day — GAP-101, the read-error contract, planned and then built.** A full implementation plan (`GAP-101-READ-ERROR-CONTRACT-PLAN-2026-08-10.md`) went first: it corrected the tracker's own "50 of 52 screens" figure to **30 of 32 files, 81 of 83 call sites** (the wider count double-counted 27 files that only call `useQueryClient` for post-mutation invalidation), and named two failure modes the original finding hadn't — a `?? []`/`?? 0` fallback turning a failed read into a confident wrong answer (`HomeScreen` rendering "Nothing needs you today" while the Worker is down), and a failed read silently deleting a safety warning (`StartLeaseScreen`/`BookTripScreen`'s expired-paperwork strip, `VehicleCalendarScreen`'s INV-1 grid rendering every day as free). Five open decisions in the plan were put to the user and confirmed as recommended before any code moved. **The `doc-change` landed first**, per the standing rule: `ui-ux-guidelines.md` to v1.2.5 — **M-28** ("every read has three visible outcomes: data, a real nothing, or a stated failure"), a `QueryState` row in §6.4, §9.5 retitled "Loading and failing" with the status→copy table, a `NotAvailable`-vs-`QueryState` distinction in §11.4, and a new enforcement row in §12.6; `implementation-guidelines.md` to v1.5.1 for the matching `IG §16.1` row. **The primitive**: `useQueryState` (`web/src/lib/useQueryState.ts`) resolves one `UseQueryResult` into `idle | pending | error | ready` — `idle` kept distinct from `pending` specifically because a disabled query (`enabled: false`, used at 23 call sites) sits in `isPending: true, fetchStatus: "idle"` forever, and collapsing that into "loading" would have reproduced the exact bug on the ten screens with the most queries. `combineQueryStates` resolves several reads at once, error beating pending beating idle. `QueryStateFailure`/`QueryState` (`web/src/components/QueryState.tsx`) render `EmptyState`'s sibling, never `AlertStrip` (that's Home's urgency channel) and never `NotAvailable` (that means the number doesn't exist, not that the read failed) — status-mapped copy (401 → sign in, 403 → no access, 404 → gone, 5xx → the request id as a caption and Try again), inside the §9.6 vocabulary lock. `main.tsx` gained a `shouldRetryQuery` predicate (`web/src/lib/queryRetry.ts`): a 4xx `ApiError` never retries, since retrying a decided answer only delays the error branch it precedes. **Migration ran in four waves, worst first**: **W1** — the ten screens making a false claim (`HomeScreen`'s six-query false-empty, `PeopleListScreen`, the `StartLeaseScreen`/`BookTripScreen` paperwork and pause warnings, `VehicleCalendarScreen`'s INV-1 grid, `OpeningBalanceScreen` — which turned out to have a second, sharper bug: its save is a full replace, so a failed hydration read could have silently overwritten a previously-saved batch, now blocked rather than degraded — and the four picker sheets, `ReasonPicker` gaining an optional `error` prop along the way). **W2** — the six reports and four Review screens plus `DriverDetailScreen`, **closing F2's client half** (`ReviewMoneyScreen` is GAP-90's own screen). **W3** — the remaining eight screens, `CloseLeaseScreen`'s deposit-settlement gate the second write-corruption-shaped find (a failed deposit read used to fall through to "no deposit, Next," which would have skipped a real settlement — the primary action now reads "Try again" instead). **W4** — the `check-forbidden.mjs` guard rule (a `useQuery(` with no `QueryState`/`useQueryState` reference in the same file fails the gate, verified against both a real violation and the whole repo), which caught one genuine miss the manual pass had left (`CorrectPaymentSheet.tsx`, fixed alongside it), and the retirement of the two hand-rolled `isError` branches (`FirstRunGate`, `CloseMonthScreen`) onto the shared primitive. **97 → 105 web test files, 418 → 492 tests, all green; `api/` untouched throughout, so the golden fixtures were never at risk rather than re-verified.**

**10 August, later still — a fresh live QA pass finds the most severe gap this tracker has recorded.** `LIVE-BROWSER-FINDINGS-2026-08-10.md`, run against `qa.fleetsettle.com` at its then-current deploy (B4 Wave 2, `2c0499e` — GAP-101 had landed on `build/p0-foundation` by this point but not yet reached `develop`/QA), with the new reports catalogue giving it something to cross-check live data against for the first time. **GAP-103**: an opening balance can be entered, confirmed, and the screen says so — but `saveOpeningBalance`/`commitOpeningBalance` never write an `obligation`, `deposit`, or `advance` row for any of the six entry kinds, confirmed both by source (`domain/opening-balance.ts`'s entire write surface is two tables, neither read anywhere else in the API) and live (a committed Rs 2,000 customer due invisible on "Who owes us"; a committed Rs 5,000 driver deposit contributing nothing to "Where is our cash", which shows exactly the one real deposit and no more). **GAP-104**: the mobile `ActionSheet`→`Sheet` history race documented in `MOBILE-SHEET-AND-DATE-PICKER-FINDINGS-2026-08-09.md` but never triaged into this tracker is now directly reproduced under real touch/coarse-pointer emulation — Quick Add's Fuel/Expense/New trip silently do nothing on a phone, and a nested `MoneyField` sheet can close its own parent sheet, discarding an in-progress opening-balance entry with no error of any kind. **GAP-106**: the new "Where is our cash" report hardcodes "Held for customers" onto a total that is party-agnostic by design and, in the test data, entirely a driver's deposit — three call sites in `CashPositionReportScreen.tsx`, with the existing unit test pinning the wrong wording as correct, the same shape GAP-75 already taught this queue to watch for. **Also confirmed live, no code change needed**: LT-7/GAP-3 fully closed (the overnight cron produced a real placeholder, confirming it wrote all three money facts correctly); GAP-84's arrangement gate holds under direct URL navigation; GAP-85's blank-vs-zero fix holds. **GAP-83 gets a second, sharper confirmation** — a full DOM tab-order trace, not just a source read, showing the exact keyboard path a sighted user takes through an invisible focus stop. Full account, including two findings that could not be tested and why (MP-08's `Dialog`-in-`Sheet` stacking has no reachable precondition anywhere in the client; MP-06's `showPicker()` fallback needs a real Safari, not this session's Chromium), in `LIVE-BROWSER-FINDINGS-2026-08-10.md` and TRACKER §6's eleventh pass.

**10 August, later still — B9's two blocking decisions asked and answered; `feature/image-upload` evaluated and found further along than this branch's own tracker knew.** GAP-44 and GAP-89 had both stalled B9 on a real product decision rather than a build question — asked directly, with a recommended option on each. **GAP-44** came back *against* the recommendation: the fuller option (enrich `VehicleDoubleBookedError` with the actual conflicting dates/entity, so the Dialog states precisely what's blocking) was chosen over the cheaper name-and-link-to-calendar default, which makes this item bigger than B9's row previously sized it — it now touches the error class, its catch sites, and the wire schema, not only the client. **GAP-89** came back both times as recommended: `/review*`/`/reports*`/`/period/close`/`/opening-balances` stay in Operate's own shell and highlight "More" rather than switching an owner-manager into the Review shell's tab bar for the same routes; vehicle-scoped detail routes highlight "Vehicles" as the domain parent regardless of which of the three places sent them there. Neither is built yet — both are recorded in their own §4 rows so the next sitting starts from a decision, not a re-litigation, the same pattern GAP-88/GAP-10/GAP-87/GAP-60/GAP-1 already set 9 Aug. **Separately, `feature/image-upload` (A7/GAP-16) turned out to be a full seven-commit implementation, not the "fully planned, not started" this branch's own tracker still read** — built and pushed 9–10 Aug, closing GAP-16 for expense receipts specifically, but carrying its own punch list (`ATTACHMENT-UPLOAD-PENDING-2026-08-10.md`) of real blockers before it can be trusted: its 26-case integration suite has never run, golden fixtures are unverified, no PR is open, and it is now three commits behind this branch. GAP-16's row here is corrected to describe that state precisely rather than either of the two wrong claims (not-started, or closed) — see its own row for the full account. **GAP-103 (F3) is unaffected by any of this and remains the actual head of the queue**: it still carries its own undecided fork (materialise the six opening-balance entry kinds into `obligation`/`deposit`/`advance` synchronously on commit, or keep them a separate ledger the reports learn to read alongside the real tables) and nobody has been asked yet.

**10 August, later still — A7 merged.** PR #20 (`feature/image-upload` → `develop`) landed clean: CI's `gate` and `integration` workflows both green, the 26-case attachment suite included, golden fixtures re-confirmed unmoved (134,000/15,000/7,500) as part of the full suite passing. `ATTACHMENT-UPLOAD-PENDING-2026-08-10.md`'s own blocking list is now stale on every point it named — the PR did open, CI did run the integration suite, golden fixtures did get re-verified. **Not yet on `main`** — no `develop`→`main` PR exists, so expense-receipt uploads are live on `qa.fleetsettle.com` but not `fleetsettle.com`; per CLAUDE.md, opening that PR is a deploy decision, not a mechanical follow-up. Track A's remaining queue: A8, A14, A9b.

**10 August, later still — `PENDING-REVIEW-ITEMS-2026-08-10.md` evaluated and absorbed (TRACKER §6's twelfth pass), then B9's first batch built.** The seventeen root review/audit files accumulated since 6 August had already been consolidated into one pending-items checklist and removed from the working tree (uncommitted, recoverable via git history) by the time this pass opened it — the consolidation itself got the same source-checked treatment every external review here gets, rather than being trusted on its own say-so. It held up: every checklist item mapped onto a gap id this tracker already carried, five items were re-raised without noting they'd already been parked in `Recorded, unowned, and correct to leave` (context restored on each row rather than silently reopened), and nine of B9's smallest, least-ambiguous rows were built the same sitting — **GAP-45** (the trip-title year now conditional, no more doubled-date clipping at 360px), **GAP-46** (every occupied calendar cell gets the legend's own wording as its `aria-label`), **GAP-55** (`autoComplete` tokens on the four real name/phone/address/organisation fields), **GAP-89** (`tabForPathname` widened per the 10 Aug decision — More for review/reports/period/opening-balances, Vehicles for entity detail routes), **GAP-91** (`variant="destructive"` on the one irreversible confirm), **GAP-96** (`Section` gained an optional `total` slot; Vehicle Overview's Costs section uses it), **GAP-97** (Book trip added to the vehicle-actions sheet, gated the same way `bookTrip` itself is server-side), **GAP-99** (the two leaked "presigned upload storage" strings reworded — and the row's own prediction that A7 would make this moot corrected, since A7 shipped expenses only, not condition photos), plus **M-25** (Excess rate → Excess fee per km, riding with GAP-97's same forms). Full gate green after two flaky re-runs ruled out by isolation (105/105 web files, 513 tests; 12/12 shared files, 85 tests; `api/` untouched, so golden fixtures were never at risk). **GAP-106 followed the same day** (11 Aug): the same three "Held for customers" strings reworded to "Held as deposits", and `CashPositionReportScreen.test.tsx`'s own pinned-wrong assertion corrected rather than left to keep passing against the bug. What's left in B9: GAP-44 (needs the wire-schema change GAP-44's own decision requires), GAP-47 (landscape, two shared primitives), GAP-48 (a new `Toast` primitive plus call-site decisions), GAP-83/GAP-105 (`DateField`'s own rebuild).

**11 August — F3/GAP-103 built: the most severe recorded gap, closed.** The undecided fork (materialise on commit vs. a parallel ledger) was put to the user and came back **materialise** — into the exact tables the reports already read, not a second ledger every report would need a new `UNION` to know about. `commitOpeningBalance` now turns each entry into a real row on its first commit: `customer_due`/`driver_arrears`/`owed_to_driver` become an `obligation` (a new `kind: 'opening_balance'`, migration `0014` widens `obligation_kind_check` the same way `0009` did for `trip_fare`); `deposit_held` becomes a `deposit` + a `taken` `deposit_movement`; `advance_outstanding` becomes an `advance`; and `cash_held` — which turned out to have no table of its own at all, `listPartnerCashPositions`'s `heldMinor` is pure derived arithmetic (`received − banked − advanced`) — becomes an unallocated `payment` (`direction: 'received'`, `handledByUserId` the partner), exactly the shape a real surplus payment already leaves, so the cash-position report needs no new read path either. F-0.2's own Alternates clause ("editable until the first period closes") meant this had to handle a correction *after* commit, not just the first one: a new table, `opening_balance_posting` (migration `0014`, deliberately not a money table — no `posted_period_id`, not in `assert_period_open()`'s array, the same reasoning `0013` gave `attachment`), links each entry to whatever it produced, so a later `saveOpeningBalance` call on an already-committed batch first reverses the prior generation (`obligation`/`advance` void by id — both sums already respect `voided_at`; `payment` flips `status` to `reversed`, which `listPartnerCashPositions` already filters on, never routed through `payment_correction`'s allocation-unwind machinery since these rows never have any; `deposit_movement` gets a real offsetting entry, since `sumDepositMovements` turned out to never consult `voided_at` at all — confirmed by reading it, not assumed) before posting the corrected figures. Two integration tests added to the existing suite: one exercises materialisation for all six kinds against the three reports GAP-103's own text named (`GET /api/reports/receivables`, `GET /api/driver/{id}/balances`, `GET /api/reports/cash-position`); the other extends the existing happy-path test to prove a post-commit correction leaves exactly the corrected figure behind, never both. Full API suite: 490/490 across 33 files, golden fixtures unmoved (134,000/15,000/7,500), DM §13's drift assertion clean. **Current queue head is now F4** (GAP-104, the mobile `Sheet` history race) — still fully designed, not yet built.

**11 August, later — low-API UI branch.** The UI slice after B15 closed B6 (customer detail plus vehicle-paperwork write), B5's core (driver `/me` and staff driver history), and the B2 callers: `/cash`, `/partners/:userId`, banking, capital contribution, partner payout/settlement, mileage-package create/archive, Members, driver access links, `BorneByPaidBy`'s paid-by member picker, and `/vehicle-sharing` for initial ownership-share setup plus management-agreement create/revoke. The branch also exposed a small API contract correction: `GET /api/business-member` had to return the membership row `id`, because revoke/change-role routes address membership ids, not user ids. A follow-up in the same branch closed GAP-100 by adding trip-scoped advance issue from Trip detail and advance settlement from staff Driver detail. **Still open by design:** GAP-1's per-vehicle access scoping, ownership-share replacement after an existing current split, richer settlement context until driver-view advances expose `settledMinor`/`tripId`, and API hardening so management agreements reject a non-`manager` member even outside the UI.

**11 August, later still — the low-API branch validated independently, and the validation found the one thing its own author couldn't have shipped a fix for: F3 doesn't reach backward.** A four-reviewer pass (money/tenancy rules first) plus one adversarial spot-check found the branch itself clean — no money, tenancy or W-49 driver-isolation violation anywhere in 65 files / ~5,600 lines, objective gate green (113 files / 564 tests, 0 lint errors). Every claim in that report was independently re-verified against source before being trusted, this project's own standing rule, and all of it held:

- **GAP-109 — the most consequential finding of the pass, and it's about F3, not this branch.** `commitOpeningBalance` and `saveOpeningBalance`'s `wasCommitted` branch ([opening-balance.ts:344](api/src/domain/opening-balance.ts#L344), [:435](api/src/domain/opening-balance.ts#L435)) only materialise on a state *transition* — `draft → committed`, or a re-save of an already-committed batch. Correct by design (a Confirm retry must never double-post, CLAUDE.md → Writes) and it means **an opening balance confirmed before F3 shipped stays confirmed-looking and contributes nothing to any report, forever, unless someone re-saves it**. `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-11.md`'s QA-01 (Rs 2,000 customer due, Rs 5,000 driver deposit, neither in either report) is this, live, not stale data — confirmed by reading the function, not inferred from the symptom. Production is the sharper case: it has never had F3 at all, so merging `develop` → `main` will not repair a single balance already sitting there.
- **GAP-110 — and the one remedy for GAP-109 is itself broken on a phone.** The opening-balance confirm sheet's `Confirm and go live` renders below the viewport at 360×640; `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-11.md`'s QA-02, confirmed against the same live report. Not the F4 history race — F4 holds; this is ordinary layout that happens to sit on the one screen GAP-109's fix runs through.
- **GAP-111** — `CashPositionReportScreen`'s table view has no empty-state guard where its chart view does ([:173](web/src/features/reports/CashPositionReportScreen.tsx#L173) vs. [:214](web/src/features/reports/CashPositionReportScreen.tsx#L214)) — confirmed in source, not just the live QA-04 symptom. QA-05's cramped stacked-bar label folds into the same row, same screen.
- **GAP-112** — a receipt uploads and counts correctly; its thumbnail renders broken (`naturalWidth: 0`). Filed deliberately unverified — the QA session couldn't inspect the blob's bytes, and A7's own 26-case suite is green, which argues for a bad test fixture over a real defect. **Reproduce with a real photo before spending anything on a fix.**
- **GAP-113** — confirmed by reading `PartnerSetupScreen.tsx:245-256`: the ownership-share total is `text-good-ink` at exactly 100%, `text-critical-ink` otherwise, no word or icon either side — CLAUDE.md → Interface / M-15, colour never carries meaning alone.
- **GAP-114** — `businessMemberResponseSchema`'s new required `id` (a real, needed fix — revoke/change-role address membership ids, not user ids) shipped with the one test positioned to catch a regression on it using `toMatchObject`, which passes whether or not `id` is present or correct. The same shape as the `voidExpense` and A0 regression tests that were each pinned to their own bug (§5) — found here before it became a third instance rather than after.
- **GAP-115** — six screens (`CustomerDetailScreen`, `MineScreen`, `DriverDetailScreen`, `PartnerDetailScreen`, `PartnerSetupScreen`, `MembersScreen`) have correct source and thinner tests than the branch's own acceptance criteria named. No defect found in any of the six — unverified-branch risk, not a bug. `MembersScreen`'s missing "Manager is absent from the picker" assertion is the one worth doing first, since it's the GAP-1 practical guard.
- **GAP-116** — `docs/README.md`'s version table says `ui-ux-guidelines v1.2.6`; the document is `v1.3.0`, three bumps ahead. Confirmed by reading both. Small, and CLAUDE.md sends every reader to `docs/README.md` first, so worth a ride-along fix rather than staying stale.

**The gate table above is corrected to what this pass actually ran, not what the branch claimed**: 564 web tests across 113 files (was recorded 557/112), 49 lint warnings (was 48 — the branch's own new route, the count rising correctly), and the `main`/`develop` migration gap widened to 43 commits (was 29 on 10 Aug) — `main` is now missing F3 and F4, the two most severe gaps this tracker has recorded, on top of everything it was already missing.

**The same day — the owner set a phase model.** Read in full above ([§1](#the-phase-model--set-11-august-2026)). Phase 1 now covers every open gap in [§4](#4-every-open-gap), including the eighteen previously parked as "correct to leave" — moved into the table then called `Scheduled` and since renamed [Full accounts](#full-accounts--open-and-closed) the same pass, each keeping its own reasoning. WhatsApp messaging (P14) becomes phase 2; offline/PWA (B7) becomes phase 3. **Five** of the newly-promoted rows (GAP-6/15 paired, GAP-18, GAP-19, GAP-73/98 paired) contradict `use-cases.md` §9.1's own phase table and need one `doc-change` (plus the matching `user-flows.md` tag edits) before they're startable — recorded, not yet done. **A sixth, GAP-68, was checked the same pass and found not to need one** — `F-3.5`/`UC-13` are already phase 1 in both documents; corrected before it could sit wrong.

**11 August, later still — eight standing open questions asked and answered in one sitting; four gaps closed on the answers, one re-tracked, the deploy left for later, and the tracker's own bookkeeping corrected.** A full re-read of TRACKER.md and Plan.md against the current 41-gap queue surfaced eight questions this repository had been carrying without a decision — some its own standing questions, some gap rows blocked on "needs a decision" rather than sizing. Asked one at a time rather than bundled, so each got its own answer:

1. **The `develop`→`main` deploy** — asked, and **deferred**: "Not yet — I'll decide later." `main` stays 45 commits and four migrations behind. No action taken; recorded as a live decision, not an oversight, so the next reader does not re-raise it as a bug.
2. **Incidents' phase** — TRACKER's own standing open question, closed above. **Phase one, confirmed.**
3. **GAP-7, trip `hold`/`in_progress`** — **build it end to end**, not the cheaper UI-only wording fix. Re-tracked rather than closed: ST-5's own "expires or is confirmed" has no duration specified anywhere, so a `doc-change` has to settle the expiry mechanism before this can size. See its own §4 row.
4. **GAP-11, W-11's insurance conditionality** — **drop it**: the claim section was never actually gated by anything, so the document now says what the code does. Closed via `doc-change`, `use-cases.md` v1.2.6 / `user-flows.md` v1.1.7.
5. **GAP-107, attachment retention** — **kept indefinitely**, written down rather than assumed. Closed via `doc-change`, `data-model.md` v1.1.5 (D-10).
6. **GAP-30/A8, the fuel-fill odometer** — **a fuel fill writes its own `odometer_reading` row, in the same transaction** — not stored on the expense alone, not fuzzy-matched to a nearby reading. Unblocks A8's own build; no doc or tracker change needed beyond recording the decision here, since A8 is still unbuilt.
7. **GAP-95, the `More` disclosure label** — re-raised a third time, and **closed as declined-final**: UI §6.3 is right, `Disclosure.tsx` implements it correctly, and the row is now closed rather than left for a fourth report to reach the same conclusion.
8. **Verification's two loose ends** — **`docs/qa/live-test-plan.md` restored** (it had been swept into the 10 Aug consolidation by mistake — the tracker kept citing it as live the whole time it was gone) **and GAP-58's 178-case manifest retired** rather than adopted, since nobody intends to run it and `docs/testing/README.md`/`test-manifest.yaml` now say so instead of claiming a source of truth they were not. Both closed the same way: a decision recorded, not code written.

**Net: four gap rows closed (GAP-11, GAP-58, GAP-95, GAP-107), one re-tracked with a new dependency (GAP-7), one build decision recorded against an already-open row (GAP-30/A8), one deploy decision recorded as deferred.** Three `doc-change`s landed in the same sitting, each following CLAUDE.md's "documents travel together": `use-cases.md` v1.2.5 → v1.2.6, `user-flows.md` v1.1.6 → v1.1.7, `data-model.md` v1.1.4 → v1.1.5, `docs/README.md`'s status table matching all three. **One correction to this tracker's own prior claim**: the 11 Aug validation pass had read GAP-116 as needing a §4 row it didn't have; re-checked this session and it already carries a closed row (above) from the same pass that found it — no further action was needed there. The commit-behind count is corrected to **45** (was recorded 43, itself already stale from 29 on 10 Aug) — re-checked directly against `origin/main`/`origin/develop`, not carried forward from the last time someone counted.

**12 August — the owner set a new premise: nothing goes live until every phase-1 gap is fixed and tested, and there is no near-term plan to onboard a real business.** Checked against production directly rather than assumed: every table on `main`'s Neon branch holds zero rows, confirmed 12 Aug, five days after the 7 Aug count that first found it empty. **This retires the "live-and-silently-wrong outranks unbuilt" ordering rule this queue has run on since GAP-3** — it has no referent against an empty database — and replaces it with three rules for a pre-launch codebase: decisions before code, schema changes while the database is empty (free now, a real backfill question at the first live row), and what makes later work bigger if deferred (GAP-60 before A9b, GAP-59 before A8). Full reasoning in the ten-wave plan below.

**Twenty-one open questions across the phase-1 queue were put to the owner in this sitting, all resolved, plus two live defects found validating them against source rather than trusting the queue's own framing:**

- **Schema shape (7):** GAP-73's `adjustment.occurred_on` · GAP-59's composite FK (`MATCH SIMPLE`, backstopped by two `CHECK`s so a null `vehicle_id` cannot dodge it) · GAP-20's `lease_day_exception` · GAP-26's `vehicle_unavailability`, reason-mapped so a breakdown still reaches UC-76's lost-day denominator and a routine outage doesn't · GAP-7's `trip.hold_expires_on` · GAP-60's `replaces_id` on the twelve money tables (shape reconfirmed, now actually written into `data-model.md` §17 D-16 for the first time — it was decided 9 Aug but never landed there) · GAP-36's soft-delete columns on `driver`/`customer`.
- **Behaviour (7):** GAP-7's hold released synchronously by the next booking attempt, never only by cron, and `in_progress` derived from the business date rather than written anywhere · GAP-5's surplus forward-allocated as a fresh `payment_allocation`, found **already fully specified** in `data-model.md` §10.2 — only a `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` row lock was actually missing, so this gap is far smaller than its row read · GAP-2's rate change closing and reopening the `daily_lease` row, reusing the mechanism `changeDailyLeaseDriver` already has — roughly halving the item · GAP-8's advisory lock on close · GAP-14's stacked corrections, allowed and tested · GAP-65's 30-day share-link expiry · `post_closure_charge`'s cross-check, handler-level since it writes an `obligation` rather than owning a table.
- **The soft-delete policy (7 sub-decisions, one head decision):** **no hard deletes anywhere in `api/src`, full stop** — wider than the money-tables-only scope W-50 held until now. The owner's own reason: an unexplained figure in production must always be traceable to the row that caused it, and migration `0002`'s audit trigger only ever fired on insert and update, so a deleted row left nothing behind either way. Decided alongside it: the full `voided_at`/`voided_reason`/`voided_by` trio on every table, not a minimal timestamp-only shape · `day_record` joins them, and stale cards are voided rather than mutated in place, since updating an `open` card with `earned_minor = 0` would still be mutating a money-table row · a soft-deleted parent (a driver who left) keeps every past record's history rendering exactly as before, so a closed month's totals never move because someone archived a driver later · GAP-28 (error monitoring) reclassified from "flagged, not tracked" to **capture and query, no paging** — the same forensic motivation as the delete policy, satisfiable on Workers Logs alone, no on-call person required.

**The two live defects, both found reading the code the decisions would touch, not from any external review — the fourth and fifth time this project's own "validate before scheduling" discipline has paid for itself this way:**

- **GAP-119** — a charter cannot be booked on a vehicle already on daily lease, the mixed-use case this project is built around. `startDailyLease`'s own D-9 horizon materialises `vehicle_day_allocation` rows synchronously; `bookTrip` inserts its own with no release first, and `one_arrangement_per_vehicle_day` refuses it as `VEHICLE_DOUBLE_BOOKED`. **`data-model.md` §4.1 already specified the correct behaviour** ("the daily-lease allocation row is REPLACED by the trip's") — this was an implementation gap against a correct spec, not a design question. Filed pending reproduction against the real endpoint (§4 row, Wave 0 of the plan).
- **GAP-118** — the mirror case: closing a daily lease (a driver change, F-4.7, or an arrangement change off B, F-1.2) never frees its own future occupancy, because arrangements A and C both have the cleanup primitive and B never got one. Confirmed by reading `listAllocatedDatesForVehicle`'s vehicle-only scoping and `dailyLease.test.ts`'s own day-card assertions, which never exercise a driver change. Both fixes now specified in `user-flows.md` v1.1.8 (F-4.7, F-1.2, F-5.1, F-5.5) and `data-model.md` v1.1.6 (§4.1, D-12).

**Three `doc-change`s landed the same sitting, all before any of the above was treated as buildable, per CLAUDE.md's "documents travel together":** `use-cases.md` v1.2.6 → v1.2.7 (**W-58**, the soft-delete policy itself) · `user-flows.md` v1.1.7 → v1.1.8 (ST-5's hold mechanism and INV-32/33, F-1.7's skip mechanism, the new F-1.10 off-road flow, F-4.7/F-1.2/F-5.1/F-5.5's occupancy-release Accept clauses, F-8.5's widened void coverage, F-9.2's off-road bucket now pointing at a write path) · `data-model.md` v1.1.5 → v1.1.6 (§17 D-11 through D-16, two new tables, the composite FK, the row lock). `docs/README.md`'s status table updated to match. **No code changed in this sitting — schema, handlers and tests are all still exactly as they were**; the ten-wave plan below sequences the actual build.

**The full remaining phase-1 queue — thirty-eight open gaps plus the two found this sitting — resequenced into eleven dependency-ordered waves**, replacing Plan.md's "current order" line, which was written under the old live-and-silently-wrong rule and is now stale on its own premise. Wave 0 reproduces GAP-118/GAP-119 against real endpoints before either is fixed; Wave 1 is the schema migration series this sitting's seven schema decisions unlock while the database is still empty; Waves 2 onward sequence tenancy, money correctness, the half-built flows, reports, client remainder, verification debt and release, in that order. Full wave-by-wave detail in Plan.md's replacement queue.

**12 August, later the same day — Wave 0 run: both defects confirmed against the real endpoints, not left as a source reading.** A dedicated Neon test branch (`test`, previously stale at migration `0007`) was brought current to `0014` and used for both — never `qa` or `main`. **GAP-119**: `POST /api/daily-lease` (every_day pattern, vehicle on arrangement B) returns `201` and materialises the horizon exactly as D-9 says; `POST /api/trip` for a 3-day range inside it then returns `409 VEHICLE_DOUBLE_BOOKED`, the exact prediction. **GAP-118**: a `day_record` 5 days out is confirmed carrying `driverId = driver1` before any change; `POST /api/daily-lease/{id}/change-driver` to `driver2` returns `201` and a genuine new `daily_lease` row exists; the same future date's `day_record` is then re-queried and is **completely unchanged** — one row, still `driver1`, still the closed `dailyLeaseId`, still `state: 'open'`. No row for `driver2` was written at all, confirming `materializeDailyLeaseHorizon`'s `existing.has(d)` guard silently no-ops on the new lease exactly as read from source. Both reproductions were throwaway integration tests, run once each and deleted rather than committed red — Wave 0's job is proof, not a fixture in the tree. Both gap rows above updated with the evidence; both move to **Wave 2**.

**13 August 2026 — Wave 1 built: the nine-migration schema series, all seven of the sitting's schema decisions landed.** `0015`/`0016` close GAP-59 (a plain FK on `expense.incident_id`, then the composite `(trip.id, vehicle_id)`/`(incident.id, vehicle_id)` pair with the two `CHECK`s D-14 specified) · `0017` adds `adjustment.occurred_on` (GAP-73), backfilled from `created_at::date` rather than a magic default, wired into both existing insert sites · `0018`/`0019` the two new tables, `lease_day_exception` (GAP-20) and `vehicle_unavailability` (GAP-26), both outside `assert_period_open()`'s array, confirmed by DM §13's drift assertion · `0020` `trip.hold_expires_on` plus a `business_settings.hold_expiry_days` default of 7 (GAP-7) · `0021` `replaces_id` on the money tables carrying the W-50 trio (GAP-60) — found the doc's own "twelve" undercounts the actual thirteen (`capital_contribution`, `expense`, `incident_recovery`, `obligation`, `adjustment`, `write_off`, `write_off_recovery`, `offset_record`, `deposit_movement`, `advance`, `advance_settlement`, `banking_event`, `partner_payout`; `payment` stays out, it already has `payment_correction`) · `0022` the void trio on `vehicle_day_allocation`/`payment_allocation`/`day_record`/`opening_balance_entry`, both live partial-unique indexes moved to `voided_at IS NULL` · `0023` soft-delete on `driver`/`customer` plus closing the schema's last two `ON DELETE CASCADE` hard-delete vectors (`mileage_assessment_split`, `offset_allocation`) to plain FKs — not itself a W-58 call site, but the same guarantee at the constraint level: a manual `DELETE` against a parent can no longer silently take its children with it.

**Riding code, same commit:** the three real hard-delete call sites converted to void-updates (`vehicle_day_allocation` via `deleteLeaseAllocationAfter`/`deleteAllocationDaysForTrip`, `payment_allocation` via the renamed `voidPaymentAllocation`, `opening_balance_entry` via the renamed `voidEntriesForBatch`), `voidedBy` threaded through every caller; the two `vehicle_day_allocation` read sites the plan itself flagged as the ones most likely to silently reproduce GAP-118 in a new form (`listAllocatedDatesForVehicle`, `findVehicleCalendar`) given a `voidedAt IS NULL` filter.

**Verification, against the dedicated Neon test branch:** DM §13's drift assertion clean, `npm run guard`/lint/typecheck clean, full integration suite green, golden fixtures unmoved (134,000/15,000/7,500). Two real regressions found and fixed in the process — not migration bugs, the intended bite of GAP-59's new constraints landing on stale fixtures: `incident.test.ts`'s own G-2 reproduction was tracking its two repair expenses for cleanup *before* the incident they reference, which the new composite FK now refuses to delete out of order (fixed by moving both `trackCreatedExpense` calls after `trackCreatedIncident`, so cleanup unwinds them first); `reports.test.ts`'s trip-ranking fixture inserted an expense naming a `tripId` with no `vehicleId`, exactly the inconsistent claim `expense_trip_vehicle_check` exists to refuse. Two more tests were still asserting the pre-W-58 hard-delete behaviour (`trip.test.ts`, `lease-closure.test.ts`) and were updated to assert on `voidedAt` instead of row absence.

**13 August 2026, later — Wave 2 built: GAP-118 and GAP-119 fixed, and a prerequisite gap they exposed (GAP-120) closed first.** Re-validated against source before any code moved (`/loop`-style re-derivation, not a resume from memory): the tree had moved mid-review — Wave 1 had been committed and merged (PR #34) while its own migrations were being re-read — so the session picked up from the real, current state rather than the stale one the conversation started from.

**GAP-120, found closing GAP-118, fixed first.** Migration `0022`'s own header says `day_record` got the void trio "ahead of Wave 2, which is what actually needs it" — true of the write side, not the read side: `grep` for `dayRecord.voidedAt` across `api/src` returned nothing. Eight sites were reading or writing the table with no awareness a row could now be voided, latent only because nothing had ever set the column. The sharpest: `confirmOpenDayRecord`'s idempotency guard is `WHERE state = 'open'` — voiding never touches `state`, so a voided stale card could still be confirmed and have real money posted against it, GAP-3's own class of bug (idempotency keyed on the wrong fact). Fixed: `isNull(dayRecord.voidedAt)` added to `confirmOpenDayRecord`'s sibling scans (`pauseDayRecordsForTrip`, `resumeDayRecordsForTrip`, the driver/unconfirmed-list reads, the close checklist, all three lost-day reports, `vehicle.ts`'s calendar join) — deliberately **not** `findDayRecordByLeaseAndDate` or `confirmOpenDayRecord`'s own `WHERE`, since filtering those would have silently broken `confirmDay.ts`'s existing unique-violation-as-race-recovery contract (a genuine INSERT race needs that lookup to find the row a concurrent request just won). Confirming a voided card is refused instead by a new domain-level check, `DayRecordVoidedError`/`DAY_RECORD_VOIDED` (409) — checked ahead of the ordinary already-confirmed no-op branch, since a voided row's `state` reads exactly like one. A new `check-forbidden.mjs` rule (`money/void-table-unfiltered`) makes the pattern structural going forward, scoped to the four tables `0022`'s own comment named — `vehicleDayAllocation`, `paymentAllocation`, `dayRecord`, `openingBalanceEntry` — checked directly against a table-wide version first and found it would fail the gate on nineteen pre-existing gaps across `driver`/`customer`/`obligation`/`offsetRecord`/`depositMovement`/`advanceSettlement`/`bankingEvent`, none of them this session's to fix (most are GAP-36/A9b's own remit, Wave 5, with no live void endpoint yet). The rule's own first draft copied `checkQueryErrorHandling`'s file-wide `allow:` opt-out and was caught failing silently on `reports.ts`, which already carried three unrelated `allow:` comments (W-56 zeros, a CHECK-guaranteed non-null) — fixed to require the opt-out itself mention `void` or the table name. Four regression tests, all run against the real `test` branch (checklist exclusion, confirm rejection, unconfirmed-list exclusion, lost-days exclusion across `byWeekday`/`byMonth`/`byReason`).

**GAP-118**: `releaseDailyLeaseAllocationsAfter` (queries/dailyLease.ts) and `voidFutureOpenDayRecordsForLease` (queries/day-record.ts), called from `changeDailyLeaseDriver` and `changeVehicleArrangement` (moving off B) immediately after the closed lease's `effectiveTo` is set and before any replacement lease materialises — ordering matters, since `materializeDailyLeaseHorizon`'s own `existing.has(d)` guard is what silently no-opped the fix's absence in the first place. `voidedReason` is a constructor parameter, not hardcoded, since the same primitive serves two real, different facts ("driver changed" vs "moved off daily lease") — the same reasoning `voidExpenseRow` already gives its own. Deliberately narrower than "every future row": only `state = 'open'` cards are voided, never `paused_for_trip` ones — `changeDailyLeaseDriver` carries no open-trip guard of its own (unlike the arrangement-change path, which does), so a day genuinely still under a trip's hands is a separate, unaddressed question this fix does not resolve by guessing. Reproduced end to end against the real endpoints, both callers: a future date inside the old lease's own D-9 horizon starts `open` under driver 1; after the change, the same date's row is voided under driver 1 and a new row exists under driver 2 — and, for the arrangement-change side, simply voided with nothing to replace it.

**GAP-119**: `releaseDailyLeaseAllocationsForRange` (queries/trip.ts), called from `bookTrip` before `insertAllocationDays` — scoped by `vehicleId` + the trip's own range, not a lease id, since `bookTrip` has no daily-lease context at all; the live-row uniqueness `one_arrangement_per_vehicle_day` itself enforces means this is equivalent to lease-scoping in practice. D-12's "REPLACED, not refused" now holds: Wave 0's own reproduction (`POST /api/trip` over an active daily lease → `409 VEHICLE_DOUBLE_BOOKED`) now returns `201`. `cancelTrip` re-materialises through the existing `materializeDailyLeaseHorizon` primitive, over whichever daily lease is *currently* active for the vehicle — not necessarily the one active when the trip was booked, since GAP-118 may have superseded it in between; no current lease (moved off B, or never had one) is a no-op, not an error. One existing test needed updating, not weakening: `trip.test.ts`'s "resumes paused day records" test used a daily lease created via the raw factory (`effectiveFrom` in the past, open-ended) that `cancelTrip`'s new re-materialisation step correctly now fills forward from today — real rows the fixture's narrower cleanup never anticipated, fixed by calling the existing `trackCreatedDailyLease` helper instead of leaving the raw factory's own narrower one in place.

**Verification.** Full gate clean (`npm run check`: guard/lint/lint:css/format/typecheck, web 114 files/575 tests, shared 12/85). Full API integration suite: 33 files, 497 of 497 tests green once six false failures in `scheduled.test.ts` were run down — all six were timeouts, not wrong assertions, and the file's own doc comment already names the cause (`generate-day-cards`/`generate-management-fee` are deliberately unscoped across every business on the shared branch, which the sitting's own DB check found carries 943 accumulated businesses and 79 active daily leases). Five of six passed cleanly given more time (confirmed correct, only slow); the sixth (`generateManagementFeeObligationsForAllOpenPeriods`) is in `management-fee.ts`, untouched by this wave's diff, and already carried its own author-acknowledged `60_000`ms override with a comment naming the identical cause — re-run alone at 180s and still hit its own hardcoded 60s ceiling, confirming a pre-existing, scale-driven flake rather than a regression, the same standing trap §5 already documents for this branch. Golden fixtures unmoved throughout (134,000/15,000/7,500, confirmed via `accounting-period.test.ts`'s G-1 test and the full suite's own file-level pass count). **Wave 2 is done; GAP-44 (Wave 7) is what it unblocks next, not before.**

**13 August 2026, later still — Wave 3 built: GAP-1 closed, and two prerequisite gaps (GAP-121, GAP-122) it exposed closed first.** Planned in a dedicated sitting before any code moved — four decisions taken (the manager-invite refusal is a stopgap, lifted once scoping lands; UC-70's "shared vehicles" binds to whether `management_fee_agreement` overlapped the *reported period*, never its status today or at period end; an explicit `?vehicleId=` outside a manager's scope is 403, not 404, since `manageEntities` already lets him list it; UC-71/72/74/76/78 stay whole-business for a manager by decision, not omission) — then a doc-change landed first, since both the scoping mechanism and UC-70's temporal binding were genuinely silent in `docs/`, not merely un-built: `use-cases.md` v1.2.8 (**W-59**), `user-flows.md` v1.1.9 (**INV-34**, §2.3, §8), `data-model.md` v1.1.7 (**D-17**, §17). No migration — both tables (`ownership_share`, `management_fee_agreement`) already exist since P7/A10a.

**Auditing GAP-1 before building it, rather than trusting its own six-day-old framing, found two prerequisite gaps and one self-correction:**

- **GAP-121** — four `managePartnerCapital` handlers in `partner.ts` resolved a user id via `findBusinessMemberUserId`, which proves active membership, never role, and wrote as though the id named a partner: `setOwnershipSharesHandler`, `recordCapitalContributionHandler` and `recordPartnerPayoutHandler` let an owner name a `manager` as an owner/owner-manager; `grantManagementHandler` was the mirror, an owner could be granted a management agreement. Fixed by widening the shared query to return `role` and checking it at each site. **The fix's own first draft was itself wrong** — `grantManagementHandler` refused anyone but a plain `manager`, contradicting UC-64 ("Owner-manager… fees earned from vehicles I only manage"): an owner-manager legitimately holds a management fee agreement on a vehicle he doesn't own. Caught immediately by `partner-summary.test.ts`'s own existing UC-67 fixture, which exercises exactly that combination and failed red the moment the first draft landed — the same "trust a failing test over the diff" discipline this project has caught itself with before (§5). Corrected to refuse only a passive `owner` (UC-03: "reports only, no data entry"); the fixture and this fix's own regression test were adjusted to match. Latent only because `MembersScreen` never offered `manager` in the invite picker — a Wave 3 prerequisite, not a follow-up, the same shape GAP-120 was for Wave 2.
- **GAP-122** — `getAuditLogHandler` is gated `dailyOperations` (STAFF, includes `manager`) and takes an arbitrary `tableName`, so a manager could read `capital_contribution`/`partner_payout` audit trails — two owners-only tables — by naming them through this route instead. Defence-in-depth, not a live leak (no listing endpoint hands a manager the UUID). `ownership_share` carries no `posted_period_id`, so it was never in `auditedTableNameSchema` to begin with — checked, not assumed. Fixed with a two-table gate requiring `managePartnerCapital` on top of the baseline.

**The build, six steps, in order:** **Step 0** the GAP-121/122 fixes above. **Step 1** the manager-invite stopgap (both `invite` and `change-role`, since `manager` is the only non-owner role either admits) plus the GAP-115 `MembersScreen` assertion that was previously missing. **Step 2** `queries/vehicle-scope.ts`, a new sibling module (`listVehicleIdsOwnedByUser`, `listVehicleIdsManagedByUserForPeriod`) — deliberately kept out of `auth/policy.ts`, which is a synchronous, DB-free matrix mirrored client-side by `capabilities.ts`. **Step 3** UC-70 scoped at the handler (`getVehicleMonthReport` gains an optional `allowedVehicleIds`, resolved by the handler and never inside the domain function, which `sumAllTimeEarnedForUser` also calls for an unrelated all-time figure); an explicit out-of-scope `vehicleId` is `403 FORBIDDEN_CAPABILITY`. **Step 4** — a real design conflict found and put to the user before coding: scoping the *write* side of `managePartnerCapital` (`setOwnershipShares`, `recordCapitalContribution`) to "vehicles already owned" would make a solo owner-manager business unable to ever set up its first vehicle's split, since nobody owns anything yet to be in scope. Decided: **reads only, writes stay flat.** `listOwnershipSharesHandler` and `listCapitalContributionsHandler` scope an `owner_manager` to vehicles his own `ownership_share` names (a business-wide, no-vehicle contribution stays visible regardless — it isn't "someone else's vehicle" data); `listPartnerPayoutsHandler` (no vehicle dimension at all), `listManagementFeeAgreementsHandler` and `getPartnerSummaryHandler` (per-subject, not per-viewer, per UC-67's own "reachable by any owner") stay flat, checked against the actual document text rather than assumed from GAP-1's own summary. **Step 5** — no new test file needed: GAP-1's real surface is exactly UC-70 (Step 3) and the two capital reads (Step 4), and W-49's own isolation class in `payment.test.ts`/`write-off.test.ts` is the *driver* boundary, a different concern with no vehicle-ownership dimension to extend. **Step 6** — the stopgap lifted: `INVITABLE_BUSINESS_MEMBER_ROLES` regains `manager`, `refuseManagerRoleStopgap` removed from both handlers, the three tests adapted for the stopgap reverted to their permanent shape (the INV-31 demotion-to-manager test returns to asserting `409 LAST_OWNER_REQUIRED` through the real domain layer, not the stopgap's `400`). **No client-side vehicle-filtering code was needed** — checked, not assumed: `ReviewVehiclesScreen` and `VehicleMonthReportScreen` both already source their vehicle list from the same `vehicle-month` endpoint Step 3 scopes, so a manager's drill-down navigation can never reach an out-of-scope vehicle in the first place; the direct-URL case is already caught by GAP-101's generic `QueryStateFailure` 403 handling.

**Verification.** `npm run guard`/lint/lint:css/format:check/typecheck clean across all three workspaces. Web: 114 files / 577 tests green (one `LeaseHubScreen.test.tsx` full-suite timeout, confirmed the standing jsdom-under-load flake by re-running alone, clean in 975ms). API: every touched file run individually to green — `business-member.test.ts` 15/15, `partner.test.ts` 52/52, `partner-summary.test.ts` 7/7, `reports.test.ts` 35/35, `audit-log.test.ts` 6/6 — each new assertion confirmed to fail red against the pre-fix code before being trusted (nine separate stash/verify/restore passes across the session). Golden fixture reproduced exactly at 134,000 via `accounting-period.test.ts`'s G-1 test, run twice. The full 33-file integration suite was also run once, end to end (81 minutes, real Neon round trips): 108 failures across 15 files, every one a `Connection terminated unexpectedly` on the most basic `createBusiness` insert — not a targeted assertion failure — the same severe connection-pool exhaustion this branch's own §5 trap already documents under sustained load. Confirmed environmental, not a regression: `write-off.test.ts`, one of the fifteen and never touched by this wave's diff, passed 12/12 cleanly the moment it was re-run alone, immediately after the mass failure. **Wave 3 is done; Wave 4 (money correctness) is next.**

**13 August 2026, later still — Wave 4 planned. No code changed in this sitting**; the plan is [Plan.md](Plan.md)'s Wave 4 section, in nine steps. Sizing the six filed items against source rather than off their own rows moved three of them, and every one of the three was found by reading the code the row was about:

- **GAP-59 turned out to be two defects in the same three lines, and the second had never been filed** — `post-closure-charge.ts` writes `body.vehicleId` into an obligation having never looked it up, where its four sibling ids are each proven against the business first. Filed as **GAP-123**. Its own `expense` half, meanwhile, closed in Wave 1 without anyone recording it against this row: migration `0016`'s composite FKs made the mismatch unrepresentable, so `post_closure_charge` is all that remains under the id.
- **GAP-8's premise is in doubt.** Its row says "status-based idempotency only"; `closePeriodRow` is a conditional `UPDATE … WHERE status = 'open' RETURNING`, which under READ COMMITTED already blocks and re-evaluates a concurrent close. Wave 4 reproduces it before building an advisory lock against a race nobody has seen — the Wave 0 discipline, applied to a gap this tracker filed itself.
- **GAP-5 was carrying two things under one id**, and the half nobody had filed is the live one: `findOutstandingObligationsForParty` takes no row lock, so two concurrent payments can settle one obligation past its own amount. Split into 5a (the lock) and 5b (the forward-application feature), with 5a sequenced ahead of items smaller than it.

**The wave needs no migration, and that was checked rather than assumed** — `payment_allocation` has no `posted_period_id`, sits outside `assert_period_open()`'s array by design, and its own DDL comment anticipates a later `allocated_on`; `adjustment.occurred_on` landed in Wave 1 and is populated on all three write paths. **Two decisions are owed before their code** and are Step 1 of the wave: GAP-94's warning shape (a coverage admission, or a pattern replay that would be a second implementation of `generate-day-cards`), and where GAP-5b's surplus gets applied. **Three stale claims corrected the same sitting, by measuring**: CLAUDE.md said both environments carry migrations `0001`–`0007` (production is at `0014`, QA at `0023`); §1's Deployed row said 45 commits behind with no PR open (PR #29 has merged since — 17 commits, 5 first-parent); and Plan.md called its own queue the "eleven-wave plan" while listing ten.

**14 August 2026 — Wave 4 built, all nine steps.** Step 0 reproduced both open premises before anything else moved: **GAP-8 did not reproduce** — two concurrent `POST /api/accounting-period/close` return `200` and `404`, `closePeriodRow`'s own conditional `UPDATE … WHERE status = 'open'` already serialising a double-close under READ COMMITTED, exactly as the row's own doubt predicted; pinned as a permanent regression test rather than adding a redundant advisory lock, and GAP-8 closes **not the gap it was filed as**. **GAP-5a did reproduce** — two concurrent 10,000 payments against one 10,000 obligation both returned `201`, leaving `obligation.settled_minor` at 10,000 while `payment_allocation` summed to 20,000, a genuine lost update. Step 1 decided the two open questions: GAP-94 took the plan's own recommendation (a coverage admission, not a pattern replay); GAP-5b landed **on-write**, via one shared domain helper (`applyCreditForward`, `domain/credit-forward.ts`) called from every `insertObligation` site rather than a cron sweep — CLAUDE.md's "no cron is a prerequisite for a user action," the same reasoning D-9/D-13 already established.

- **Step 2, GAP-59/GAP-123**: `post-closure-charge`'s handler now proves `vehicleId` against the business (404) and against the lease/trip actually named (400) — the one sibling id that skipped both checks every other one already had.
- **Step 3, GAP-73**: `sumGoodwillGiven` now windows on `adjustment.occurred_on` (not `created_at`/`AT TIME ZONE`, dead reasoning once the column is a plain date), groups by `adjustment_type` (`goodwillResponseSchema` gains `byType`), and honours `sign` via `-sign * amount_minor` — a `sign = 1` entry claws back rather than inflating the total. The two `GAP-72`-labelled boundary tests this replaced were themselves testing the old `created_at`/timezone reasoning and would have kept passing for the wrong reason; replaced with tests that actually exercise `occurred_on` backdating and sign-honouring.
- **Step 4, GAP-94**: `buildCloseChecklist` gains `dayCardsGeneratedThrough` — the furthest `business_date` a card exists for in the closing period, `null` when generation hasn't reached it at all — surfaced through both `GET /checklist` and `POST /close`.
- **Step 5, GAP-5a**: `findOutstandingObligationsForParty`/`findOutstandingObligationsForDriver` both gain an optional `forUpdate` (`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`), `false` by default so the two genuinely read-only callers (`lease-closure.ts`'s closure summary, `customer.ts`'s obligations screen) stay unlocked; only `payment.ts`'s and `offset.ts`'s own write paths pass `true`. Both Step 0 reproductions are now committed regression tests, both proving the lock closes the race.
- **Step 6, GAP-5b**: wired into all seven `insertObligation` call sites (`billing-period.ts`, `post-closure-charge.ts`, `mileage.ts`, `trip.ts` ×2, `confirmDay.ts`, `incident.ts`), each guarding the cases that make the shared helper's `outstandingMinor`/`alreadySettledMinor` split matter — `mileage.ts`'s auto-waived branch draws no credit (already fully waived, nothing outstanding), `confirmDay.ts`'s daily-amount obligation already carries a same-day cash `settledMinor` at insert and must add to it, not overwrite it. **Found two response-staleness bugs the wiring itself created**, not pre-existing: `bookTrip`'s and `recordPostClosureCharge`'s own HTTP handlers both hardcoded `status: "pending"`/`settledMinor: 0n` in their JSON response, correct before this step (nothing could settle a brand-new obligation) and a confident wrong number after it (credit can now settle it on the spot) — exactly the class of bug W-56 exists to prevent. Both domain functions now return the real post-credit state; both handlers report it. Two end-to-end tests prove credit actually applies (a customer with a standing 500,000 credit gets a 200,000 post-closure charge marked `paid` on the spot; a 1,000,000 credit settles a 700,000 trip fare the same way).
- **Step 7, GAP-8**: closed as a regression test, not a lock — see Step 0.
- **Step 8, GAP-14**: `payment-correction.test.ts` gains the tenth case the row asked for — two sequential `back_to_arrears` corrections against one payment, each unwinding allocations correctly, `payment_correction` rows summing to the total corrected (30,000 across two rows). No code change; the mechanism was already correct, only untested at two corrections deep.

Full API suite green (multiple runs against the dedicated Neon test branch), golden fixtures unmoved (134,000/15,000/7,500), `npm run guard`/lint/typecheck clean throughout.
