**14 August 2026, later — the QA/design pass evaluated, and one of its two gaps re-diagnosed.** `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-14.md`, UI §7.11/M-31, UI §14/§15 and BR §5.5 landed with GAP-124 and GAP-125 already filed and slotted into Wave 7. Evaluated against source rather than accepted on the filing, this project's standing rule for any review — and **the filing held on scope and moved on both cause and position**:

- **GAP-125 is not a GAP-104 regression.** Its row proposed that class "until proven otherwise", so this is the proving. A `useMobileHistoryDismiss` fault presents as *the wrong sheet closing*; it cannot present as a correctly-titled sheet containing nothing, and the manager's same-commit handoff provably nets to zero before touching `window.history`. The real cause is a **GAP-101 miss**: [`ReasonPicker`](web/src/components/ReasonPicker.tsx#L35) has an `error` branch and no pending branch, and `QuickAddSheet` feeds all three of its pickers from `?? []` over `enabled:`-gated queries, so each renders an empty list for its own fetch's whole flight. **The report's evidence supports this over the filed cause**: 105px is a sheet with a title and zero rows, both `y` values are mid-animation, and "a slower run loaded the picker fine" is what a completed fetch looks like — not something broken layout recovers from. Blast radius is three pickers, not one; the other three `ReasonPicker` call sites are static and unaffected, checked rather than assumed. **A guard gap rides with it**: `checkQueryErrorHandling` skips a whole file when `useQueryState` appears anywhere in it, so three error-only wirings exempted every unhandled pending state beside them — the same file-wide-scope weakness Wave 2 worked around in the sibling `money/void-table-unfiltered` rule without fixing here. **Reproduced live 14 Aug 2026 (S0-1), against `qa.fleetsettle.com`, and it confirms the diagnosis exactly.** Chromium touch/390×844/Slow 4G, cold query cache, sampled on a tight interval rather than a settled snapshot — a settled snapshot is what hid this on the first, unpolled attempt at the same steps. Cold cache: the picker held at 105px/one button (Close only) for the full 500–800ms fetch flight, then jumped to 389px/seven buttons the moment `/api/vehicle` resolved. Warm cache, identical throttling and click sequence: 389px/seven buttons from the first sampled frame (17ms) — only the slide-up position still animates. **The geometry is not incidental to the bug — it is caused by it**: the sheet's height is driven by its content, so an empty list produces exactly the "stuck below viewport" shape for the entire fetch flight, not one passing frame of it.
- **GAP-124 splits, because its halves want opposite positions.** The route-by-route pass (**124b**) is right to follow the flows it styles and stays in Wave 7 as filed. The shared primitives (**124a**, M-31's own step 1) are consumed by nearly every screen Wave 5 builds — twelve void sheets, an end-a-lease screen, three calendar states, two settlement sheets, a printed slip — so landing them after Wave 5 means building all of it in a language already scheduled for replacement. **124a also absorbs B16 Phase 2 and 124b absorbs B16 Phase 3**, checked against both texts: Phase 2 restates M-31 step 1 almost verbatim, Phase 3 restates §7.11's Reports and Review rows. Wave 7 listed B16 and GAP-124 as separate items, which would have had someone do that work twice.
- **Both move to Wave 5 · Step 0, a shared prelude on the base branch**, with both track branches rebasing after it lands. Neither is large; both sit at the root of the client's dependency graph, which is the same argument ordering rule 1 makes for `packages/shared`. **5A is not blocked** — its server halves start immediately and only its screens need the rebase.

**14 August 2026, later still — Track 5B's own Step 0 landed, on `wave5b/corrections-2026-08-14`.** GAP-36's archive-refusal decision (12/14 Aug) had no invariant and no flow to hang it on — `use-cases.md` gained **W-60** and **UC-100**, `user-flows.md` gained **INV-35** and **F-1.11**, naming the exact check the A9b endpoint must run: `obligation.status IN ('pending','part_paid')` (either direction), `deposit.status IN ('held','hold_window')`, and — driver only — `advance.status IN ('open','part_settled')`; voided rows never count, and INV-3's no-netting rule carries over so a driver's two open figures are named separately, never combined. No schema change — migration `0023` already carries the `voided_*` trio A9b writes through. `npm run guard` clean. **5B-1's void endpoints are next**, against `voidExpense`/`voidExpenseRow` as the reference shape.

**14 August 2026, still later — 5B-1's first real slice built: driver and customer archive/unarchive.** `POST /api/{driver,customer}/{id}/archive` and `/unarchive`, `domain/party-archive.ts`'s `findOpenMoneyForParty` shared by both rather than duplicating the INV-35 check per party. Two things the plan hadn't specified, resolved by building rather than by asking: `archivedAt` is `.optional()` on the wire, not only `.nullable()`, so the ~15 existing web mocks predating this field keep compiling with zero edits to Track 5A's territory; and **`reason` turned out required, not optional as F-1.11/UC-100 first said** — migration `0023`'s own `CHECK` on `driver`/`customer` already refused `voided_at` set with an empty `voided_reason`, caught by the test suite as a 500 instead of the 200 expected, and the docs were corrected the same sitting (`use-cases.md`, `user-flows.md`, both bumped again) rather than left describing a shape the schema had never allowed. 20 new integration tests (the due/payable-never-netted case, deposit `held`/`hold_window`, driver-only advance, voided rows excluded, cross-tenant 404, already-archived 409, missing-reason 400), full `npm run check` clean. **Still open in 5B-1: `POST /api/vehicle/{id}/archive` (the `lifecycle` column, unmoved since `0001`) and the twelve money-table void endpoints**, against `voidExpense`/`voidExpenseRow` as the reference shape.

**14 August 2026, after PR #44 merged — GAP-12's nine remaining tables: every cascade decision asked and answered, then re-validated against practical use, before any code.** The same "decisions before code" order this queue has used since GAP-70/71. Each table was read first (what the forward path actually mutates), so the questions were about real forks, not hypotheticals. **The principle that emerged and decided five of the nine: cascade into rows minted by the same call; refuse when separately-entered user actions sit beneath.** A `write_off_recovery`'s payment is minted by `recordWriteOffRecovery` itself and the manager never enters it separately → cascade. An `advance_settlement`, a `write_off_recovery` under a write-off, a receipt under an incident recovery are all separately-entered facts → refuse and name them.

**The re-validation pass changed two answers, which is the reason it was worth doing.** Both had been decided on internal consistency and failed when walked through as a real correction a manager would attempt:

- **`adjustment` flipped from refuse to allow-with-credit.** The first answer refused when reversing a `+1` adjustment would push `amount_minor` below `settled_minor`, telling the manager to reverse the receipt first. Walking it through: there is no honest way to do that. `correctPayment` is UC-93, explicitly *"the entry was right and the money genuinely did not arrive"* — using it on cash that **did** arrive misdescribes physical money; a waiver would record a chosen discount (W-28/INV-14) for what was a typo. Both misdescribe. The only description matching reality — he paid 1,500, he owed 1,000, he is 500 up — is to unwind the allocation and let the surplus sit as unallocated credit, which is DM §10.2's existing convention and moves no cash, only what it is allocated against.
- **`incident_recovery`'s premise was simply wrong.** The first answer refused while money was received, filing "there is no un-receive path" as a new gap. There is one: `recordIncidentRecoveryReceived` **overwrites** `received_amount_minor`, so re-recording corrects both the recovery and its obligation. But `recordRecoveryReceived` mints a **fresh `payment` on every call, unconditionally** — so correcting a receipt silently double-posts cash into UC-75's position and the customer's payment history. **A live bug, pre-existing and unrelated to voiding**, found only by asking how a manager would actually fix a fat-fingered recovery. The void keeps refusing; the re-record path gets fixed instead, since it is the one a manager would reach for first.

**Two further findings, both pre-existing:** **`offset_allocation` never received the W-58 void trio** — migration `0022` gave it to `vehicle_day_allocation`, `payment_allocation`, `day_record` and `opening_balance_entry` but not to this one, despite it being structurally identical to `payment_allocation` (a child allocation row moving `obligation.settled_minor`), and `0023` closed its `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so today an offset allocation cannot be undone by any legal route. That is Wave 5's second migration. And **three read-side filters are mandatory alongside their writes** — `sumDepositMovements`, `sumSettledForAdvance`, and the new `offset_allocation` reads all currently ignore `voided_at`, which is GAP-120's exact shape (trio added, read side unaware, money silently unchanged by a void).

**One cross-cutting consequence for Track B, stated now rather than discovered later:** four of the nine decisions refuse with a "void the children first" workflow. On 360 × 640 that is only usable if each refusal names the blocking rows and the client can reach them — otherwise U-5's "every figure can be corrected later" is true in the API and false in the hand.

**14 August 2026, later still — GAP-12's doc-change landed, before any of the nine endpoints.** `use-cases.md` gains **W-61** (correcting a mistake undoes exactly what the correcting act minted, never what a separate act entered separately) and references it from UC-96; `user-flows.md` gains **INV-36** and extends **F-8.5** with the per-table mechanics for all nine (`adjustment`, `offset_record`, `deposit_movement`, `advance`, `advance_settlement`, `write_off`, `write_off_recovery`, `incident_recovery`, `obligation`), the two answers considered and declined (refuse-only for `adjustment`, the offsetting-entry convention for `deposit_movement`), and the two closed-period facts stated explicitly rather than left implicit. §8's traceability updated to match. `npm run guard` clean. The full reasoning stays in `GAP-12-VOID-CASCADE-DESIGN.md` (repo root, now committed) rather than restated here.

**Same sitting — cherry-picked onto `wave5a/occupancy-2026-08-14` as well, docs-only.** Track A had not started and was still sitting behind Track 5B's own Step 0 (the INV-35/UC-100 archive-refusal decision, 14 Aug, and its same-day reason-required correction) — both doc-only slices were cherry-picked across first, then this GAP-12 doc-change on top, so `wave5a` now carries every doc change `wave5b` does with none of `wave5b`'s endpoint code. Verified byte-identical on the three doc files (`diff` against `wave5b`, clean) before pushing. **5B-1's nine void endpoints are next** on this track, unblocked; Track A can now start from current docs.

**14 August 2026, later still — GAP-12's nine void endpoints built, all of them, plus the migration, the two read-side filters, and the `recordRecoveryReceived` fix, in one sitting.** Migration `0024` gives `offset_allocation` the void trio (applied and drift-checked against the shared `test-parallel` Neon branch, not against `main` — that stays for the deploy pipeline, per DEPLOYMENT.md). `sumDepositMovements`/`sumSettledForAdvance` now filter `voided_at`, closing the GAP-120 shape the design doc's own §5 called out before any endpoint used them. Per table: **`adjustment`** reverses via a new `reverseAdjustmentOnObligation` combined write, unwinding `payment_allocation` newest-first through a new `findPaymentAllocationsForObligation` (obligation-scoped, alongside the existing payment-scoped one) when the reversal pushes `amount_minor` below `settled_minor + waived_minor` · **`obligation`** direct-voids only `kind = 'post_closure_charge'` (the design doc said `source_type`, which turned out not to hold for any real row — `post-closure-charge.ts` sets `sourceType` to the lease or trip it's against, never the literal string; corrected against the actual insert path, not the doc's wording) and refuses (`VOID_BLOCKED`) while a live `payment_allocation`, `offset_allocation` or `adjustment` sits against it · **`deposit_movement`** voids and recomputes `deposit.status` unconditionally on every void (always correct, since it's pure re-derivation) · **`advance_settlement`** recomputes `advance.status`; **`advance`** refuses (`VOID_BLOCKED`) while any settlement is live · **`write_off`** restores the linked obligation's status via `computeObligationStatus` and refuses while a recovery is live; **`write_off_recovery`** cascades `markPaymentReversed` · **`incident_recovery`** refuses once `received_amount_minor > 0`, else cascades `voidObligationBySource` · **`offset_record`** unwinds both allocation sides symmetrically (INV-3), reversing each touched obligation's `settled_minor`/status before voiding the allocation, then the record. One new shared error, `VoidBlockedError` (`VOID_BLOCKED`, carrying `details.blocking`), used by every refuse-guard instead of one bespoke class per case — the "same shape `PartyHasOpenMoneyError` already uses" the design doc asked for. Nine `*AlreadyVoidedError` classes, one per table, matching the existing Capital­Contribution/BankingEvent precedent exactly.

**Two things found and fixed building it, neither in the design doc:** `createAdjustmentHandler`'s response never carried the adjustment's own id — only the resulting obligation's, "one round trip instead of two" — so nothing a caller held after creating one could ever address it for a later void; fixed by adding `adjustmentId` to the response (`createdAdjustmentResponseSchema`), the same way `deposit`'s movement id and `advance`'s settlement id needed adding for their own void endpoints to be reachable at all. And `recordRecoveryReceived`'s double-post (§3.9, flagged as a known pre-existing bug in the doc-change) is fixed: before posting a new payment it now finds and reverses whatever this obligation's own live `payment_allocation` already is (`voidPaymentAllocation`, `markPaymentReversed`) — first call finds nothing to reverse, a correction reverses exactly one, so there is never more than one active payment behind a recovery.

`check-forbidden.mjs`'s `money/void-table-unfiltered` rule widened to all nine tables (verified table-by-table first: every file touching one already references `<table>.voidedAt` somewhere, so widening added no new findings) — `capitalContribution`/`bankingEvent`/`partnerPayout` and `obligation` deliberately left out, since none of them is ever summed across rows the way GAP-120's four originals were. 23 new integration tests (`tests/integration/void-cascade.test.ts`), covering the happy cascade and its own assertion (status recompute, allocation unwound into credit, both offset sides reversed, payment marked reversed) plus 409-already-voided, 409 `VOID_BLOCKED` naming the blocker, and cross-tenant 404 for each — all 23 green against the shared `test-parallel` branch. `npm run guard`/`lint`/`typecheck` clean across every workspace.

**14 August 2026, later still — PR #42's `checks / gate` failed on push, and Gitar's full comment history on the PR was swept for anything still open.** The gate failure was `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` on `recoveredBody` at `void-cascade.test.ts:503` — the recovery response was captured only to consume the body, never read; the write-off-with-live-recovery test now just asserts `recovered.status` instead. Fixed and pushed (`05aee57`); lint clean, the one affected test re-run green against `test-parallel`. **The sweep also re-confirmed all five of Gitar's PR #42 findings against current source, not just against this row's own prior prose**: the double-void race (partner) and the archive TOCTOU race are both fixed, exactly as this row already said. The other three were never given their own gap id and are added now rather than left to live only inside this paragraph — **GAP-131** (redundant party lookup), **GAP-132** (N+1 deposit-sum), **GAP-133** (disposed-vehicle lifecycle guard), all below.

**14 August 2026, later still — GAP-60 built, closing 5B-1 (GAP-12 + GAP-36 + GAP-60) for good.** The nine cascade endpoints and the archive pair were already live; the one piece left was the linkage itself — thirteen tables carrying a `replaces_id` column since migration `0021` with nothing anywhere writing to it. Built table by table against a reference shape proved twice first (`expense`, then `capital_contribution`/`banking_event`/`partner_payout` in `domain/partner.ts`) before repeating it across the remaining nine: `adjustment`, `offset_record`, `deposit_movement`, `advance` and `advance_settlement`, `write_off` and `write_off_recovery`, `incident_recovery` (both its create paths — a customer's own contribution and an insurer's claim, since either can be the one entered wrong), and `obligation` (only through a post-closure charge, the one kind INV-36 lets void directly). Every create path now takes an optional `replacesId`: validated in the same transaction the period check already opened (the named row must belong to this business, else 404; must already be voided, else the new shared `ReplacesTargetNotVoidedError`, 409), and written alongside the insert. **Migration `0025`** — not planned for this wave, found necessary while designing the validation — adds a partial unique index per table (`WHERE replaces_id IS NOT NULL`) so two concurrent replies at the same voided row can't both land; the loser gets the new shared `ReplacesTargetAlreadyReplacedError`, the identical constraint-not-code shape the void race itself already uses. Verified against a disposable Neon branch forked from `qa` (not `main`, not the shared `test-parallel` branch already in use): migration applied clean, the DM §13 drift assertion clean, all 26 new integration tests green plus the full existing integration and unit suites, `npm run check` clean throughout. Deliberately left out of scope: threading `replacesId` through every narrower per-context list (a vehicle's own cost tab, a trip's own cost breakdown) — the general list-for-business read and the record's own create response both carry it, which is what F-8.6 actually needs, and going further would be the same UI-surfacing work GAP-81 already owns for `voidedAt`. Scratch branch set to auto-expire 12 hours after creation rather than torn down by hand.

**14 August 2026, later still — PR #45 opened against `develop`, and Gitar's review found one real gap, fixed the same sitting.** The validation built above proved a `replacesId` target belongs to this business and is voided, but never that it belongs to the *same parent* as the new record — Gitar's finding: an adjustment could name a voided adjustment against a **different** obligation, or a recovery a **different** incident, leaving F-8.6's "what corrected this?" pointing at an unrelated fact rather than answering it. Fixed with a parent-scope check (400, the same shape GAP-59/GAP-123's own id-consistency fix already uses) everywhere a table has one real parent: `adjustment` (`obligationId`), `incident_recovery` (`incidentId`, both create paths), `offset_record`/`advance` (`driverId`), `deposit_movement` (`depositId`), `advance_settlement` (`advanceId`), `write_off_recovery` (`writeOffId`) — Gitar named the first two, the same class of bug generalised cleanly to the rest, checked and fixed rather than left half-closed. `write_off` and `obligation` (post-closure-charge) have no single FK to check, so their check is party identity instead; `obligation`'s needed a new narrow query (`findObligationPartyForReplacesCheck`) rather than widening the shared `ObligationForAdjustment` row, which is also used to build response objects elsewhere in `adjustment.ts` and broke two of them the first time this was tried (caught by typecheck before it shipped). `expense` stays deliberately exempt — F-8.5 names "wrong vehicle" as a legitimate correction, so its replacement is expected to cross exactly the boundary these checks close for every other table. 8 new regression tests, one per fixed table. All four PR checks green: Gitar, SonarCloud, `checks / gate`, and `verify` (the full integration suite, 26m44s) — confirmed directly against the CI run, not assumed from a local pass.

**14 August 2026, later still — 5B-2 built (GAP-30, GAP-32, GAP-34), Track 5B's second item.** Plan.md's own order for the track is `5B-1 → 5B-2 → 5B-3 → 5B-4`; 5B-1 closed above, this is the next unblocked item. All three gaps rode the same A8 screen (the fuel-fill/expense form) and none needed a migration — every column and table involved has existed since `0001`. **GAP-30**: a fuel fill's own odometer reading now writes a real `odometer_reading` row inside the same transaction as the expense insert, the identical shape `bookTrip`'s opening reading and `startLease`'s handover reading already use, and sets the `expense.odometer_reading_id` column `listUsBoughtFuelFills` (UC-72's fuel-efficiency report) has been reading since it was written with nothing populating it. **GAP-32**: a new `GET /api/expense/borne-by-default` preview endpoint reuses `resolveBorneByDefault` rather than a second implementation of §6.7's matrix, letting a client show the default before offering an override to a different party. **GAP-34**: rather than build a last-touched-vehicle column (a new, undecided concept), "the vehicle with something pending" (U-3) reuses Home item 4's own unconfirmed-day definition, oldest first — one query, narrowed from the existing `listUnconfirmedDayRecordsForBusiness` filter, avoiding a second definition of "pending" for the same schema. All three are backend-only, consistent with 5B-1 and with Web-P8b already being the client's current phase-1 ceiling — no screen consumes any of the three new/changed endpoints yet. 15 new API integration tests (across all three gaps, including tenant isolation and the linked-driver 403 class), 3 new shared-schema unit tests, one end-to-end test proving GAP-30's full chain (create → `/api/reports/fuel-efficiency` → a real km/l figure). Verified against `gap-60-scratch`, the disposable Neon branch already forked from `qa` for the previous item's own testing (schema confirmed identical — no new migration, so no drift risk) — full integration suite green (44/44 in `expense.test.ts` alone), full `npm run check` (guard, lint, lint:css, format:check, typecheck, unit tests across all three workspaces) clean throughout. `web`'s typecheck required updating eight test fixtures with the new `odometerReadingId` field on `expenseResponseSchema` — a wire-schema addition, not a behaviour change, caught by the type system rather than a runtime failure.

**14 August 2026, later still — 5B-3 built (GAP-6, GAP-15), Track 5B's third item.** Both gaps had already been sized as "settle money from somewhere other than a fresh payment/cash arrival," and both turned out to need the exact same shape credit-forward.ts's own comment names: never `allocateAgainstOldest`'s generic oldest-first sweep when a specific record is what has to end up settled. **GAP-6**: `recordDepositMovement` (F-6.7/UC-58) now takes `obligationId`, required exactly when `movementType` is `'applied'`, validated against business/void/direction/same-party/outstanding-amount before moving `settled_minor` directly — and `settleLeaseDeposit` (F-2.6 step 6) gains the fourth action ("apply") its own docstring had explicitly deferred to this tracker, sweeping a customer's held deposit against their outstanding dues oldest-first, one `recordDepositMovement` call per obligation. **GAP-15**: `recordPostClosureCharge` gains `deductFromFee`, composing a new targeted `deductFromDriverFeeTx` (domain/offset.ts) into the charge's own transaction — settles *this* charge directly on the owed-to-us side (never `createOffset`'s oldest-first sweep, which could clear an unrelated older due instead of the one just created) while the owed-by-us side still sweeps oldest-first like an ordinary offset. **One real bug found and fixed building GAP-6's own tests, not before**: the test harness's `trackCreatedLease` teardown deleted `billing_period`-sourced obligations before clearing `deposit_movement.obligation_id` references to them, a foreign-key ordering nothing had ever hit because nothing had ever populated that column — now nulls the reference first, the same "clear the reference, don't delete the row it still owns" shape the rest of that teardown already uses. Neither gap needed a migration — every column involved has existed since `0001`. 18 new integration tests across `driver-money.test.ts`/`lease-closure.test.ts`/`post-closure-charge.test.ts`, 3 new shared-schema unit tests, `npm run check` clean throughout (guard, lint, lint:css, format:check, typecheck, full unit + integration suites). 5B-3 closes as filed; 5B-4 (GAP-68, the predictive maintenance prompt) is next, sequenced after 5B-2 since it reads the odometer history 5B-2 wrote.

**14 August 2026, later still — Track 5A's own first item, 5A-1 (GAP-7), built end to end on `wave5a/occupancy-2026-08-14`, with GAP-127 riding along as its own row said it would.** Trip `hold`/`in_progress` were reachable at the schema level since Wave 1 and nowhere in the domain layer since — `bookTrip` gains `asHold` (F-5.1 step 4/ST-5: "Confirm → booked, or hold if the enquiry is tentative"), which still reserves the calendar (`vehicle_day_allocation.is_hold: true`, so INV-1 still catches a real conflict) but defers pausing the daily lease's day records and raising the `trip_fare` obligation to a new `POST /api/trip/{id}/confirm` — F-5.1's own Accept clause, "a hold reserves the calendar but does not suppress daily cards," read literally rather than simplified away. Release is synchronous everywhere the three decided mechanics said it would be: `bookTrip`/`startDailyLease`/`startLease` each call a new `releaseExpiredHolds` (queries/trip.ts) for their own vehicle before touching an allocation, and a new `release-expired-holds` nightly cron unit only tidies up what nobody booked around. `in_progress` is derived (`deriveTripStatus()`, domain/trip.ts) at every read, never written. **One correctness gap found and closed in the same pass**: `closeTrip` had no guard against a still-`hold` trip — closing one directly would have skipped both the pause and the receivable it never got. Now refuses with `VALIDATION_ERROR` until confirmed. **GAP-127 closed alongside it**: `VehicleCalendarScreen`'s `byDate` map, built from `days ?? []` with no pending gate, read every date as free — startable, bookable — for the whole of the calendar's own initial fetch, on the one screen whose job is stopping a double-booking; fixed with the same `useQueryState` pattern its own error branch already used, and six pre-existing tests that had unknowingly depended on the bug were corrected from `getByTestId` to `findByTestId`. Client: `BookTripScreen` gains a "Hold instead" action beside "Book trip"; `TripDetailScreen` gains a `Confirm` primary action and a hold-expiry line for a `hold` trip, gating Close/advance-recording to `booked`/`in_progress` and Cancel to any of `hold`/`booked`/`in_progress`. **`0020`'s stale "Wave 2" comment attribution was deliberately not corrected** — the migration runner hashes each file's full text and would refuse on a checksum mismatch against every environment already past `0020` (QA is at `0023`); recorded here instead of edited there, per CLAUDE.md's forward-only rule. **Verification gap, stated plainly**: `npm run guard`/`eslint`/`tsc --noEmit` clean across all three workspaces and every touched web test green, but this session had no `TEST_DATABASE_URL` or Neon MCP access — the new `api` integration tests (`trip.test.ts`: hold booking, synchronous and cron-driven expiry, confirm happy/error paths, the close-guard, cancelling a hold; `scheduled.test.ts`: `release-expired-holds`) are written and self-consistent but **have not been run against real Postgres**. Whoever next touches this branch should run the full `api` suite once before trusting them, then continue with 5A-2 (GAP-25, end a daily lease).

**15 August 2026 — PR #45, #46 and #47 evaluated together, every open Gitar finding fixed, and `wave5a` merged into `wave5b` so both tracks land in one PR to `develop`.** PR #46 (`wave5a` → `wave5/plan-2026-08-14`) turned out to be redundant with #47 (`wave5a` → `develop`) — its base was already a fully-merged ancestor of `develop` — so only #45 and #47's own findings needed fixing before consolidating. **PR #45 (5B), three findings**: `findObligationForDepositApply` gained the same `forUpdate` flag GAP-5a's own driver/party lookups already carry, passed `true` from a new `recordDepositMovementTx` (the transactional core `recordDepositMovement` now wraps) so `settleLeaseDeposit`'s "apply" sweep could lock every outstanding obligation and settle them in one all-or-nothing transaction instead of one commit per obligation; `findVehicleWithOldestUnconfirmedDay` now joins `vehicle` and filters `lifecycle = 'active'`, so `/api/expense/prefill-vehicle` can no longer default a form to an archived vehicle. **PR #46/#47 (5A), two findings**: `releaseExpiredHolds` now returns the `(businessId, vehicleId)` pairs it released, and a new shared `restoreDailyLeaseOccupancy` (factored out of `cancelTrip`'s own GAP-119 restore) re-materialises each one's current daily-lease horizon — `bookTrip`, `startDailyLease`, `startLease` and the nightly cron all call it, closing the calendar-hole Gitar named; `findTripForBusiness` gained a `forUpdate` flag and `confirmTripHold` re-reads and locks the trip inside its own transaction rather than trusting the handler's pre-transaction read, closing the double-confirm/resurrected-hold race, with `confirmTripHoldRow`'s `UPDATE` itself guarded on `status = 'hold'` as a second, independent check. **Running the fixed code's own tests for the first time against real Postgres — the verification gap 5A-1's own row already named — found three more bugs neither review had caught**, detailed in this row's own entry above (`vehicle_day_allocation_void_check` migration `0026`, `cancelTripRow`'s `hold_expires_on`, two test-fixture gaps). All fixed, and the merge's two real conflicts (`TRACKER.md`'s own narrative, an import list in `handlers/vehicle.ts`) resolved by keeping whichever side had advanced further. Verified end to end on a fresh disposable Neon branch with migrations `0001`–`0026` applied from scratch: the full `api` integration suite (37 files, 657 tests) green, `guard`/`typecheck`/`lint`/`lint:css`/`format`/unit tests clean across all three workspaces. `wave5b/corrections-2026-08-14` now carries both tracks; #45 is the one PR that will carry them both to `develop`, with #46 and #47 to be closed as superseded once that merge is confirmed.

**15 August 2026, later still — 5B-4 built (GAP-68), closing Track 5B's fourth item; the migration count corrected; A14 deferred out of the wave.** `vehicle` gains an optional `service_interval_km` (migration `0028`), editable on the vehicle's own page and never required to save it (U-2). `GET /api/vehicle/{id}` returns a `maintenance` prompt — present only on the single-vehicle detail read, computed live from the vehicle's latest odometer reading on file against its last live `servicing` expense's own linked reading, degrading to `{ kmSinceLastServiceKm: null, due: false }` rather than a guess whenever either side has nothing recorded yet (W-56). Client: `SetServiceIntervalSheet` off `VehicleOverviewScreen`'s actions menu (blank clears the interval) and an informational-only `AlertStrip` when due, never blocking or pre-filling Record Expense per F-3.5's own Accept clause. `vehicle-maintenance.test.ts` and the touched web suite pass against real Postgres; an incidental migration-checksum drift on the test-parallel Neon branch (`0026`'s tracked checksum vs. its committed content — the live schema already matched, only a comment had changed since it first applied there) was found blocking `0028` from applying and is fixed, QA/production untouched.

**Checking the migration count while closing this out found decision 4's own record (§0, 14 Aug, above) wrong in two places, not one.** "This is Wave 5's only migration, `0024`" was true of nothing by the time this sitting started: `0024` had already gone to GAP-12/5B-1's `offset_allocation` void trio, decided and built the same day as decision 4 itself; `0025` (GAP-60's `replaces_id` constraints) and `0026` (5A-1's own fixes) followed, then `0028` (this item). **`0027` (5A-3/GAP-20's `lease_day_exception_unique_live`) was never recorded against either document at all** — Plan.md's own rule for exactly this case ("a second Wave 5 migration means a fact nobody planned for; stop and say so") went unfollowed twice. Wave 5 shipped five migrations, not one: `0024` (5B-1/GAP-12), `0025` (5B-1/GAP-60), `0026` (5A-1), `0027` (5A-3/GAP-20), `0028` (5B-4/GAP-68). Recorded here and against each row.

**A14/GAP-65 deferred out of Wave 5, checked rather than assumed while sizing 5B-5's own backend-only build.** The three endpoints this item was scoped as (issue, revoke, public read) have no reachable consumer without the actual unauthenticated page a driver opens the link on — unlike 5B-2/3's own backend-only endpoints, which had real future callers already implied by flows that already exist. That page is real, previously undiscussed scope: every route in `web/` sits behind `AuthGate` today (`main.tsx` wraps the entire router in it on the non-stub path), so this client has no unauthenticated path at all yet, and building A14's backend alone would ship three endpoints nothing can call. The two decisions already made — 30-day expiry, a stored revocable row — stand for whenever it is picked up; what changes is that it is resized and built whole next time (backend, the `AuthGate` exception, and the share button together), not split the way this wave's own plan had it.

**Wave 5 closes as Track 5A (5A-1…5A-5, all built) and Track 5B-1 through 5B-4 (all built); A14/GAP-65 carries forward, unscheduled, to a future wave.**

**15 August 2026, later still — PR #49 opened (`wave5-remainder/2026-08-15` → `develop`), and five money-correctness/race bugs from gitar-bot's review of PR #48/#49 fixed the same sitting.** All five are the "voiding/reading a row without accounting for a concurrent write" shape this project has hit before (GAP-118/119/120, the archive TOCTOU fix on PR #42) — found by review, not by this session's own reading, and worth naming precisely rather than folding into "code review fixes": **`voidDepositMovement`** (domain/deposit.ts) never reversed `obligation.settled_minor` for an `applied` movement, so voiding one left the same money readable as both held and settled at once. **`recordRecoveryReceived`** (domain/incident.ts) had no `voided_at` guard on its own `UPDATE`, so a concurrent `voidIncidentRecovery` could still settle an obligation and mint a payment against a recovery already voided — found auditing for the shape below, not independently reported. **`createLeaseDayException`** (domain/dailyLease.ts) read the day card before opening its own transaction and reused that stale read inside it, so a concurrent `confirmDay` could leave one day both confirmed and marked skipped. **`getVehicleMaintenanceStatus`** (domain/vehicles.ts) — this session's own GAP-68, above. **`settleLeaseDeposit`**'s "apply" branch (domain/lease-closure.ts) read the held balance outside its transaction; moved inside defensively, since `recordDepositMovementTx` already re-validates it live — a spurious-failure risk rather than a live correctness bug, fixed for consistency with the other four rather than because it was independently wrong. New tests in `vehicle-maintenance.test.ts`, `lease-day-exception.test.ts` and a new `void-cascade.test.ts` file. Commit `69a8437`, on top of `3070ece`; PR #49's own CI (gate, integration, Gitar, SonarCloud) all green against it.

**15 August 2026, later still — GAP-124b's own first slice built, closing GAP-126 along the way.** UI §7.11's route-inventory table is ten rows; this pass touched nine of them and finished three (Auth entry, First-run setup, Operate home), left six partial, and left Mine untouched — the full per-row account is on GAP-124's own row above rather than duplicated here. Two things worth naming precisely: **GAP-126 closed as a side effect of Home's own rebuild**, not a separate pass — the same `allSectionsReady` gate the cockpit layout needed is exactly the fix that row was waiting on. **GAP-125's diagnosed root cause was already fixed (14 Aug, S0-2)**; this pass added a second, independent guard on top of it (`QuickAddSheet`'s source sheet now closes whenever any child does) rather than touching the actual defect again — recorded against GAP-125's own row as hardening, not as re-closing something already closed. Not run this pass: `npm run check`, the full web/api suites, or §7.11's own verification step (browser route sweep at 360×640/390×844/`lg`, axe-core, 200% text size) — due before this is trusted, not before it's committed, per this project's own standing discipline (§5).

**15 August 2026, later still — Wave 6 started: GAP-19 and GAP-98 built, GAP-18 evaluated and marked, not forced.** Following Plan.md's own wave order rather than continuing the UI pass. GAP-19 first, since GAP-98's own utilisation card reads the figure it computes: `sumVehicleEarnedForDateRange` (queries/reports.ts) is `sumVehicleEarnedForPeriod`'s exact logic — `rent`/`daily_amount`/`mileage_excess` obligations (`owed_to_us`, not voided) plus closed trips — re-keyed to the report's own `from`/`to` window via `obligation.dueOn`/`trip.closingDate` instead of `postedPeriodId`. **Checked against W-25 before writing it**: a monthly rent obligation counting on its own due date, un-prorated, whichever arbitrary window it lands in, is the identical reading `postedPeriodId` already gives it across accounting periods — not a new risk this query introduces. `availableDays = totalDays - offRoadDays`; `revenuePerAvailableDayMinor` is `null`, never a guessed `0n`, when there are none (the whole window off the road). Two new integration tests, both run against real Postgres. GAP-98: `ReportsCatalogueScreen` grows from six cards to nine — three new screens (`AgeingReportScreen`, `GoodwillReportScreen`, `UtilisationReportScreen`), three new routes, owner-only cards **filtered by role before render** rather than rendered-then-hidden by `<Can>` (which would leave a group reading one card short with no explanation to a manager). `App.test.tsx`'s own phase-2-absence pair flipped to pin the new permanent shape, the same treatment GAP-1's `MembersScreen` test got when its own guard lifted — caught one real test bug building it (`owner_manager` is inside `viewOwnerOnlyReports`'s own `OWNERS` set, same as UC-77/79's "Sees: owner, owner-manager" text, so the first draft of the "filtered for owner_manager" test had its premise backwards; fixed to test against a plain `manager`). **GAP-18 evaluated, not built — two items under one id, only one genuinely blocked.** UC-73 (the yearly view) is fully specified in `user-flows.md` F-9.2 and not blocked at all, just unsized. UC-99 (export) splits: a CSV "year of transactions" dump is ordinary, but F-9.3's PDF-statement half has no obvious home in a Workers runtime with no filesystem and bounded CPU per invocation (api/CLAUDE.md) — building one silently would be exactly the kind of architecture call this project asks be decided first, not assumed mid-build. **15 August 2026, later still — PR #50 amended: Gitar's Quick Add finding fixed.** `selectPaymentParty` (`web/src/features/quick-add/QuickAddSheet.tsx`) opened the payment sheet without clearing `receivedPartyPickerOpen`/`paidPartyPickerOpen`, so once `QuickPaymentSheet` closed (recorded or canceled) the `open={pickerOpen && !paymentSheetOpen}` gate flipped back to true and the party picker reappeared instead of the flow returning to closed. Both flags now clear in `selectPaymentParty` before the payment sheet opens.

**16 August 2026 — LT-13's own "continue the wider sweep" instruction run for the first time, live against `qa.fleetsettle.com`, using Chrome DevTools MCP with genuine CDP mobile/touch emulation rather than a manual session or a plain desktop resize.** GAP-125's fix (14/15 Aug) was reconfirmed holding — the vehicle picker never rendered blank. But continuing past that, into the tap this row's own reproduction had always stopped short of, found a second, unrelated, more severe defect one step later: selecting a vehicle never reaches `/vehicles/:id/trip/new` at all — the app silently reverts to Home, no error anywhere. Instrumenting `window.history` directly (not inferring from source) traced it to a genuine race between the router's own `pushState` for the new route and `SheetHistoryStack`'s deferred `history.back()` — the browser's back-navigation, meant to unwind the closing sheet's own pushed entry, fires after the router has already pushed the new route's entry and consumes that instead. Confirmed systemic, not Quick-Add-specific, by reproducing it a second and third way from a plain `ActionSheet` with no nested picker at all (Vehicle actions → Book trip, Vehicle actions → View calendar) — three for three. Filed as **GAP-134** rather than folded into GAP-125, since the failure mode, mechanism and fix are all unrelated; full reproduction and raw history logs in `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-16.md`. `docs/qa/live-test-plan.md`'s LT-13 row and `Plan.md`'s Wave 8 line both corrected the same sitting. **No code changed this sitting** — filing and correcting the record only. Sequencing decision, made explicitly by the owner rather than by this session's own priority read: finish the rest of Wave 7's styling pass first, then move to Wave 8, where GAP-134 now effectively heads the queue.

**16 August 2026, later — Wave 7 closes in full, four undocumented steps recorded retroactively plus the verification pass that was still genuinely outstanding.** Four sessions had built against GAP-124b/Wave 7's remaining rows without a TRACKER update landing alongside any of them — corrected here in one pass rather than left to drift further, checked against each commit's own diff rather than assumed from its message. **Step 1** closed all five of Wave 7's independent, non-GAP-124b gaps in one sitting: **GAP-27** (`--color-trip`/`--color-trip-ink`, a real semantic token replacing the borrowed `--color-serious` on arrangement C, swapped into `arrangementLabel.ts` and both `VehicleCalendarScreen` call sites), **GAP-111** (`CashPositionReportScreen`'s table view gets the same length-guarded empty-state message the chart view already had, both banked and driver-advances sections), **GAP-113** (`PartnerSetupScreen`'s ownership total now states pass/fail in words — icon + "totals 100%"/"must total 100%" — not colour alone), **GAP-17** (the photo pipeline moves off the main thread: `downscaleAndEncode` runs inside a new `photo-pipeline.worker.ts`, raced against a 3s timeout, falling back to the original unresized file rather than blocking past the deadline), plus **GAP-128**/**GAP-129** (the same pending-query false-empty shape GAP-125 already fixed once, applied to `PeopleListScreen`'s driver/customer counts and `AddOpeningBalanceEntrySheet`'s four pickers via a new `EntityPicker` `pending` prop). **GAP-44** closed separately the same window: `VehicleDoubleBookedError` now carries a structured `VehicleDoubleBookedDetails` payload (conflicting dates, resolved holder name/destination, next-fit date), built in `domain/trip.ts`'s `buildDoubleBookedError`; auditing `lease.ts`/`dailyLease.ts` as the row's own text asked found neither ever throws this error (both are gated by arrangement/overlap checks instead), so the reachable sources are `bookTrip` alone, narrower than the row assumed but the Dialog itself unchanged in scope. **Steps 3 and 4** worked GAP-124b's own §7.11 route families row by row against the table rather than restyling uniformly: **Vehicles + Trips/incidents** (`VehicleOverviewScreen` gets a `Truck` icon, `LeaseHubScreen` gets status `Badge`s and a `StatTile` for the monthly amount with Dues reordered before Billing periods per §7.11's own detail-screen ordering rule, `CloseLeaseScreen` gets a `StatTile` for Total unpaid and `Badge`-driven incident status; `VehicleCalendarScreen`/`StartLeaseScreen`/`StartDailyLeaseScreen`/`BookTripScreen` checked and found already compliant, no change forced) and **People + Cash/admin** (`CustomerDetailScreen` gets due-age chips via a new shared `ageingBucket.ts` mirroring the ageing report's own bucketing exactly, `CloseMonthScreen`'s checklist rows get warning structure — icon, accent border, `Badge` — instead of a flat list; the other eight named routes in this pair of rows checked and found already compliant). **Step 5** closed B16 Phase 3's own remaining half, money-direction consistency: `TwoBalances`' brand-in/payable-out border-and-colour pattern applied to `VehiclePerformanceCard`, `ReviewMoneyScreen`, `ReviewVehicleDetailScreen` (converted from plain `Card` to `StatTile`) and `VehicleMonthReportScreen`; `ReceivablesReportScreen`, `MineScreen` and the three single-metric chart reports checked and correctly left alone (no earned/spent split to mark, or an already-documented UC-74 table-only decision). All five steps: full gate clean at the time, no regressions found. **Overlay/form inventory checked this session, before the verification pass — no fixes needed.** All 30 sheets in `web/src/features/**/*Sheet.tsx` audited against §7.11's own five criteria (plain title, category icon only where it aids recognition, one primary action, no nested cards, field-level errors and a visible success/error path): zero `<Card` usages inside any sheet; the only three multi-`Button` files are step-gated or a separate `Dialog` footer, never two competing CTAs; every sheet's error/success path already matches the identical `mutation.isError` → inline message → `onSuccess`-closes-the-sheet convention used everywhere else in the client, screens included, so there is no sheet-specific inconsistency to fix. **The three report screens the first slice had listed as untouched turned out already compliant too, built later the same day (15 Aug) as GAP-98/Wave 6, after `StatTile` existed**: `GoodwillReportScreen` and `UtilisationReportScreen` both ship hero `StatTile`s and `NotAvailable` from their first commit; `AgeingReportScreen` is `ReceivablesReportScreen`'s own documented UC-74 table-only sibling, not a gap. **The verification pass itself — never run before this session, confirmed by every prior account's own "not started" note — found one real defect, live, via chrome-devtools MCP against `qa.fleetsettle.com` and a local instrumented reproduction**: at 200% text size (root font-size doubled, the CSS media-query-proof way real text-only zoom behaves, since a `min-width` breakpoint never changes under it), Home's critical-alert `AlertStrip` and three-up summary row overflowed the viewport by roughly 2×, invisibly — `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` stayed pinned to the viewport width throughout, so this was silent clipping, not a scrollbar, the specific failure mode 200%-text checks exist to catch. Root cause, found by walking the DOM chain from the overflowing element up rather than guessing: `HomeScreen`'s outer two-pane `grid` had no base `grid-template-columns` (only an `lg:` one), so its single implicit column sized to content instead of clamping to the viewport — confirmed the *only* screen in the client using this exact pattern (`grid -rn 'lg:grid-cols-\['` across `web/src`), so the blast radius is one file, not systemic. **Fixed**: `grid-cols-1` added as the explicit base track; `HomeSummaryItem`'s grid parent changed from a rigid `grid-cols-3` to `flex flex-wrap` with each item `flex-1 min-w-28`; `AlertStrip` changed from a non-wrapping `flex` row to `flex flex-wrap` with `min-w-0` on its text paragraph. Verified three ways before trusting it: the exact live-reproduced scenario re-run locally against the fix (0 overflowing elements beyond the pre-existing, intentional `truncate` ellipsis cases), the `lg` two-pane layout re-screenshotted to confirm no regression, and normal (100%) text size re-screenshotted pixel-identical to before. **A second, unrelated defect found by the same pass, pre-existing since 9 Aug and unrelated to any of this session's own diff**: `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`'s F-1.7 test asserted a `"Rs 0"` button name — GAP-85 (9 Aug) had already changed `MoneyField`'s empty state to `"Enter {label}"` and fixed the ~29 other call sites that asserted the old string, but this one e2e spec was outside `npm run check`'s own gate and was never touched; fixed to assert `"Enter daily lease amount"`, confirmed failing before the fix and green after. Full gate clean (`npm run check`: guard/lint/lint:css/format/typecheck, web 121 files/636 tests, shared 12/91) and the full `e2e` suite (10/10) both re-run after every fix in this paragraph. **GAP-124 closes as filed; Wave 7 is done.** Wave 8 is next, headed by GAP-134 rather than by its own previously-filed order, per the owner's explicit sequencing decision above.

**16 August 2026, later still — Wave 8 opens with GAP-134 fixed, not by patching the race but by removing the mechanism that could race at all.** Researched before building, per the owner's own instruction: the `SheetHistoryStack`/`history.pushState`+`.back()` scheme this row's mechanism section named is not a defect in this codebase's version of a legitimate pattern — it is the exact motivating problem the WICG `CloseWatcher` explainer names for its own replacement API, verbatim: *"a shared component that attempts to use the history API to implement these techniques can easily corrupt a web application's router,"* because *"both moving forward and backward through history emit the same `popstate` event."* `CloseWatcher` (shipped Chrome/Edge 126+, Firefox 149+, Chrome Android 151+, Samsung 28+; 72.3% global) answers a close request — Android back button/gesture, Esc — without ever touching `window.history`, so there is nothing left for a `navigate()` call to race, structurally, not by timing. The one real trade-off, decided with the owner before writing code: iOS Safari has no stable support (Baseline-blocked, Technology Preview only) and gets no history-based fallback — §3.3's rule is about the hardware back button, which iOS has none of, so iOS keeps drag-to-dismiss and the visible close button (M-23) exactly as before, and the gap closes on its own as Safari ships. **Built**: `useMobileHistoryDismiss.ts` renamed `useCloseWatcherDismiss.ts` and `SheetHistoryStack` deleted outright — no stack, no microtask flush, no `pendingSelfBacks`, no test-only reset hatch, 123 lines down to under 50; each `Sheet` now owns one `CloseWatcher`, constructed while open on a coarse-pointer device and destroyed on close, with zero cross-sheet bookkeeping (the platform's own "only the most-recently-constructed watcher gets events" rule is what used to need the whole stack to get right for nested sheets). A new ambient `close-watcher.d.ts` fills the gap in this repo's pinned TS 5.9 `lib.dom`, which does not ship `CloseWatcher`'s types yet. Both call sites (`Sheet.tsx`, `ReceiptSheet.tsx`'s lightbox) swapped. One corollary gap closed alongside it: `QuickAddSheet` lives in `RootLayout`, outside every route, so a real navigation (an iOS edge-swipe, which is a genuine history traversal with no back button/gesture for `CloseWatcher` to answer) could otherwise leave it open over the wrong screen — `RootLayout` now closes it on every `pathname` change. **Docs moved with it** (UI §3.3, §6.1's `Sheet` row, M-27's Why column — mechanism only, M-27's conclusion that F-2.1 stays a route is unchanged; `ui-ux-guidelines.md` → v1.4.1). **Tests**: `useMobileHistoryDismiss.test.tsx`'s manager-level suite replaced by `useCloseWatcherDismiss.test.tsx` (6 tests, against a `MockCloseWatcher` jsdom stands in for — construct/destroy lifecycle, `onclose` → `onOpenChange(false)`, the coarse-pointer gate, and a no-throw feature-detection fallback for browsers without it); `Sheet.test.tsx`/`QuickAddSheet.test.tsx` lost their `__resetSheetHistoryStackForTests()` calls, since there is no more module-level singleton to reset. **The regression coverage gap this row's own tracker text named — no e2e case for a sheet closing and *navigating* rather than opening another sheet — is closed**: a new case in `e2e/mobile-sheet-history.touch.spec.ts` drives Quick Add → New trip → pick a vehicle under real CDP touch emulation, asserting the app both reaches and stays on `/vehicles/:id/trip/new`. Confirmed with real teeth, not just written and trusted: temporarily reinstated the pre-fix `SheetHistoryStack` behind the same `useCloseWatcherDismiss` export name and re-ran this exact spec — failed reliably (`toHaveURL` timed out on `/vehicles`, the live symptom exactly), then reverted to the fix and confirmed green. Full gate clean (`npm run check`: 121 web files/635 tests, 12 shared files/91 tests) and the full touch-emulated e2e project (3/3) both green.

**16 August 2026, still later — PR #60's own automated review answered, and it found a real (if latent) gap one layer under the one it named.** Gitar-bot flagged `web/src/lib/ageingBucket.ts`'s `computeAgeingBucket` as bucketing off `dueOn` while the server's `listAgeingBuckets` buckets off `effectiveDueOn`, contradicting the file's own doc comment that the two "never disagree." Verified independently before acting: every obligation-insert path (`billing-period.ts`, `trip.ts`, `management-fee.ts`, `post-closure-charge.ts`, `opening-balance.ts`, `confirmDay.ts`) sets `effectiveDueOn` equal to `dueOn` at creation, and obligations are append-only, so the divergence the bot describes cannot happen yet for the one party type this screen shows (customer). **Fixed the cheap, correct-by-construction way rather than softening the doc comment**: `effectiveDueOn` piped onto the wire (`leaseObligationRowSchema`, `LeaseObligationRow`/`OutstandingObligation` on the API side, both `findObligationsForLease` and `findOutstandingObligationsForParty`/`findOutstandingObligationsForDriver`'s shared select, both handler mappings), `computeAgeingBucket` and `CustomerDetailScreen` switched to read it. New coverage: `ageingBucket.test.ts` (didn't exist before — bucket boundaries at 30/60/90 days, matching `listAgeingBuckets` exactly) and a new `CustomerDetailScreen.test.tsx` case that deliberately diverges `dueOn`/`effectiveDueOn` and asserts the chip follows the latter — confirmed failing red against the pre-fix call site (`due.dueOn`) before confirming green against the fix, the same discipline this project already holds e2e regressions to. **The more consequential finding was underneath the one reported**: the fields' current equality is coincidence of every write path, not a rule that holds by construction — DM D-5 already licensed a real divergence for `driver.settlement_rhythm = 'weekly'` and flagged its own maintenance as "worth confirming." Confirmed negative: `confirmDay.ts` never reads `driver.settlementRhythm`, and nothing in the codebase writes or reads that column beyond its schema default. Filed as **GAP-135**, D-5 resolved to say so (`data-model.md` v1.1.8). `npm run check` clean; relevant API integration suites (`customer.test.ts`, `lease.test.ts`, `void-cascade.test.ts`, `driver-money.test.ts`, `reports.test.ts`) re-run. **Four Wave 8 · Verification debt items closed the same sitting, by owner instruction rather than left for their own — GAP-114, GAP-115, GAP-86, GAP-130** (each row's own closing note has the full account): GAP-114's `id`-vs-`userId` test gap closed with a real DB-backed assertion; GAP-115's five remaining thin-coverage screens checked individually — two (`DriverDetailScreen`, `MineScreen`) found already adequately covered by later feature work and left alone, three (`CustomerDetailScreen`, `PartnerDetailScreen`, `PartnerSetupScreen`) had genuine gaps and got one new test each; GAP-86's two test-suite warnings (`scrollTo`, `useRouter`) both silenced at their real cause, confirmed gone via a full-suite grep; GAP-130's two "renders nothing while pending" spots (`ExpenseCostRow`, `CorrectPaymentSheet`) each gained the missing branch plus a never-resolving-fetch test. Full web suite: 122 files / 645 tests, all green — 9 new since Wave 7's close (636): 3 in the new `ageingBucket.test.ts`, 2 on `CustomerDetailScreen` (the divergence case plus a dues-read failure case), 1 each on `PartnerDetailScreen`, `PartnerSetupScreen`, `ExpenseCostRow` and `CorrectPaymentSheet` — see each closed row above for which. `business-member.test.ts` (15/15) re-run directly against the real Neon test branch for GAP-114's fix, not assumed from the mock suite alone.


**17 August 2026 — six open questions put to the owner and answered, and the shape of the answers is the finding.** Five of six deferred rather than decided, all on one principle: production holds zero rows, so a judgement about how this business behaves would be a guess wearing a decision's clothes. **GAP-65** deferred out of phase 1 — the first time the 12 Aug "nothing goes live until every phase-1 gap is fixed" premise has been bent, recorded in the row itself rather than absorbed quietly. **GAP-18** split: CSV ships, the PDF half becomes **GAP-136** and defers. **P14** deferred entire with **the twelve Meta approvals deliberately not fired** — against this tracker's own standing advice to submit them because they queue and cost nothing; the owner's reasoning was better, and [Blocked](#blocked) now records why: `use-cases.md` §8 already flags W-14 as a decision to keep attacking, and **assisted sending needs no approved templates at all**, so firing twelve would have quietly settled the question §8 says to leave open. **W-4 and W-2** parked with observable conditions instead of "after real use" — the failure mode the Asgardeo row demonstrates for months at a time. **The release gate** is now all four live-test items, which means the last thing between this build and production is evidence, not code.

**GAP-135 was the one that did not defer, and it is the one worth reading.** Asked how to close it, this session recommended a `CHECK` pinning `settlement_rhythm` to `'daily'` — "make the wrong state unrepresentable, force the design when a real weekly driver exists." **Reading the owning documents before writing it showed weekly drivers already exist**: UC-31 names "or pays weekly", UC-37 calls it "the ordinary case for some drivers rather than an exception", F-4.5 carries the acceptance criterion, FL A-26 has a dedicated edge case for exactly this. The constraint would not have been a schema tidy-up; it would have been a silent product decision to drop a described phase-1 capability, taken in `data-model.md`, one level below where such a decision belongs — **the precise failure CLAUDE.md's "the owning document decides" exists to catch, and it was caught by the procedure rather than by judgement.** Recommendation withdrawn mid-plan and the real choice put back to the owner. **Built instead**: `confirmDay`/`confirmDaysBulk` refuse a non-`'daily'` driver (`SETTLEMENT_RHYTHM_UNSUPPORTED`, 409) rather than write an `effective_due_on` known to read as overdue — W-56's rule applied to a write. One read per call, not per day, because `confirmDaysBulk` carries one `driverId` for its whole batch and a per-day check would have reintroduced the N+1 GAP-132 had just removed. Both new tests confirmed failing red (201/200 rather than 409) against a disabled guard before being trusted.

**Documents travelled with all of it**, per the rule rather than as an afterthought: `use-cases.md` v1.2.13 (UC-57, UC-99, §9.1's First row, §8's two triggers), `user-flows.md` v1.1.14 (F-6.6, F-9.3, F-4.5), `data-model.md` v1.1.9 (D-5), and `docs/README.md`'s status table. **No acceptance criterion was weakened anywhere** — F-6.6's "Not optional" clause is untouched, F-9.3's and F-4.5's criteria stand as written; what changed is that the suite now states which of its promises are built, in a new table in `docs/README.md`, so the index stops implying more than the product delivers. **One pre-existing traceability hole closed on the way**: F-9.3 has cited UC-99 as its source since it was written and FL §8 carried no row for it, so traceability did not close in the use-case direction; found only because the split forced a re-read of the flow. `npm run check` clean, `day-record` integration 29/29 against real Postgres.
**16 August 2026, last — this tracker and [Plan.md](Plan.md) re-validated against the repository rather than against themselves, and the pattern in what was wrong is the finding.** No code changed. **Every stale item was an aggregate; every accurate one was narrative.** §0 below was correct throughout; §1's summary table — the part anyone reads to learn the current state — was five days behind it, and in three places the document contradicted itself across two sections. Measured, not re-read: `web` **122 files / 648 tests** (the row said 113/565, from 11 Aug, while §0 had already recorded 121/636 then 122/645); `packages/shared` **12 / 91** (said 11 / 78); `api` integration **40 files / 729 `it(` blocks**, grep-verified (said 33 / 489, from 10 Aug); lint **53 warnings** (said 49); **routes 37** (said 34 — and the three missing were GAP-98's own `/reports/ageing`, `/reports/goodwill`, `/reports/utilisation`, which [§4](#4-every-open-gap) had recorded as built the day they shipped). `api` unit re-run and genuinely unchanged at 3 / 10. **The Deployed row was the worst of it and the least fixable by measurement**: it claimed production sat on migrations `0001`–`0014`, 17 commits behind, when `main` and `develop` now carry the identical 28 migration files and the gap is 25 commits / 4 first-parent. The same wrong number was in [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md)'s always-loaded repository-state paragraph — corrected there by deleting the numbers rather than refreshing them, since a hand-copied count in the file that loads every session is read constantly and updated never. **What measurement could not settle is now the open item**: files present on `main` is not proof they are applied to production's Neon branch, and nobody has run `migrate:status` against it. **[§4](#4-every-open-gap) had stopped being able to answer its own question** — `Scheduled` held 48 rows of which 39 are closed, so "what is left?" took a row-by-row read; there is now a five-row table of what is genuinely open at the top of it, and the section says plainly that it holds both. Four rows were repaired: GAP-12 and GAP-36 still read as scheduled although §0 twice recorded 5B-1 closing as filed, and **GAP-124 and GAP-7 had no Track cell at all** — two fields where every other row has three — so neither rendered a status and neither could be counted, both being closed work. **The largest gap was in [Plan.md](Plan.md), and it was an absence rather than an error**: the ten-wave plan ended at Wave 9 · Release, so the file CLAUDE.md defines as "what remains" described nothing after phase 1 — `grep` for `P14`, `WhatsApp` or `B7` across its live section returned zero, every mention sitting in the block that file marks superseded, **including B7's full specification, which was phase 3's only one.** Both phases now have waves (10 · P14, 11 · B7), B7's spec is summarised where it will survive a compaction, and the historical block carries a marker at its head, since its own opening line — *"Every remaining item on both tracks, sequenced"* — introduced the deprecated three-quarters of the file as the live plan. Wave 5, the largest in the plan, had never been struck through and eight of its ten items carried no `done` marker, so a finished wave read as the next thing to build; GAP-135 was missing from Plan.md entirely though this tracker had carried it since filing. **`npm run guard` clean, `format:check` clean, all five new cross-document anchors verified to resolve.** **The rule this pass suggests, not yet built**: every number corrected here is mechanically computable — test counts, route count, migration count, branch divergence — and every one of them drifted because a person had to remember. That is the same shape as `assert_period_open()`'s hand-maintained array, which this project answered with a CI assertion after it drifted once. A `guard` rule asserting §1's figures against the tree would close this class; a sixth manual correction pass will not.

**16 August 2026, after that — the same validation extended to every markdown file in the repository root and `docs/`, and the specification suite came out clean.** `docs/README.md`'s version table matches all seven documents' own `Status:` lines **exactly** — use-cases v1.2.12, user-flows v1.1.13, data-model v1.1.8, tech-stack v1.3, implementation-guidelines v1.6.1, ui-ux-guidelines v1.4.1, brand-guidelines v1.0.2 — and both directories it names as supporting material exist. **That is the part of this repository's documentation that works, and it works because `doc-change` makes the version bump and the index update part of the edit rather than a thing to remember afterwards.** The defects are all in the working documents that grew up around the suite, and every one is the same shape as the ones found earlier the same day: **a file that finished its job and never said so.** `GAP-12-VOID-CASCADE-DESIGN.md` still reads *"No code written against it yet"* — GAP-12 closed 14 Aug as 5B-1, and `api/src/route-defs/` carries 16 `/void` references with `void-cascade.test.ts` beside them. `UI-LOW-API-WORK-PLAN-2026-08-11.md` planned a branch that has merged, carries no status marker, and is referenced by nothing. `AGENTS.md` is referenced by nothing either, and restates guidance CLAUDE.md owns — the "two copies of a rule become two different rules" problem, applied to tool entrypoints. **`docs/testing/` — 35 files, ~4,900 lines — is unmapped by `docs/README.md` entirely**, though the tree's own README is honest that it is retired (GAP-58) and points at docs/qa/live-test-plan.md, which is what keeps this a navigation gap rather than a trap. Root `README.md` is three lines. All five are filed as **Wave 8b · Document dispositions** in [Plan.md](Plan.md) rather than fixed in passing; the `docs/README.md` line is a specification edit and needs `doc-change`, a version bump and a Status-table update, so it is scheduled, not slipped in. **One real correction landed here, and it is the sharpest illustration of the class**: [Plan.md](Plan.md)'s Wave 8 listed **LT-11 as open**, four days after [docs/qa/live-test-plan.md](docs/qa/live-test-plan.md) — which owns LT status — and this tracker's own `Alongside this tracker` bullet had both recorded it closed on 12 Aug. Worse in the other direction, **LT-13 was missing from the plan altogether**: the phase-1 mobile interaction sweep, the item that produced GAP-125 and then GAP-134, still open with its own row reading "the rest of the sweep remains open." **The plan was carrying a finished item and omitting the one with the highest yield in the wave.** Both corrected; LT-13 now heads Wave 8's live-test queue. **Two things confirmed rather than corrected, so they are not re-derived**: `testCred.md` is untracked *and* matched by `.gitignore:56`, so no inattentive `git add .` can commit it — correct as it stands; and the `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-{11,14,16}.md` series is dated by filename and cited from here, needing no disposition, because a finding record is supposed to stop being current.

**17 August 2026, later still — GAP-18 built in full, and Wave 8b's five document dispositions closed the same sitting.** With GAP-65, GAP-135 and GAP-136 already moved to phase 2, GAP-18 (UC-73's yearly view, UC-99's CSV half) was the only buildable phase-1 gap left in the plan — Plan.md's own 17 Aug narrowing had already split the CSV half out as startable, "two ordinary jobs with nothing outstanding."

**UC-73, the yearly view — `GET /api/reports/vehicle-year`.** `sumVehicleCostsForDateRange` and `sumOverheadsForDateRange` (`queries/reports.ts`) mirror `sumVehicleEarnedForDateRange`'s own date-window reasoning (GAP-19) for the cost and overhead halves — `expense.spentOn`/`obligation.dueOn` in place of `postedPeriodId`, same kind/direction/borne-by filters. `getVehicleYearReport` (`domain/reports.ts`) returns one report, not `vehicle-month` plus a second client-composed `overheads` call the way the Review shell's month screen does it — "the same basis as UC-70, with overheads stated beneath vehicle profit" is one response, `{ from, to, vehicles: [...], overheadsMinor }`, since a year has no single accounting period for a `periodId`-keyed overheads query to key on. `viewOwnerOnlyReports`-gated (UC-73's own "Sees: owner, owner-manager" — narrower than `vehicle-month`'s manager-inclusive audience, so unlike that endpoint this never takes a manager's restricted vehicle set at all). `VehicleYearReportScreen.tsx` reuses `VehicleMonthReportScreen`'s own `toKpiTotals`/`toChartData` (structurally identical row shape) rather than re-deriving them, with the overheads figure as its own `StatTile` beneath the per-vehicle KPI row.

**UC-99's CSV half — `GET /api/reports/export`.** `listTransactionsForDateRange` (`queries/reports.ts`) is three bulk queries — earned obligations, cost obligations, closed-trip income, below-the-line expenses — never one per row (api/CLAUDE.md's bounded-CPU rule), returning row-level facts rather than the sums the other reports compute. `getTransactionsCsv` (`domain/reports.ts`) sorts oldest-first and renders plain decimal Rs (never `format()`'s comma-grouped display string — this is data for a spreadsheet import, not a screen a person reads) with RFC 4180 field escaping and `\r\n` line endings. The route follows `getAttachmentRoute`'s own precedent for a non-JSON 200 (`description` only, no `content` schema) and the handler returns raw `text/csv` via `c.body()` with a `Content-Disposition: attachment` header, the same shape `getAttachmentHandler` already uses for a receipt download. `ExportTransactionsScreen.tsx` calls `api.getBlob` (already built for A7's receipt downloads, so the bearer token travels the same way a plain `<a href>` to the API never could) and triggers a normal browser save via a temporary anchor + `URL.createObjectURL`, revoked immediately after — a spreadsheet import is exactly what leaving the app is right for, so this is a browser-native save, not a second in-app viewer. One primary action (`Screen`'s own sticky CTA, `CloseMonthScreen`'s precedent for a single terminal action).

**Both wired into the catalogue and the router**: `ReportsCatalogueScreen` grows from nine cards to eleven (`vehicle-year` in "Trips and vehicles", `export` in "Money questions", both `ownerOnly`), two new routes (`/reports/vehicle-year`, `/reports/export`) added to `router.tsx` following `GoodwillReportRoute`'s own current-calendar-year default. **7 new API integration tests** (`reports.test.ts`, both endpoints' happy path plus 403/400/404 boundaries, plus both paths added to the shared `ownerOnlyGated` 401/403 sweep) — full file re-run clean, 45/45 against real Postgres, golden fixtures unmoved. **4 new web tests** across two new files (`VehicleYearReportScreen.test.tsx`, `ExportTransactionsScreen.test.tsx`), full web suite 124 files / 652 tests green, shared unchanged at 12/91. `npm run check` clean throughout (guard, lint 0 errors, lint:css, format:check, typecheck all three workspaces).

**Wave 8b closed the same sitting, all five dispositions exactly as filed 16 Aug**: `GAP-12-VOID-CASCADE-DESIGN.md`'s header now reads built and closed, pointing at this tracker's own GAP-12 row rather than claiming unbuilt work; `UI-LOW-API-WORK-PLAN-2026-08-11.md` marked superseded rather than deleted (recoverable from git either way, but a plan a reader can still check against its own "Implementation status" section is worth more than a commit hash); `AGENTS.md` rewritten from a 68-line restatement of CLAUDE.md into an 18-line pointer at it, `.claude/rules/docs.md`'s "two copies of a rule become two different rules" applied to a tool entrypoint rather than only a specification document; `docs/README.md`'s supporting-material paragraph now maps `docs/testing/` (35 files, ~4,900 lines) as retired reference material pointing at `docs/qa/live-test-plan.md`, via the `doc-change` skill; root `README.md` grown from three bare lines to a real pointer at `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/README.md`.

**What this leaves in the plan: nothing to build.** Every gap in phase 1 is now closed or deliberately deferred to phase 2 (GAP-65, GAP-135, GAP-136). What remains before `develop` → `main` is evidence, not code — the four Wave 8 live-test items (LT-9, LT-10, LT-12, LT-13) — and then Wave 9 · Release itself.

**17 August 2026, later still — LT-13's own sweep finished and LT-12 done, both live against `qa.fleetsettle.com`, both via Chrome DevTools MCP with real CDP mobile/touch emulation. Two of the four live-test items gating release are now closed.**

**LT-13**, source-first rather than click-first, because GAP-134's fix changed what "the rest of the sweep" needs to mean — it didn't patch the race, it deleted `SheetHistoryStack` and every `window.history` call it made, so every `Sheet`/`ActionSheet` in the client now goes through one shared primitive (`useCloseWatcherDismiss`) that never touches history at all. Every screen in the sweep's own bullet list (Quick Add, entity-creation sheets, vehicle/detail actions, people/money actions, cash/partner/admin, trips/incidents/costs) checked against source for a sheet-closes-and-navigates handoff — the actual shape GAP-134 fixes. **Six such call sites exist in the whole client, not thirty-one**: everything else either stays on the same screen after closing (entity creation, money actions, cash/admin, trip close/cancel) or never involved a sheet at all (the calendar's free-day tap-through is a plain grid-cell click). **Five of the six directly confirmed live**: the four from 16 Aug (New trip, View calendar, Book trip ×2) plus `ReportIncidentSheet` → `/incidents/:id`, new this session — a real, clearly-labelled test incident created on `QA-52656` and confirmed reaching the incident detail screen with zero console errors, left in place rather than voided since `incident` carries no void mechanism in this build (not one of GAP-12's voidable tables) and the row is zero-value. *New trip* also re-confirmed at **360×640** specifically (the row's other named viewport), clean. **One item — `LeaseHubScreen`'s "Close the lease" — stays open**: no monthly-lease QA vehicle was available to click into this session, and closing a lease is one-way (CLAUDE.md: "there is no reopen"), so this was never going to be forced to completion regardless. Full account and the per-category reasoning in [docs/qa/live-test-plan.md](docs/qa/live-test-plan.md)'s own LT-13 row.

**LT-12**: at 360×640, `Confirm and go live` reachable and tappable (GAP-110's fix holds). Pressed on the real QA batch — `PUT /api/opening-balance` then `POST /api/opening-balance/commit`, both 200 — and `QA2 Driver 0808155856`'s own detail page confirmed `Held deposit — Rs 5,000` immediately after, matching `Where is our cash`'s `Rs 6,000 held as deposits` total. **Pressed a second time in the same session**, specifically to witness step 5's idempotency claim rather than infer it: same 200/200, and the driver's held deposit stayed exactly Rs 5,000 — not doubled. Zero console errors across both presses. **One honest caveat, recorded rather than smoothed over**: the batch's Rs 2,000 customer half had already self-healed before this session — `QA2 Customer 0808155856`'s own page shows a real Rs 5,000 payment received against them on 12 Aug, and their current balance is ordinary `trip_fare` activity since, not an opening-balance obligation — so the very first materialisation moment for this specific batch was inferred from evidence rather than watched live. What *was* watched live is the retry-idempotency guarantee GAP-109 exists to give, under a real repeated press, not only in the integration suite. Full account in [docs/qa/live-test-plan.md](docs/qa/live-test-plan.md)'s own LT-12 row.

**What remains: LT-9 and LT-10, then Wave 9 · Release.** LT-9 needs a linked-driver account created first (redemption requires a real identity this session cannot supply). LT-10 needs a real iOS Safari / Android Chrome device — CDP emulation, however thorough, is not the standard that row exists to hold, precisely because `CloseWatcher` has no iOS fallback by design.

**17 August 2026, last — a structural cleanup pass over this file and [Plan.md](Plan.md). No code changed, and no gap row's content was edited.** Narrower than the 16 Aug validation pass that preceded it, and deliberately so: that pass corrected *figures*, this one corrects *where things are filed* and *what a heading claims*. **The finding is that the previous pass's own workarounds had become the problem.** Where it found a section that could not answer its own question, it added a summary table above and a note below explaining that the heading was wrong — both correct, neither addressing the heading. Three consequences, all fixed here:

- **[§4](#4-every-open-gap)'s `Scheduled` subsection is renamed [Full accounts — open and closed](#full-accounts--open-and-closed).** Only **7 of its 49 rows are not closed**; a reader taking the old heading at face value got the wrong answer about the other 42. The rows are still **not** relocated to [Closed](#closed) — that decision stands and its reasoning is unchanged (`Closed` compacts to one line per gap; these carry accounts worth keeping in full) — but the heading now says what it holds, which is what the summary table and the explanatory note were each substituting for. Both stay: the table is still the fastest answer to "what is left". Two `#scheduled` anchors elsewhere in this file were repointed, with the historical prose left accurate to its own date.
- **The count in that section's own note was stale in the direction it warns about**: it read `48 rows, of which 35 carry a closed Track`; measured, it is **49 and 42**. Now derived by counting rather than by hand.
- **§1's `npm run check` row was the stalest figure in the document** — it carried `web 114 files/575 tests, shared 12/85` from 13 August while the `web` and `packages/shared` rows immediately beneath it had both been re-measured since and disagreed with it. **It no longer carries test counts at all**: three rows quoting the same two numbers is three places to update and two that get forgotten, which is precisely what happened. The per-workspace rows are now the single source. Re-run directly for this pass: web **124/652**, shared **12/91**, `api` unit **3/10**, lint **0 errors / 55 warnings** — all four matching what the table already claimed.
- **`api` integration re-counted at 40 files / 739 `it(` blocks**, one above the `738` recorded hours earlier the same day: `06c5943`'s CSV-formula-injection test landed in between.
