Left visible as its own correction rather than overwritten, because it is the smallest possible instance of this document's recurring defect and the sharpest argument for the `guard` rule the 16 Aug entry proposes — **a number a person maintains drifts even while that person is actively looking at it.**

**In [Plan.md](Plan.md), the same shape, and one instance of it was actively misleading rather than merely stale.** Its `Skipped by decision` section reads as standing policy — *"out of scope on both tracks"*, *"also out"* — but is dated 4–11 August, and **six of its seven entries were subsequently built** (A7, GAP-1, GAP-6, GAP-12, GAP-19, GAP-18). It sits inside the block that file marks superseded, but that marker says the block is *historical*, and a list of things that were reversed is neither historical nor live. Each entry now carries what actually happened, with the original reasoning kept beside it per this repository's *record what you did not take* convention. **The deprecation marker itself was under-scoped**: it named one exception (B7's phase-3 spec) and there are four — the other three being two genuinely live conventions buried under "do not read it" (`The bar every item clears`, restated there expressly "so no session goes looking", and `When an item here is finished`, the write-up convention pointing at this file), plus `Skipped by decision` itself. **And Plan.md contradicted itself on P14**: its Wave 10 records the owner's 17 Aug decision that the twelve Meta approvals were *deliberately not fired*, while its tail still read *"worth firing now"* — struck through, with the reasoning that overruled it carried across.

**Verification: `npm run guard` clean, `format:check` clean, every anchor introduced or repointed checked against the heading it targets** (including the double-hyphen slug the em-dash headings generate, confirmed against an existing working anchor rather than assumed). **Deliberately not done: compacting [§0](#0-build-log).** It has the precedent (31 entries compacted 9 Aug) and it is long enough to qualify again, but every closed-row account in §4 and every trap in §5 already points into it by date, and the 16 Aug pass found this log *correct throughout* while every summary above it had drifted. Compacting the one part of this document that has never been wrong, to shorten it, is a trade this pass declined to make.

**18–19 August 2026 — the platform-admin/multi-business initiative: three phases, six PRs, all merged into `develop`, none of it recorded in this file until now.** Found and fixed only because it was asked about directly, the same "a file that finished its job and never said so" shape the 16 Aug validation pass already named for other documents — this one had simply never been opened. **Say plainly what this is not**: not phase 1/2/3 of §1's own phase model (that phase 1 closed 17 Aug with nothing left to build; WhatsApp/offline stay phase 2/3 there). This is a third, unrelated thing that happens to use the same word for its own three-part rollout — a platform-operator tier (a person who administers the whole install, not any one business) and the ability for one identity to belong to more than one business. Two design docs preceded any code, PR #68/#69/#71 (three validation passes, decisions 1–29 settled) — `PLATFORM-ADMIN-AND-MULTI-BUSINESS-DESIGN-2026-08-17.md` and its companion implementation plan, both still current and cited below rather than re-derived.

- **Phase 1 — PR #72, `develop`.** `api/migrations/0029_multi_business_membership.sql` drops `one_active_business_per_user` and makes `driver.linked_user_id` business-scoped rather than a global `UNIQUE`. `resolveMembership` → `resolveMemberships` (`identity.ts`): a real `UNION ALL` of the `business_member` and linked-driver shapes, replacing an `or()`-joined cross-product that only ever worked because the dropped index capped each side at one row. `authMiddleware` gains the five-step `X-Business-Id` header rule (IG §7.5) — a filter over memberships the server derives from the token, never a grant, never itself the source of `businessId`. `BusinessAlreadyExistsError` → `AlreadyAMemberError` (`BUSINESS_ALREADY_MEMBER`); new `BusinessNotSelectedError` (400). 745/745 integration, 753/753 unit; G-1 independently re-verified at 134,000 after a 90s-budget miss on that ephemeral branch's own compute, confirmed environmental.
- **Phase 2 backend — PR #73, stacked on the phase1 branch per decision 18 (that branch cannot function without Phase 1's schema), landed on `develop` via bridge PR #74** once it became clear #72 had merged to `develop` before #73 merged into `phase1` — stranding Phase 2 on a branch nothing else built from. `api/migrations/0030_platform_tier.sql`: `platform_admin` (PK on `user_id`, matching `business_settings`'s own precedent), `business_creation_request` (a held request, never a pending business row — decision 2), `platform_audit_log` (separate from the tenant-scoped `audit_log`), `app_user.business_allowance` (decision 22, default 5). Two new triggers mirror `business_member`'s own shape one level up: `assert_platform_has_admin()` (INV-40) and `platform_admin_audit`. `platformAdminMiddleware` mounts `/api/admin/*` — never `authMiddleware`, no `businessId` ever in scope — and a new `check-forbidden.mjs` rule bars `queries/platform/` from importing money-table schema, as an **allowlist**, deliberately, so a future business-internal table that isn't strictly "money" still can't leak through by omission. `POST /api/business` now runs `requestOrCreateBusiness`: under the requester's allowance, creates immediately; at or over it, holds a `business_creation_request` instead (check-then-act race accepted, not fixed — decision 20, recorded in the domain function's own comment). `GET /api/session` (decision 17) answers before any business is selected — every membership, platform-admin status, the caller's own pending/rejected request — and upserts `app_user` on first call, solving the platform-admin bootstrap ordering problem. `/api/admin/*` covers the full F-11 surface (requests, businesses, users — name/created/count only, never a money field, INV-38 — grant/revoke admin, set-allowance, the platform's own audit log). **Two bugs caught before merge**: a first draft minted session ids via `crypto.randomUUID()` where DM §2 requires UUIDv7 (fixed to `newId()`); `DEPLOYMENT.md`'s bootstrap checklist needed a `set_config` call before its own `INSERT`, found running the migration for real. A follow-up commit then answered SonarCloud's quality gate: migration `0030`'s `'pending'` literal, repeated three times (DEFAULT, `CHECK`, partial index), narrowed to two by dropping the DEFAULT; `BusinessCreationRequestStatus` extracted as a single type alias in place of three inline unions; `checkPlatformQueryImports` refactored under the cognitive-complexity threshold; a "use VARCHAR2" suggestion declined outright — VARCHAR2 is an Oracle type, not valid Postgres, and `char(3)` matches migration `0001`'s own existing convention for the same column. 761/762 integration (the one failure is G-1's own 90s budget again, independently re-verified correct the same way as #72), 754/754 unit.
- **Bridge PR #74.** No new code — the same tree #73 already reviewed, now reaching `develop`. Folds in the second of "two post-merge review-finding fixes" made directly on the `phase1` branch after #73 merged there: `revokePlatformAdmin` was silently returning 200 for a non-admin — its `UPDATE … WHERE revoked_at IS NULL` guard matches zero rows for someone never granted or already revoked, and the query returned void with no row-count check, so the endpoint reported success with no audit entry written (the trigger is `AFTER UPDATE`, never fires on a zero-row update). Fixed via `.returning()`, throwing `NotFoundError` when nothing was actually revoked, matching the route's own documented 404. 36/36 across `admin.test.ts`, `session.test.ts`, `auth.test.ts`, verified against a live branch with `0029`/`0030` actually applied.
- **Phase 2 client — PR #75.** `FirstRunGate` rebuilt onto `GET /api/session`, replacing the old `/api/me`-404-sniffing first-run detection, which conflated a brand-new identity with a revoked one. `CreateBusinessForm` gains its third and fourth branches — pending, and rejected-with-reason-and-a-way-to-retry. Six new screens under `web/src/features/admin/` (request queue, businesses, users, admin management, the platform audit log, an allowance `Sheet`), reusing `Screen`/`Card`/`Sheet` throughout — the only primitive changes are `AppShell`'s `shell` union gaining `"admin"` and `DialogConfirmFooter` gaining an optional `confirmDisabled`. Judgment calls recorded in the PR itself: the admin-grant picker reuses the Users list rather than a raw user-id field; every admin action is a `Sheet`, never a `Dialog` — M-32's own rule (reject/revoke/grant/allowance are all reversible), `Dialog`'s three reserved call sites (INV-1/INV-17/M-10) untouched; `FirstRunGate` stays prop-driven (`pathname`/`renderAdmin`) rather than reading the router itself, matching every other non-route screen in `router.tsx`. **Two gitar-bot findings fixed before merge**: the approve confirm button could double-submit (a second tap while `decideMutation` was in flight surfaced a spurious `REQUEST_ALREADY_DECIDED` 409 after the first call had already succeeded) — now disables in flight, matching reject/grant/revoke's own footers; the allowance form's blank-input handling was actually wrong, not just unpolished — `Number("")` is `0`, a valid integer, so clearing the field and saving silently submitted a real zero allowance, while `Number("abc")` failed the guard with no feedback at all — `validateAllowance()` now mirrors the wire schema's own 0–1000 bound and surfaces a real inline `Field` error. 806 tests total across `api`/`web`/`shared`, 130 web test files including the six new admin suites.
- **Phase 3 — PR #76, the business switcher.** `createApiClient` takes a second, parallel `BusinessIdGetter` — read fresh on every call, the identical shape `TokenGetter` already uses — threaded into both `request()` and `requestBlob()` (the latter builds its own header object independently and is easy to miss), sent as `X-Business-Id` only when non-null. New `web/src/lib/storage.ts` — the first use of `localStorage` anywhere in `web/src`, confirmed by grep before writing it. `AuthActionsContext`'s `signOut` clears the stored selection alongside its existing `queryClient.clear()`; deliberately not `AuthGate.tsx`, which fires on every unauthenticated render including an ordinary token refresh and would wipe a live selection. New `BusinessSwitcherSheet` (modelled on `MoreScreen`'s own sign-out confirm sheet): a valid stored selection routes straight into that membership's shell with no prompt; unset, or naming a business no longer in the list, opens the switcher as the primary content with an inert `onOpenChange` — there is no valid fallback state a dismiss could reveal. **The "hard remount" finding, verified empirically, not assumed**: a mounted `useQuery(["session"])` observer does not recover on its own after `queryClient.clear()` — neither `invalidateQueries`, `refetchQueries` nor `resetQueries`, called immediately after, un-sticks it; only an actual React unmount/remount does. `FirstRunGate` is now a thin wrapper holding a `remountGeneration` counter around `FirstRunGateInner`, keyed by it. **Three more `businesses[0]`-reading sites, not flagged as a stopgap but the identical bug**: `useMe.ts`, `Can.tsx`, `ReportsCatalogueScreen.tsx` — left alone, switching business would have been cosmetic, the right shell rendering while every capability check and `useMe()` call inside it kept resolving from whichever business sorted first. New `web/src/lib/selectedMembership.ts` (`resolveSelectedMembership`) centralises the fix across all three plus `FirstRunGate`. **`smoke.spec.ts`, `sheet-a11y.spec.ts` and `mobile-sheet-history.touch.spec.ts` found already broken on this branch before any Phase 3 change** — all three mock `/api/me` only, and `FirstRunGate` had already moved onto `/api/session` in Phase 2; all 11 tests across the three files confirmed failing/timing out on `ECONNREFUSED` before the fix, passing after matching `/api/session` mocks were added.
- **GAP-137 — the one real defect this initiative shipped and then found, closed the same session, never reached `main`.** `main.tsx`'s 403 handler — the A11 demoted-user mechanism, "a role change never invalidates a token, so the client's own stale copy of what it can do has to be told to refetch" — still invalidated the `["me"]` query key. PR #75 moved `useMe()`/`<Can>`/`ReportsCatalogueScreen` onto `["session"]` but missed this call site, so the invalidation had been a silent no-op on `develop` from the moment #75 merged (02:50 UTC) until this fix landed inside #76, before #76 itself merged (05:24 UTC) — roughly two and a half hours where a demoted user's stale role/capabilities would never refetch on a 403, contrary to what the comment directly above the call claimed. Found rereading this file to add Phase 3's own `BUSINESS_NOT_SELECTED` branch beside it, not by anyone looking for it. Same shape as GAP-120/121/122 — a real gap in already-shipped code, found and closed in the course of unrelated next work — so it gets a gap id rather than living only in a commit message. No existing test covered the handler; none needed updating.
- **A second gitar-bot finding, fixed before #76 merged**: `storage.ts`'s three functions called `localStorage` directly with no guard. `localStorage` throws `SecurityError` in a storage-blocked environment (historically Safari private mode; any "block all site data" setting) — and `getSelectedBusinessId` runs synchronously inside `request()`/`requestBlob()` on *every* API call via the new `BusinessIdGetter`, so an unguarded throw there would have failed every request in such an environment, not only the switcher. All three now fail soft — the selection is best-effort by design (one membership sends no header either way; more than one just asks again), so degrading to "no stored selection" is always safe where an app-wide outage never is. Two new regression tests force the `SecurityError` via `Storage.prototype` spies; 6/6 in `storage.test.ts`.
- **Verification, PR #76's own final state**: `npm run check` clean across all three workspaces; web **133 files / 729 tests**, including 5 new `BusinessSwitcherSheet` tests (one proves a switch clears `PartnerDetailScreen`'s four `userId`-keyed queries), 8 new/updated `FirstRunGate` tests, 4 `storage.ts`, 4 `selectedMembership.ts`, 6 `api.ts` header-injection, 1 `AuthActionsContext`; all 11 e2e specs (`smoke`, `sheet-a11y`, `mobile-sheet-history.touch`) pass.
- **What this does to the two figures this file measures most carefully.** `develop`/QA now carries migrations `0001`–`0030` (was `0028`); `main`/production is unchanged at `0028` — the gap widens again, measured 19 Aug 2026 at **47 commits / 11 first-parent**. None of it touches a money table, a golden fixture, or anything the 17 Aug release-gate paragraph counted — this is scope layered on top of an already-closed phase-1 gate, not a regression against it, but the divergence is real and the next reader should not assume `main` carries any of Phases 1–3 above.
- **Track A (real Asgardeo claims) closed out the same sitting, separately from the three phases above.** The QA Asgardeo app's Access Token Attributes gained `email`/`given_name`/`family_name`/`name` (the exact claims `api/src/middleware/auth.ts:80-81` reads); confirmed live by a fresh sign-in producing a real `app_user` row with `email`/`display_name` populated for the first time, where every prior row — including the one behind `testCred.md` — had both `NULL`, unable to self-heal since nothing re-derives them after the first insert. That one pre-existing row hand-backfilled the same session.
- **One thing looked into and deliberately left alone**: `AdminLayout`'s `<Outlet/>` (`router.tsx`) renders unconditionally, the same shape `RootLayout`'s inline Operate branch already has, while `ReviewLayout`/`MineLayout` both guard theirs with a redirect effect. Traced rather than assumed: `AdminLayout` itself is not buggy — `FirstRunGate.tsx:256` gates on `isPlatformAdmin && pathname.startsWith("/admin")`, a real role check, so a non-admin can never cause it to mount. The actual gap is in the pre-existing Operate shell, which every route (including `/admin/*`) is a flat sibling of, and which has never guarded its own `<Outlet/>` against an out-of-shell pathname — a manager typing `/admin/users` into the address bar would see the admin screen's chrome-mismatched content flash inside Operate's own tab bar before its 403 read resolves. Not a data leak (the read still 403s), not something this initiative introduced, and not a quick fix — it touches the most-used shell in the app. Left unfixed by owner decision this session; recorded here so the next reader finds this reasoning instead of re-diagnosing it as a new admin-panel bug.

**19 August 2026, later the same day — the business switcher's first live pass, against `qa.fleetsettle.com` under real touch/mobile CDP emulation (360×640), not jsdom or Playwright's own emulation.** Neither of the two real QA accounts (`nimesha.isholi94@gmail.com`, `mbckchamathsilva@gmail.com`) belonged to more than one business — the switcher's whole trigger condition never existed on QA before this — so a second `business_member` row was written directly (nimesha, `manager`, on Chamath's `TestBusinesByChamath`), by decision, kept afterward rather than reverted, since a multi-business QA identity is worth more going forward than a QA environment that matches a state nobody needs preserved.

- **The core mechanism holds live.** The switcher rendered both real memberships with correct roles; selecting one landed in the correct shell with correct business-scoped data; a reload — the closest this pass can get to "background and reopen" without a real phone — went straight back in with no re-prompt, the stored selection surviving exactly as designed.
- **A genuine finding, not a defect**: there is no voluntary "Switch business" entry point anywhere in the built UI — grepped, `BusinessSwitcherSheet` has exactly one call site (`FirstRunGate.tsx`) and `clearSelectedBusinessId` exactly two (sign-out, the `BUSINESS_NOT_SELECTED` branch). The switcher only ever appears automatically. Checked against the design doc before being treated as a gap: `PLATFORM-ADMIN-AND-MULTI-BUSINESS-DESIGN-2026-08-17.md` §8.1 never specifies a manual entry point either — this is a real, deliberate scope boundary of Phase 3 as decided, not an oversight this pass discovered. Reproduced the second automatic trigger by hand (`localStorage.removeItem` on the stored key, then reload) to test past it.
- **The switch itself was watched, not inferred**: TESTA showed `Rent due Rs 1,157.33`, 4 trips, driver Sunil Perera; switching to `TestBusinesByChamath` immediately showed `Rent due Rs 3,000`, 0 trips, driver Madura — and the More screen's own row set changed with it, from TESTA's full owner-manager set down to `manager`'s narrower `Reports`/`Mileage packages`/`Sign out`. That second part is the sharper proof: it's `useMe()`/`<Can>` re-resolving the *new* business's own role, live, the exact mechanism `resolveSelectedMembership` (this session's own §0 entry above) exists to guarantee, not only the money figures changing.
- **Still not what LT-10 asks for.** CDP emulation, however real the touch events, is not a real iOS Safari or Android Chrome — this tracker has held that line since GAP-104/LT-13 and it applies here unchanged. What this pass adds is live-environment confirmation beyond jsdom/Playwright, not a substitute for a real device.

**19 August 2026, later still — a full end-to-end live QA pass at the user's own request, the first against the whole platform-admin/multi-business surface (business switcher, 6-screen admin panel — PRs #74–78), which had never been clicked before. Full account: `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-19.md`.** Bootstrapped the first `platform_admin` row on QA (DEPLOYMENT.md §12's own documented procedure, explicit user approval — the auto-mode classifier correctly blocked the first attempt as a real privilege grant) to reach the panel at all. **LT-13 closed in full**: created and closed a real test lease against `QC-0808160814-A` (the one arrangement-A vehicle with no lease history on QA), confirming the sixth and last sheet-closes-and-navigates call site — GAP-134's fix holds everywhere. **Seven confirmed defects, all fixed same session, each with its own regression test, `npm run check` clean (guard, lint, typecheck all three workspaces, web 134 files/755 tests, api unit 3/10) and golden fixtures unmoved:**

- **GAP-138** — Home's "Rent due" row said "Due since {date}" even for a due date in the future (F-4/§7 deliberately lists both due-and-overdue; only the label was wrong). Fixed with a past/future branch in `HomeScreen.tsx`.
- **GAP-139** — the business switcher marked no row as currently selected. `useSelectedBusiness()` now also returns `businessId`; the sheet takes an optional `currentBusinessId` and marks the match with `aria-current`, a check icon and " · Current" — colour never carries the signal alone.
- **GAP-140** — the lease closure summary (`CloseLeaseScreen`) showed the *gross* `amountMinor` per row instead of netting `settledMinor`/`waivedMinor` like its own total already did — overstated any partially-paid due on the one screen gating a deposit release (INV-18). Same screen, `trip_fare` was missing from `DUE_KIND_LABEL` and leaked as a raw enum string; added (`management_fee` deliberately not — it's always `owed_by_us`/partner, can never reach a customer's list).
- **GAP-141** — a closed lease never recorded `closed_at` or `final_period_treatment`, despite both being asked for/computable at existing steps; `closure_summary_shown_at` was documented (DM §13/§14) as a stored-flag gate on INV-18 but never written by any code path — `lease-closure.ts`'s own comment already recorded the real, deliberate mechanism (call ordering, not a flag), it just never made it back into the document. Fixed: `updateLeaseStatus` now writes both real fields (`closed_at` automatic whenever status becomes `"closed"`, matching `accounting-period.ts`'s own `closePeriodRow` pattern); `data-model.md` → v1.1.11 corrects §13/§14 to describe the actual mechanism and its real consequence (nothing server-side stops a direct API call skipping the summary). **Caught before it shipped**: `lease.final_period_treatment`'s own CHECK constraint (verified live against QA) only accepts `full_period`/`days_used`/`agreed_figure` — `CloseLeaseInput["finalPeriodMode"]` is `full`/`days_used`/`agreed`, so writing it unmapped would have thrown a raw constraint violation on two of the three modes. `FINAL_PERIOD_TREATMENT` maps between them.
- **GAP-142** — three of the admin panel's six screens rendered a raw Postgres `timestamptz` string verbatim (`"2026-08-19 13:14:25.253818+00"`). New shared `formatTimestamp` (`web/src/lib/`) — **its own test suite caught two real bugs in its first version before either reached a browser**: `new Date()` returns Invalid Date for Postgres's bare `+00` offset (no `:00` minutes) unless normalised first, and the fix's first draft omitted `timeZone: BUSINESS_TIMEZONE` entirely, silently rendering in the device's zone — exactly CLAUDE.md's Time section, caught only because the test machine's zone (`America/Chicago`) differs from Colombo enough to move the displayed calendar day.
- **GAP-143** — every write in the admin panel (grant/revoke admin, set allowance, approve/reject a request) returned `c.body(null, 200)` — an empty body with status 200 — against a client that only skips `res.json()` for exactly 204, so every successful call threw "Unexpected end of JSON input" client-side despite the write always landing (verified against the database each time, live, before fixing). This exact pattern appeared nowhere else in the API — grepped, four hits, all in this one new file. Both sides fixed: all four route-defs and handlers now use 204, matching what the client already handled correctly.
- **GAP-144** — recording a payment left Home's own "Rent due" figure stale (showing the pre-payment amount) until a hard reload, because `["home"]` doesn't reach `["reports", "receivables"]` by prefix. Not isolated to Quick Add: `CollectPaymentSheet` has three other callers (`LeaseHubScreen`, `CustomerDetailScreen`, `TripDetailScreen` — the last missing even `["payment"]`/`["home"]`), found via the dependency graph while fixing the first, all four now invalidate `["reports"]` too.

**What this pass did not reach, unchanged from every prior session**: LT-9 (linked-driver 403 boundary — no linked-driver account exists on QA, and forging one needs Asgardeo's own signing key, not just a script) and LT-10 (a real iOS Safari/Android Chrome device). Both still open.

**19 Aug 2026, still later — a second live pass, after PR #79's own seven fixes were already tested and pushed, found an eighth defect: GAP-145.** `GET /api/partner/{userId}` 500s live for an active `business_member` with no `ownership_share` and no `management_fee_agreement` on any vehicle — an ordinary state (an owner-manager who was never assigned a fractional share of a specific vehicle), reproduced from `Cash` → tapping a partner's own name. Read every function `getPartnerSummary` (`api/src/domain/partner.ts`) calls; all handle a zero-row result defensively (`share?.profitShareMinor ?? 0n`, early-return `[]`, row-fetch-and-reduce rather than an unguarded `BigInt()` on a raw SQL `SUM()`) — root cause not isolated to one line this session. Not a GAP-90 regression (`findBusinessMemberUserId` still correctly filters `revoked_at IS NULL`, re-checked). Full elimination account in `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-19.md`'s own F-8. ~~**Not fixed**~~ — found after PR #79 was already CI-green and awaiting the merge decision; recorded for a follow-up session rather than reopening it mid-review. **Fixed 20 Aug 2026 (`16240c3`, PR #83), and this paragraph's own diagnosis was wrong** — see the correction directly below.

**20 August 2026 — GAP-145 closed, and the elimination account above named the wrong cause.** The paragraph above concluded the root cause was "not isolated to one line," having checked every function `getPartnerSummary` calls for undefended zero-row handling. That search could not have found it, because **the cause was not in any one function's handling of missing rows — it was the number of round trips all of them made together**: `getVehicleMonthReport` queries per vehicle in a loop, and `sumAllTimeEarnedForUser` called it once per accounting period on top of that, reaching **79 Cloudflare subrequests for one six-vehicle business against Workers Free's 50-subrequest ceiling**. The missing `ownership_share` in the repro was a coincidence of which partner was tapped, not the trigger; any business with enough vehicles crosses the ceiling regardless. Measured directly against the live QA data that reproduces it, replayed through the real Hono app rather than the domain function alone. **Fixed by batching both layers** — `getVehicleMonthReport`/`getVehicleYearReport` fetch earned, costs and ownership shares once for the whole vehicle set (three `GROUP BY vehicle_id` queries instead of one per vehicle, party names resolved once across every owner), and `sumAllTimeEarnedForUser` replays every period in memory off five flat queries instead of a `getVehicleMonthReport` call per period — staying on Workers Free rather than buying headroom, the decided trade-off. Golden fixtures unmoved. **Ships with `api/tests/integration/subrequest-ceiling.test.ts`, a regression guard that counts subrequests directly** rather than asserting the symptom, so the next fan-out fails loudly instead of at a customer's six-vehicle business. **The lesson is the one worth keeping: a defect whose cause is cumulative cost cannot be found by reading each function for correctness — every function here was individually correct.**

**20 August 2026 — the source-only platform-admin/multi-business follow-up was dispositioned on `develop`, and the release-gate claim changed again.** The source report (`docs/qa/findings/2026-08-20.md` on the `docs/qa-findings-2026-08-20` branch) found ten items by sweeping three bug classes: navigation dead ends, unconstrained free text, and backend routes with no client. Six were small, unambiguous UI/product fixes and are now closed locally on `develop`: **GAP-149** (single-membership users can open the business hub and request/create another business), **GAP-150** (vehicle type create input is a closed `Bus`/`Car`/`Van` native picker, with the write schema enforcing it), **GAP-151** (admin shell has a visible `Leave admin panel` exit), **GAP-152** (zero-membership setup/revoked states have sign out), **GAP-153** (Review screens opened from Operate's More hub have Back), and **GAP-154** (Operate blocks `/me` and `/admin/*` deep links before those screens mount under Operate chrome; the browser regression asserts `/api/admin/users` is not called for a non-admin). Verified locally against the production build: web 134 files / 768 tests, full Playwright mobile suite 16/16, workspace `typecheck`/`test`/`build`/`guard`/`lint:css`, Prettier and diff whitespace all clean.

**Three broader findings from that same source report were real build work, not evidence debt: GAP-146, GAP-147 and GAP-148.** **GAP-146 is closed locally**: change vehicle arrangement, vehicle archive/unarchive, customer archive/unarchive, driver archive/unarchive, change daily-lease driver, creating a skip-day exception, held-driver-deposit top-up/reduce/apply/refund/retain movements, driver-view deposit movement history, and deposit movement void are reachable and covered by component/API plus mobile Playwright smoke tests. Undoing a skipped day is tracked with GAP-147's wider void/correction surface rather than GAP-146's create-only slice. **GAP-147** covers the correction surface: W-50/W-58 void endpoints still exist with no UI caller; customer and driver write-off voids are now reachable from their detail screens, driver advance void and driver offset void are reachable from staff driver detail, skipped daily-lease days can be undone from the vehicle overview, and unreceived incident customer contributions can be voided from incident detail while staying visible in history, leaving expense, attachment and those six slices as the only reachable void flows. **GAP-148 is closed locally**: a lease due can now be written off from `LeaseHubScreen` by an owner/owner-manager, closed leases and closed trips can now record source-scoped post-closure charges from their own detail screens, customer and driver detail can now record vehicle-linked standalone balance write-offs and recoveries against listed write-offs, and the Worker now validates write-off `vehicleId` against the selected business before writing. Component tests, mobile Playwright smokes and the focused write-off integration test cover those slices. That makes the 17 Aug sentence "release is gated entirely on evidence, not code" still false until GAP-147 is closed or explicitly deferred by the owner with its reason recorded. The local QA account, test plan, and remaining-source-review list now live in the `develop` copy of `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-20.md`; [docs/qa/live-test-plan.md](docs/qa/live-test-plan.md)'s LT-14 row records the browser evidence for the six closed items and the later GAP-146/GAP-147/GAP-148 slices.

**The ten items map to nine gap ids, and the tenth is not a missing one.** F-1→GAP-149, F-2→GAP-150, F-3→GAP-151, F-4→GAP-146, F-5→GAP-153, F-6→GAP-154, F-7→GAP-152, F-9→GAP-147, F-10→GAP-148 — nine. **F-8 carries no gap id by design**: it is the source report's own process finding, *"TRACKER.md's release-gate claim does not hold given F-4/F-9/F-10,"* aimed at this file's 17 Aug sentence rather than at the product. Retracting that sentence in this same 20 Aug pass (below, and at [§4](#4-every-open-gap)) is F-8's disposition — a product gap would need a build decision; a claim about this tracker needed only correcting it here.

**20 August 2026, later still — fixing five UI defects on `fix/write-off-ui-review` surfaced a new defect, GAP-155, unrelated to the source sweep above: it came from reading `listWriteOffsHandler` and `recordWriteOffRecoveryHandler` side by side while gating `CustomerDetailScreen`'s write-off query.** `use-cases.md`'s own role table (§2, W-49) restricts one row — *"Write-off (UC-90), waiver above the threshold"* — to Owner/owner-manager, ✗ for Manager. `recordWriteOffRecoveryHandler` (`api/src/handlers/write-off.ts:126`) reads that row correctly: it requires `dailyOperations`, the same capability as *"Daily cards, trips, expenses, collections"* (✓ for all three operational roles), because recording a recovery is money arriving against an existing write-off, not a fresh act of forgiveness — INV-15 (`user-flows.md`) already describes it as an ordinary linked payment. But `listWriteOffsHandler` (`api/src/handlers/write-off.ts:48`) requires `writeOffOrWaiveAboveThreshold` — the *create/void* capability, Owner/owner-manager only — and it is the only route either detail screen has to discover which write-off id a recovery is recorded against. A manager can call the recovery endpoint the docs say they should be able to, and has no way to reach it: **the list endpoint's capability, not the recovery endpoint's, is out of step with the role table.** GAP-148's original build made `CustomerDetailScreen` and `DriverDetailScreen` inconsistent about this — the driver screen already gated its query on `canWriteOff` (Owner-only), the customer screen did not, so a manager got a live 403 rendered as a permanent failure banner on every customer page. **Fixed on `fix/write-off-ui-review`**: the customer screen now gates identically to the driver screen, which stops the broken banner and makes both screens consistent — but consistently unreachable for a manager, which is the safe interim, not the resolution. Loosening a list endpoint's capability is a tenancy/access decision (CLAUDE.md: *"a capability the role lacks returns 403, because the row's existence is not the secret"* still has to be decided correctly here, not patched around), so it stays a role-table decision for the owner: either `listWriteOffsHandler` drops to `dailyOperations` to match the recovery endpoint it exists to serve, or a narrower recoverable-write-offs read surface is added at that capability instead. ~~**Not fixed.**~~

**20 August 2026, later still — the owner chose the first option, and GAP-155 is closed.** `listWriteOffsHandler` now requires `dailyOperations`, matching `recordWriteOffRecoveryHandler` — a manager can list write-offs to find one to record a recovery against, exactly the reachability the role table's own STAFF row already granted them for recovery. Create and void stay `writeOffOrWaiveAboveThreshold` — only who can *see* widened, not who can create or reverse one. Closing this exposed a second, related bug the interim fix's own uniform gating had been masking: `DriverDetailScreen`'s "Void write-off" button carried **no capability guard on the client at all** — harmless while a manager could never reach the section to see it, but live the moment `canRecordRecovery` opened the section up. Fixed alongside the endpoint change; `CustomerDetailScreen`'s equivalent button was already correctly gated. Both screens' capability wiring, the integration test asserting the old 403 (now asserting 200 for a manager), and both client test suites cover the new shape; the missing-guard regression is its own test, confirmed failing against the pre-fix button before being trusted.

**20 August 2026, later still — GAP-147 closed, all sixteen W-50/W-58 void endpoints now reachable.** The 20 Aug source sweep's own inventory (F-9) found eight void endpoints with a complete backend and no client caller — every one has a UI path now, in four sittings on `fix/write-off-ui-review`. **Capital contribution, banking event and partner payout void** — all three already listed on `PartnerDetailScreen`, only missing a Void button; closing this also found the screen's other four reads had no client-side capability gate at all, so a manager reaching it from `CashScreen` (nothing blocks that navigation) got the whole summary's 403 rendered as a permanent "Loading…", the exact shape GAP-155 fixed for write-offs — fixed alongside. **Obligation void** (a post-closure charge, INV-36 §3.10's only directly-voidable kind) — reachable via `CustomerDetailScreen`'s existing outstanding-dues list, since the one client sheet that raises one (`PostClosureChargeSheet`) is customer-only; `findOutstandingObligationsForParty` already filters `voided_at IS NULL`, so a voided charge simply drops off the list, no "voided" badge needed. **Vehicle unavailability create and void** — GAP-26 shipped a full backend with zero UI at all, not even creation, so this was the one item needing two new sheets rather than a button on an existing screen; wired into `VehicleCalendarScreen`, whose own read already fetches the month's live outages to render the calendar. **Write-off recovery, advance settlement and adjustment void** — the three genuinely new backend reads, each following the same shape once the choice was made (new top-level list endpoints, not embedding sub-record arrays on the parent read, matching how write-off's own list already works): `dailyOperations`, matching the void endpoint each list exists to serve (adjustment's own create capability escalates conditionally for an above-threshold waiver; void does not, so list follows void's flat gate, not create's), a `*ListRowSchema` extending the existing create-response schema with `voidedAt`/`voidedReason` (and `occurredOn` where the table had none of its own — write-off recovery has no date column, INV-15, so an inner join to `payment` is exact rather than a guess). All three client sheets share one shape: a single `Sheet` with two modes, list or a void-reason form for the tapped row, toggled by local state — `BusinessSwitcherSheet`'s own established pattern for this — rather than stacking a second `Sheet` on top, untested territory in this client. **Every batch verified against the real test database before being trusted** — the three new list endpoints added 15 integration tests across three files (`write-off.test.ts`, `driver-money.test.ts`, `adjustment.test.ts`, `git diff`-counted rather than estimated), and every pre-existing integration file this pass touched (`vehicle-unavailability.test.ts`, `partner.test.ts`/`partner-void.test.ts`/`partner-summary.test.ts`) was re-run against the live branch and confirmed still green, not assumed. Full account across four commits on `fix/write-off-ui-review`; PR #85 carries the diff.

**20 August 2026, later still — a live QA data-accuracy and release-readiness pass against `qa.fleetsettle.com`, real CDP mobile/touch emulation (360×640), the first session to attempt LT-9 and the first to close a real accounting period through the deployed product at all.** Requested directly by the owner once phase 1's build queue read empty. A Neon branch (`qa-snapshot-pre-close-2026-08-20`, off `br-square-sound-afb68wft`) was taken first as a rollback point, verified byte-identical on row counts before being trusted. A new read-only script, `api/scripts/ledger-audit.mjs` (`npm run audit:ledger`), encodes 24 invariants re-derived from source rather than guessed (`computeObligationStatus`, live-allocation reconciliation, deposit balances never negative, every cross-entity join checked for a tenancy leak) — run via the Neon MCP tools directly against QA both before and after this session's writes, 24/24 clean both times.

- **The month close ran live for the first time in this product's history.** TESTA's August 2026 period (10 unconfirmed days, 3 open trips, 1 advance, 1 outstanding due, 3 open incidents, U-7's checklist correctly non-blocking throughout) was closed through the real `/period/close` screen; September opened in the same transaction, confirmed via direct query (no gap, no overlap, exactly one open period per business throughout).
- **The single highest-value finding: `assert_period_open()`'s closed-period guard (migration `0008`) confirmed end to end, live, for what is apparently the first time.** A pre-close fuel expense (`NC-1234`, Rs 56.66) was voided after the close — `POST /api/expense/{id}/void` → **409**, `{"error":"That accounting period is closed","code":"PERIOD_CLOSED"}`, rendered inline by the client ("That accounting period is closed"), zero console crash. The row was confirmed still untouched afterward (`voided_at IS NULL`) — the atomic-transaction guarantee held, not a partial write.
- **`applyCreditForward` (`api/src/domain/credit-forward.ts`, GAP-5b) confirmed wired at all 7 obligation-creation call sites** (`confirmDay`, `trip` ×2, `billing-period`, `mileage`, `post-closure-charge`, `incident`) after a live overpayment (Rs 588.88 against a Rs 559.74 due) left a genuine Rs 29.14 unallocated credit — verified by source rather than forced through a synthetic trip, since the one live trip available for this customer had already settled before the test.
- **Ageing buckets (1–30/31–60/61–90/over-90) verified against a clearly-labelled backdated seed** — a dedicated test customer (`ZZ AGEING-TEST SEED — do not use (2026-08-20)`) plus 4 obligations inserted directly (`kind='other'`, no FK-bound `source_id`), posted into the open period as the `assert_period_open` trigger requires. `GET /api/reports/ageing` rendered all four buckets at the exact seeded amounts. Owner decision: left in place — labelled and inert, no real report a human reads would mistake it for money.
- **LT-9 closed — the linked-driver 403/404 boundary, exercised live for the first time.** `QA2 Driver 0808155856` linked via a real invite code, redeemed by `nimesha.k.dev@gmail.com` (a second real Asgardeo identity registered under the same email for testing — a different `sub`, so `findAppUserBySub` correctly treated it as a new identity per DM §3, not a bug). `GET /api/driver-view` returned exactly this driver's own data (Rs 5,000 held deposit, matching), no id field anywhere in the response — INV-25 confirmed structurally. Ordinary navigation on `/me` triggered `/api/daily-lease`, `/api/day-record`, `/api/trip`, `/api/reports/receivables`, `/api/home/paperwork-warnings`, `/api/home/deposit-releases` — **all 403**, live, unprompted. Confirmed airtight by source: `grep`ing `api/src/auth/policy.ts` for `LINKED_DRIVER` finds exactly two hits — its own definition and its listing under `viewOwnData` alone — so no other capability, and therefore no other endpoint including the staff-facing `GET /api/driver/{id}/view` (A5), can ever be satisfied by this role, regardless of which id is requested.
- **GAP-156 found by that same LT-9 session — filed above, not fixed.** The linked-driver `Mine` shell has no sign-out affordance anywhere: `AppShell`'s tab bar is `null` for `shell === "mine"`, no `MoreScreen` route exists under `/me`, and `Sign out` appears nowhere else in the client. The mirror image of GAP-152 (zero-membership sign-out, fixed earlier the same day) for the populated single-driver case — undiscoverable before this pass because no linked-driver account had ever signed in to a real browser until now.

**What this pass did not reach: LT-10** (a real iOS Safari/Android Chrome device) — still open, still the item to be least willing to skip, unchanged from every prior session's own note. A manual attempt to replay a captured Bearer token with a forged `x-business-id` header (to probe the cross-driver boundary a second way) was correctly blocked by the harness's own safety classifier; the source-plus-live evidence above was judged sufficient without it.

**21 August 2026 — PR #88 merged to `develop` overnight, a second gap of the same shape as GAP-156 found reviewing the platform-tier UI, and LT-10 reported done by the owner outside this session's own tooling.** `git log` confirms PR #88 (`qa/ledger-audit-and-final-pass`) merged clean, carrying its own review-fix commit (`467f402` — deduplicated the accounting-period overlap check's double-counted pairs and corrected two mislabelled invariants). **What that review-fix commit did not touch**: `api/scripts/ledger-audit.mjs`'s own SonarCloud quality gate, still red on the merged commit at **75.5% duplication on new code** (required ≤3%) — the ~20 checks sharing one repeated `JOIN … WHERE x.business_id <> y.business_id` shape were flagged, not deduplicated, before the merge went through. Not a release blocker (Sonar is not a required check here), but real debt on a script this file now cites as the audit baseline, and worth fixing before it's trusted as a template for the next batch of checks. **GAP-157 found the same session**: `BusinessSwitcherSheet` — the one screen an existing member reaches to add a second business — offers "Create a business" and nothing else; `RedeemInviteForm` has exactly one call site and it only renders for `businesses.length === 0`. The backend (`redeemInvite`) has no such restriction. **GAP-149's own 20 Aug fix closed exactly half of this same screen's gap** — the create door for an existing member opened, the join-by-code door next to it did not, and nobody noticed because the two were never checked side by side until this session did. **The owner reports LT-10 (a real iOS Safari/Android Chrome pass) is now done, separately from this session's own tooling** — this file does not yet carry that row's own account (repro steps, what was tested, pass/fail) because this session did not run it and cannot supply the detail its own convention requires; update it directly once specifics are available, the same way LT-9's row above was written from what was actually witnessed rather than from "it's done."

**21 August 2026, later the same day — GAP-156 and GAP-157 both closed, and the PR #88 Sonar duplication addressed at the source.** All three fixed on one branch (`fix/gap-156-157-signout-join-code`), off `develop` post-PR-#89. **GAP-156**: `MineScreen.tsx` gained the same `Sign out` affordance every other shell already has, rendered after the read-only statement (`TwoBalances`/`DriverActivitySections`), never above it — the shell stays read-only for money, sign-out is an account action, not a write. Rather than a third hand-copied confirm-sheet (`MoreScreen.tsx` and `FirstRunGate.tsx`'s `SetupSignOut` were already two near-identical copies), the row was extracted once to `web/src/components/SignOutRow.tsx` and both existing call sites switched onto it — the same shape of fix as the ledger-audit change below, applied before a third copy could exist to be flagged. `MineScreen.test.tsx`'s own "no write buttons" assertion is now precise about what it means: `getAllByRole("button")` has exactly one, and it says `Sign out`. **GAP-157**: `BusinessSwitcherSheet` gained a "Join a business" section next to "Create a business," gated on the same `createBusiness` prop (the two doors travel together at every in-shell call site; `FirstRunGate`'s mandatory multi-choice state, which passes neither, still shows neither). `RedeemInviteResponse`'s `business_member` kind selects the newly-joined business before the reload the same way `handleBusinessCreated` already does for a newly-created one; its `driver_link` kind (no `businessId` on the wire, W-57) clears the cache and closes without changing the selection, same as `FirstRunGate`'s own zero-membership `onRedeemed` already does — the new membership is reachable from this same sheet next time it opens rather than guessed at. Opening either form collapses the other, so the sheet never shows both open at once. **The Sonar duplication**: `api/scripts/ledger-audit.mjs`'s twelve near-identical tenancy-join checks (child row's own `business_id` disagreeing with a joined parent's) collapsed onto one `tenancyCheck(childTable, childAlias, fkColumn, parentTable, parentAlias)` helper, called once per table pair — same 24 checks, same labels, same SQL semantics per check (verified string-for-string against the originals before replacing them, and the script re-run for a syntax/lint/format pass; a live run against this session's own `.dev.vars` failed on a stale Neon password unrelated to this change, so the query text is confirmed correct by inspection and by every other gate, not by a live execution this session couldn't authenticate for). **Not yet confirmed**: whether this actually drops SonarCloud's duplication percentage below the gate — that only re-scans once this branch reaches a PR, so the claim here is "the twelve duplicated blocks are gone from the source," not "the gate is green," until CI says so. Full local gate clean throughout: `npm run guard`/`lint`/`format:check`/`typecheck` (web + api) all pass, and `npm test` — web 144 files / 849 tests, `packages/shared` 12 files / 91 tests, `api` unit project 3 files / 10 tests — all green; golden fixtures untouched (no `api/` route, domain or query file changed). `detect_changes` reported low risk on all seven changed files.

**21 August 2026, later still — GAP-158 reverses GAP-87, found during manual QA.** A user testing `BookTripScreen` by hand hit *"This vehicle is configured for arrangement A, not a charter"* — investigating the raw-arrangement-letter leak (a house-style violation on its own: internal codes never reach the interface) led back to the rule itself. Checked against source rather than assumed wrong: `user-flows.md` F-5.1 carries no `Pre` on arrangement at all, unlike F-2.1/F-1.7, and its own INV-1 clause is dates-scoped, not classification-scoped — GAP-87 (9 Aug) had over-applied GAP-84's shape to a flow that never specified it. `resolveBorneByDefault` already treated an idle arrangement-A vehicle as ordinary; the real conflict was already caught, and caught better, by `one_arrangement_per_vehicle_day` → `buildDoubleBookedError`. Reversed with the user's sign-off after a plan-mode review: the arrangement gate deleted from `bookTripHandler`, `VehicleOverviewScreen`'s "Book trip" made unconditional, `BookTripScreen`'s own pre-check (added the same session while fixing the message leak) removed with it; `VehicleCalendarScreen`'s free-day tap-through stays lease-first for A, unchanged, by explicit UX decision. `user-flows.md` v1.1.16 records the corrected rule. Full account, including the Neon-flake investigation that ruled this change out as the cause of 22 unrelated timeouts, in GAP-158's own §4 row.

**21 August 2026, later still — GAP-159 widens the same audit codebase-wide, and lands a guard rule against a third occurrence.** Asked to check for more of GAP-158's shape given "this did not capture in multiple QA testing" — every `_LABEL` map in the client was enumerated, then every raw field access checked against its own wire schema before being called a finding, ruling out several look-alikes (`.reason` fields that are genuine free text, `entry.kind` inside a dev-only thrown `Error`, `xxxState.kind` — `useQueryState`'s own discriminator, not a business enum). Four real leaks found and fixed (`docType` ×4 call sites, `obligation.kind`, `partyType`, `payment.status` — full account in GAP-159's own §4 row) and a new `copy/raw-enum-in-jsx` guard rule added, deliberately scoped to three fields whose names never collide with anything else in this codebase. Built on a fresh branch off `develop`, then rebased onto `fix/gap-158-trip-arrangement-gate` once the new guard rule's own first real-world catch turned out to be GAP-158's own not-yet-merged fix — confirmed by running the rule and getting exactly the two `.arrangement` findings GAP-158's PR resolves, nothing else, before rebasing to pick that fix up and re-confirming clean. PR opened stacked on GAP-158's, not against `develop` directly, since the guard rule genuinely cannot pass without it.

**21 August 2026, later still — GAP-160 closes a real gap PR #95's own review found in GAP-158.** A reviewer flagged that removing the arrangement gate leaves INV-1 enforced only by `one_arrangement_per_vehicle_day`, which has nothing to catch a trip booked against a lease whose own dates reach past `generate-day-cards`'s 90-day materialisation horizon — confirmed real by reading `generateDayCards` directly, not taken on faith. Fixed with a direct `lease.start_date`/`end_date` check, run *after* the trip's own allocation insert rather than before (an earlier ordering regressed GAP-44's own test — caught by running the full `book a trip` group, not just the new cases, before calling it done). One premise in the first draft — that a closed lease's `endDate` is never rewritten — was itself wrong, found by reading `lease-closure.ts` rather than assumed; corrected in the code comment and the test before landing rather than shipped stating something false twice in one session. Full account, including the exact regression and the correction, in GAP-160's own §4 row.

**22 August 2026 — a live QA pass targeting the refreshed `docs/qa/scenarios/` catalogue (PR #107) found the "printed slip" acceptance criterion, recorded as shipped since 17 August, does not exist. Filed as GAP-170.** `use-cases.md` v1.2.13 (17 Aug) decided UC-57/UC-19/UC-55 split in two — "the printed slip ships, the no-login share link does not" — and `docs/README.md`'s status table, plus GAP-65's own row here, both record the slip as built. Checked live against `qa.fleetsettle.com` (customer detail's action menu, driver detail's action menu, the driver's own authenticated Mine shell) and by source (`grep` across all of `web/src` and `api/src` for any print/slip/statement mechanism — zero hits, matching what the scenario-catalogue refresh's own suite 01/05/10 rewrites had already found independently from source alone). **No printed slip, statement, or PDF exists anywhere in this client, for any role.** The consequence is sharper than a status-table correction: GAP-65's phase-2 deferral was reasoned safe on "nothing becomes unreachable — an unlinked one is handed the printed slip"; that premise is false, so as the build stands today an unlinked driver or customer has no way at all to see their financial position outside logging in. GAP-65's own row below is corrected to flag this rather than silently left to read as still-sound reasoning. `docs/README.md`'s F-6.6 status-table row corrected the same session. Full account: `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-22.md`.

**23 August 2026 — same session continued: a broader UI/UX and data-accuracy pass against `qa.fleetsettle.com`, requested after GAP-170's docs were corrected. No new defects.** Full account: `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-22.md`'s Part 2. Three live figures cross-checked directly against the QA Neon branch (`br-square-sound-afb68wft`) and matched exactly: a driver deposit's net position across six rows including five fully-corrected duplicate entries (Rs 5,000, matching the client on both the driver-detail and Mine screens — this session's own first SQL attempt had a bug, wrong enum literal, caught before trusting it); Home's "Rent due · Rs 10,000" against the four `ZZ AGEING-TEST SEED` obligations; Home's "Expected · Rs 3,000" against `day_record.expected_minor`. **One full day-confirmation write tested live end to end**: tapping "Paid in full" on NC-1234's 23 Aug card wrote `day_record.earned_minor` and a fully-allocated `payment`/`payment_allocation` pair against a `daily_amount` obligation together, correctly and atomically — direct confirmation of "every money write is one transaction" and "earned and received never collapse" under a real tap, not just a domain-code read; left in place afterward as a genuine, correct confirmation rather than voided, since it's indistinguishable from the business's existing paid-day history. UI/UX: dark mode, 360×640 and 320px reflow all checked clean on Home (previously only checked on the driver's Mine shell). One untraced console item — a CSP `eval` block with no source-code match anywhere in `web/src` and no observed functional consequence — recorded rather than chased further. **Also hit and cleared**: Asgardeo's reCAPTCHA blocked every automated re-sign-in attempt after the session's earlier sign-out/sign-in cycling; not solved by this session (that is exactly what it exists to catch) — the user solved it once manually, after which the session continued normally.

**23 August 2026, later still — same session, four basic business scenarios verified live end to end before a larger test push, requested directly by the owner. Two real defects found and filed (GAP-171, GAP-172); everything else checked out.** Scenarios: (1) register a car/bus, default it to daily lease; (2) the same bus occasionally takes a multi-day (2–25 day) charter while still on daily lease, with its own agreed amount and driver fee; (3) an incident/breakdown during that charter, its cost borne by the business rather than the customer; (4) a manager who missed a few days can confirm or act on each one separately. Grounded in F-5.1/F-5.2/F-4.1/F-4.2 and UC §6.7's borne-by matrix before touching the browser, then run live on `qa.fleetsettle.com` against a fresh vehicle (`QA-SCEN-BUS1`) built for this test alone. **(1) and (4) confirmed clean**: vehicle creation defaults to Bus + Daily lease and saves on level-1 fields only, arrangement is confirmed independent of vehicle type (switching Car/Bus never changed the pre-selected arrangement); every pending day — NC-1234's real backlog and QA-SCEN-BUS1's own first day — confirmed individually with its own tap, each producing a correct, atomic `day_record`+`payment`+`obligation` write, including one instance of a day confirmed after its own month's period had already closed correctly landing `posted_period_id` on the open period while `belongs_to_period_id` correctly held the closed one it actually happened in — the write-side half of the "facts arrive late" (W-35) mechanism, working exactly as designed. **(2) confirmed correct, but only after finding and working around GAP-171**: the booked trip paused exactly 5 lease days and showed the exact Rs 17,500 lost-income warning F-5.1 promises, agreed amount and driver fee both landed correctly once the sticky-CTA overlap (GAP-171, this vehicle's own Step 1) was worked around by scrolling first. **(3) confirmed borne-by is correct but found GAP-172**: the repair expense's `borne_by` auto-resolved to `us` exactly per UC §6.7, but the expense could not be linked to either the trip or the incident by any client screen, so "Costs so far" and "Total repairs" both stayed Rs 0 — the money direction is right, the containers that are supposed to gather it are unreachable. Full account: `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-23.md`.

**23 August 2026, later still again — an external code evaluation of 29 defects was verified claim by claim, and filed as thirteen rows (GAP-174…GAP-186).** The evaluation itself (`CODE-EVALUATION-2026-08-23.md`, by an outside evaluator) was not trusted on sight: every citation was re-read against source at `HEAD` before any of it reached this table. **Ten claims did not survive unchanged.** One was false outright (the audit trigger it called "pending" has been live since migration `0002`). One had the wrong mechanism and a worse bug behind it — concurrent claim submits do not 500 as filed, they silently insert a duplicate, because **no unique constraint exists at all**. One was a deliberate design mistaken for an oversight, and one had already been closed as GAP-141. Several were understated: `findObligationForBusiness` does not have a forgotten `forUpdate` argument, it has **no such parameter in its signature**; `allocateAgainstOldest` returns `void`, so the assertion the evaluation asked for has nothing to assert against.

**The verification found five things the evaluation did not, and one of them reverses a fix.** `listOffsetsForDriver` was filed as missing a voided-row filter — it is **not a bug**: the query deliberately projects `voided_at` and `voided_reason`, and `DriverActivitySections.tsx:217-228` renders voided offsets struck-through with their reason, the same intentional pattern the evaluation itself noted elsewhere. Adding the filter would have deleted void history from the driver's own statement. The evaluation's own wording hedged — *"inflates if summed naïvely"* — and nothing sums it. **Separately: no golden fixture exercises a voided row anywhere** (all three contain zero), so W-50's void-and-replace mechanism has no regression coverage at all — filed as GAP-181, and it is the reason two of the defects above could have shipped unnoticed.

**GAP-173's own premise was corrected by this pass.** The entry below records the write side as "genuinely correct — checked again to be sure." That holds for `day_record` and does not hold generally: `insertInsuranceClaim` (`incident.ts:619`) sets `posted_period_id` alone while the paired `insertIncidentRecovery` three lines down sets both, so two rows describing one incident disagree at the moment of insert. Filed as GAP-174. **The check that was run was real; its scope was narrower than the sentence it produced** — which is the same shape as the first-pass verdict GAP-173 itself overturned, one layer down.

**Also filed: four features agreed with the owner the same day** (`PENDING-FEATURES-DESIGN-2026-08-23.md`) — GAP-183 through GAP-186. Two of that note's own premises were wrong and are corrected in the plan: it claims the W-35 write half "has been correct all along" (see GAP-174), and that two hand-maintained trigger lists "neither fail loudly" — `check:drift` runs `assert-no-trigger-drift.sql` in three workflows and catches both, so two budgeted tests were dropped from GAP-185's scope.

**Everything above is consolidated in [COMBINED-PLAN-2026-08-23.md](COMBINED-PLAN-2026-08-23.md)** — thirteen steps on two independent tracks, eight decisions recorded with their declined alternatives, and no open questions. Its Part 2 carries all ten corrections to the two source notes; its Part 4 records the six defects closed **without** a fix, so they are not re-filed.

**23 August 2026, later still — a direct follow-up question from the owner ("shouldn't a confirmed day belong to the day it's for, not the day it's confirmed?") found GAP-173, the read-side half of the same W-35 mechanism the entry above had called correct.** The write side genuinely is correct — checked again to be sure, not just re-asserted: `belongs_to_period_id` on the late-confirmed row correctly points at August, matching `business_date` exactly. But `user-flows.md` F-8.1's own Accept clause is explicit about what should happen next: *"reports for the open month show the item flagged as belonging elsewhere."* Grepped for it properly this time: `belongsToPeriodId` appears nowhere in any report query (`api/src/queries/reports.ts` filters every sum exclusively on `postedPeriodId`), nowhere in the day-record API response, and nowhere in `web/src` at all — not read back by any handler, not rendered by any screen. So September's own report will show this Rs 3,500 blended into its ordinary numbers with **no indication anywhere that it actually happened in August** — the exact flag F-8.1/INV-10 promise never gets drawn. The first pass at this finding, in the entry above, called it "working exactly as designed, not a bug" — that verdict covered the write, which is correct, but skipped checking whether the read side the spec also requires was actually built; it wasn't. Filed as **GAP-173**, found by the owner's own question rather than by this session's own testing.

**23 August 2026, later still — same session, Part 3: the QA session expired again (a real, apparently short-lived pattern for this identity, not a one-off), reCAPTCHA hit and cleared a second time, and a further round closed one deferred item and cross-checked two more reports in full.** Full account: `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-22.md`'s Part 3. An existing open incident on NC-1234 let the incident-contribution deposit-source check (deferred in Part 1 §6 to avoid creating throwaway test data) close cleanly with no write at all — the "Customer contribution" sheet has exactly three fields, no payment-source field, confirmed live. "Where is our cash"'s deposit (Rs 6,000) and advance (Rs 500) figures, and all four buckets of the ageing report, matched the ledger exactly. "Record expense" confirmed U-2 (level-1 fields only, submit disabled until they're filled) live at 360×640. **One accessibility thread found while chasing a Chrome "no label associated with a form field" console issue, not conclusively resolved**: `DateField.tsx` is correctly wired and ruled out, but `MoneyField.tsx:67`'s button variant and `RecordExpenseSheet.tsx:162`'s Category field both render a visible label with no programmatic (`htmlFor`) association to their control, unlike `DateField`'s own pattern in the same sheet — a real inconsistency, but not definitively the trigger of the specific issue that prompted the search (buttons aren't "form fields" in that audit's technical sense). Recorded, not filed as a GAP, pending a session that opens the accessibility tree before the sheet mounts to pin it down properly.

## 1. Where things stand

**Backend is complete through P13. Frontend is complete through Web-P8b. Both environments are deployed, and a real person can now log in to them.** As of 5 August 2026 the only thing still blocked on anyone outside this repository is **P14 messaging**, waiting on twelve Meta template approvals — **11 Aug 2026: P14 is now phase 2 by the owner's own phase model (below), so "blocked" describes when it can finish, not when it can start being sized.**

**"Complete" in the row above means built, not reachable — those turned out to be different claims.** Until A0 (6 Aug), the account the product's own signup flow created held `role: "owner"`, the one role with no screen — every real signup landed on `NotBuiltYetScreen` with nothing else to do. The Operate rows in §3 below were never wrong about what was *built*; they were silent about whether anyone could *become* the role that reaches it. A0 fixed new signups; **A11 (7 Aug) fixed the other half** — a second owner, a manager, or a linked driver can now actually get an account (`POST /api/business-member/invite` + `POST /api/invite/redeem`, `POST /api/driver/{id}/link-invite`). **B0b (8 Aug) narrowed the gap further without closing it**: an `owner`/`driver` token now lands inside a real Review/Mine shell (redirect-to-default-tab, `<Can>`-gated affordances, the whole plumbing) — but every screen inside it is still `NotBuiltYetScreen`. Reachable, still not usable; B4/B5 are what change that.

That changes what "remaining work" means again. Until 5 Aug the product could not be reached at all — no deployment, and an auth stub whose token the Worker was always going to refuse. Until 6 Aug, the one role reachable through it was the one with nothing built. **As of 8–9 Aug every role can get an account, land inside its own real shell, and — for the owner and owner-manager — actually use it**: B4 Wave 1 turned the Review shell and all six phase-1 reports from placeholders into screens. **9 Aug closed the live 500 (GAP-90) and both money facts that read as zero (A10a/GAP-39, A10b/GAP-10)**, plus GAP-88's cron/user-action conflict and GAP-87's sibling to F1. **10 Aug closed B4 entirely** (Wave 2: GAP-70/GAP-71), **then closed GAP-101 — F2's client half, and the largest single item inside B9**: the client now has a real read-error contract (`QueryState`/`useQueryState`, UI §6.4/M-28), and a guard-script rule stops the next screen from shipping without one. **10 Aug, later, found two gaps ahead of everything then queued** — GAP-103 (opening balances confirm live and connect to no money table anywhere) and GAP-104 (the mobile `ActionSheet`/`Sheet` history race, disabling Quick Add's Fuel/Expense/New trip on any real touch device) — filed as **F3** and **F4**, on the same "live-and-silently-wrong outranks unbuilt" rule that put GAP-3, GAP-56, GAP-75 and GAP-90 ahead of their own queues. **Both closed 11 Aug**, alongside B15, B6, B5's core and the B2 UI callers — see §0's dated entries.

**11 Aug, later still: an independent validation of that day's own low-API branch found F3 doesn't reach backward — GAP-109, F5.** Materialising an opening balance on commit, correctly, means a balance confirmed *before* F3 shipped never got materialised and never will on its own — live-confirmed on QA (a real Rs 2,000/Rs 5,000 pair, absent from both reports) and against source. **Production is unaffected only in the sense that it has nothing to un-affect**: it has never carried F3, so the `develop` → `main` merge does not retroactively fix anything already confirmed there. Filed with its own blocker, GAP-110 (the confirm sheet's remedy path is itself unusable at 360×640), as **F5** — now the head of the queue, the same rank F3/F4 held before they closed. Five smaller findings from the same pass (GAP-111–116) are UI/test/doc gaps, none money- or tenancy-shaped. Both queues, and the whole 18-row list previously parked as "correct to leave," are now phase 1 under the owner's 11 Aug phase model — see below.

**11 Aug, later still — F5 closed the same day, both halves.** GAP-110 first, since GAP-109's own remedy runs through it: `OpeningBalanceScreen` was giving two real actions the one sticky-CTA slot `Screen`'s own `primaryAction` reserves — `Confirm and go live` (F-0.2 step 6, the actual terminal action) now takes it, `Save as draft` (F-0.2's own *Alternate*) drops to an ordinary in-content button, the same one-sticky-CTA shape `CloseMonthScreen` already uses for a single terminal action. Then GAP-109: `commitOpeningBalance`'s idempotency check moved off `status === 'draft'` (a proxy) onto whether the batch has any active `opening_balance_posting` row (the real fact) — a batch already materialised stays a no-op on retry exactly as before, but a batch `committed` with zero postings, which is every batch confirmed before F3 shipped, now materialises on the next `Confirm`. **Self-healing, deliberately, over the alternative TRACKER's own GAP-109 row had recorded as the other option**: no one-off backfill migration touching live data behind anyone's back, no write outside a normal user action — the fix is that pressing the button the fix to GAP-110 just made reachable now actually does something, on any stranded batch, indefinitely, not only once. New integration test reproduces the exact pre-F3 state directly (`insertBatch`/`insertEntries`/`markBatchCommitted` called under the domain function, bypassing it — the API itself can no longer produce this state once the fix lands, only find it) and proves both the recovery and that a further retry stays idempotent; both new tests (web and API) were confirmed to fail against their respective pre-fix code before being trusted, this session's own instance of the standing discipline (§5). Full gate clean: `npm run check` 0 lint errors, typecheck clean across all three workspaces, web 113 files/565 tests, api unit 3/3, shared 12/12; the touched `openingBalance.test.ts` file run alone against the live test branch, 13/13, twice. **What is left on Track A is A8, A14 and A9b; on Track B, GAP-44 (B9's last row) and B7.** [Plan.md](Plan.md) sequences all of it.

**Two standing observations about `docs/`, both still true, and one now updated.** The 7 August pass found that **`docs/` needed no edit for any of its five gaps** — each was the code failing a specification already correct and already specific (GAP-56 against UC-94 read with U-8; GAP-54 fully written as F-1.2/UC-94; GAP-57 as F-5.3; GAP-1's scoping as UC-70/71/72 and W-49). **A recognisable second class is where the owning document is silent, incomplete, or in tension with another one, and the documents move before the code does**: GAP-10, GAP-70, GAP-71 and GAP-88 (9 Aug), the ST-5/F-1.7/off-road/W-58 batch (12 Aug, below). GAP-70/71's `doc-change` landed 9 Aug; GAP-10's and GAP-88's landed later the same day (`user-flows.md` v1.1.5, `data-model.md` v1.1.3/D-9). **GAP-59 and GAP-60 were the two rows this note used to describe as correctly left unwritten** — the 9 Aug shape decision recorded here but never landed in `data-model.md` for GAP-60, and GAP-59 filed as a design question with no invariant of its own. **Both now have their doc-change, 12 Aug 2026**: GAP-59 is `user-flows.md` INV-33 and `data-model.md` §17 D-14 (a real composite FK, not left as a code-review note); GAP-60 is `data-model.md` §17 D-16, its 9 Aug shape finally written down rather than only remembered. Neither claim in this paragraph should have kept describing them as unwritten past this sitting — corrected here rather than left to drift a third time.
