### The phase model — set 11 August 2026

**Decided by the owner, 11 Aug 2026, and it changes what "remaining" means for most of [§4](#4-every-open-gap).**

| Phase | What is in it |
|---|---|
| **Phase 1** | **Everything in [§4](#4-every-open-gap) — every open gap, including the minor ones and every row previously parked in `Recorded, unowned, and correct to leave`.** No gap is "correct to leave" any more on the grounds of size or priority alone |
| **Phase 2** | **WhatsApp messaging (P14)** — the six templates, both languages, and `dispatch-messages`, the fifth Cron Trigger. **Now [Plan.md](Plan.md)'s Wave 10**, added 16 Aug 2026. **Joined 17 Aug 2026 by four items deferred out of phase 1 by owner decision** — GAP-65 (the no-login share link), GAP-136 (UC-99's PDF half), GAP-135 (the weekly-settlement rhythm, guarded so it cannot misreport meanwhile) and P14 itself deferred entire, with **no Meta approvals fired**. Each carries its own reasoning in [§4](#4-every-open-gap); the pattern across all four was the same, and worth naming: **each had been sized as one item and turned out to be two**, only one half of which was actually blocked |
| **Phase 3** | **Offline and the PWA (B7)** — mutation-queue replay, eviction warning, runtime caching. **Now [Plan.md](Plan.md)'s Wave 11**, added 16 Aug 2026 |

**Neither phase had a plan until 16 August 2026, and that is worth recording rather than quietly fixing.** [Plan.md](Plan.md)'s ten-wave plan ended at Wave 9 · Release — a `grep` for `P14`, `WhatsApp` or `B7` across its live section returned **zero hits**. Every mention of either phase sat in the historical block that file marks superseded and unedited, including B7's full specification, which was the only one phase 3 had. So the document CLAUDE.md describes as "what remains" described nothing past phase 1, while phase 1 came down to five open gaps and four live-test items. Both now have waves. **P14's real content is still unsized** — see [Blocked](#blocked), which has been saying so, and naming the Asgardeo precedent for why the "external" label is what hides it.

**What this does and does not move.** It empties the `Recorded, unowned, and correct to leave` bucket as a *scheduling* category — those eighteen rows are now phase-1 work needing sizing and a slot, not decisions to leave them. It does **not** touch [`Not in this tracker`](#not-in-this-tracker): depreciation and disposal, driver retainers, loan and lease schedules, tax, offline photo capture and the desktop analytical dashboard are UC §9.1 / UI §15 **phase Third product scope**, never gap rows, and nothing here promotes them. If they are meant to move too, that is a `doc-change` to `use-cases.md` and a different conversation.

**Five phase-1 promotions contradicted their own owning document — resolved 11 Aug 2026, same day, by a `doc-change`.** CLAUDE.md is explicit — *"the owning document decides… or the document needs changing first, deliberately, with its reason recorded."* `use-cases.md` → v1.2.5 and `user-flows.md` → v1.1.6 both landed before any of these five were treated as buildable, per "documents travel together." **Checked precisely before scheduling, not just re-cited** — one entry that looked like it belonged here (GAP-68) turned out not to, and was removed rather than carried along by inertia:

| Gap | Wanted phase 1 | Owning document said, before the `doc-change` | Now says |
|---|---|---|---|
| **GAP-15**, **GAP-6** | phase 1 | `use-cases.md` §9.1's Second row named `write-offs (UC-90)` and `post-closure charges (UC-91)`; `user-flows.md` tagged `F-8.3`/`F-8.4` `*Phase:* 2` | Both First / `*Phase:* 1`, v1.2.5 / v1.1.6 |
| **GAP-18** (UC-99 half) | phase 1 | `use-cases.md` §9.1's Second row named `export (UC-99)`; `user-flows.md`'s `F-9.3` tagged `*Phase:* 2` | First / `*Phase:* 1` |
| **GAP-18** (UC-73 half) | phase 1 | **Not a wrong label — an omission.** `UC-73` ("How was the year") was in *neither* row of `use-cases.md` §9.1's table; only `user-flows.md`'s F-9.2 report catalogue phased it, `2` | Added to First; F-9.2's own Phase column now `1` |
| **GAP-19** | phase 1 | `use-cases.md` §9.1's Second row named `utilisation and per-km reporting (UC-79)`; `user-flows.md`'s F-9.2 table tagged it `2` | First / `1` |
| **GAP-73**, **GAP-98** (UC-77 half) | phase 1 | **Same omission as UC-73** — `UC-77` ("Goodwill given") was in neither row of `use-cases.md` §9.1's table; only `user-flows.md`'s F-9.2 phased it, `2` | Added to First; F-9.2's own Phase column now `1` |
| **GAP-98** (UC-78 half) | phase 1 | `use-cases.md` §9.1's Second row named `receivables ageing (UC-78)`; `user-flows.md`'s F-9.2 table tagged it `2` | First / `1` |

**GAP-68 checked and removed from this list, 11 Aug 2026 — it never belonged here.** `F-3.5` (`user-flows.md`, its own source flow) was already `*Phase:* 1`, and `UC-13` (its `use-cases.md` source) was never in the Second row either. GAP-68 is ordinary unbuilt feature work — the predictive maintenance *prompt*, not the recording action, which already ships — with no document to change first. Filed correctly as phase 1, startable, no `doc-change` needed, and never was.

**The `use-cases.md` edit, as landed, v1.2.4 → v1.2.5:** `UC-90`, `UC-91`, `UC-78`, `UC-79`, `UC-99` moved out of the Second row into First; `UC-73` and `UC-77` added to First (they were never phased in Second to move *out of*). "Notifications and the message log" and "side-by-side condition comparison" stay in Second, untouched — the first is WhatsApp, already phase 2 by the owner's own decision; the second still has no gap asking to promote it. **One edit covering all five gaps, the reason recorded once, in §9.1's own "moved into the first phase" running list** (the same place v1.2/v1.2.3's earlier phase moves live) rather than five separate edits that would have drifted against each other. `user-flows.md`'s matching tag changes landed the same commit: `F-8.3`, `F-8.4`, `F-9.3` and the four F-9.2 report rows. **All six previously-blocked gap rows below are now startable** — the other twelve of the eighteen promotions never had a document conflict and were startable from the moment they were promoted.

### The five rules that decided the order

Still binding on everything in [Plan.md](Plan.md):

1. **`packages/shared` is the root of the dependency graph.** A bug in it is structural, not local.
2. **The schema is the synchronisation point, and the main lever for parallelism.** Once a resource's Zod schema lands in `packages/shared`, the two tracks separate: the Worker builds against a real Neon branch, the client against mocks derived from *the same schema object*. Hand-mirrored types drift and what drifts is a money field. **This rule is what makes the plan's Track A / Track B split legal.**
3. **Auth blocks the client, not the Worker.** `tests/support/auth.ts` mints a user per W-49 role, so endpoints are tested before Asgardeo works.
4. **No cron is ever a prerequisite for a user action.**
5. **Reports come after the writes that feed them.**

### The golden fixtures

The regression suite. Any change that moves one is breaking and must fail loudly (FL §9.1).

| Fixture | Figure | Reproduces at | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **G-3** Mileage on an open-ended rental | **7,500** | P5 | ✅ end to end through real endpoints |
| **G-2** One accident | **15,000** | P8 | ✅ against the real period lifecycle |
| **G-1** One month of the bus | **134,000** | P9 | ✅ open July → write → close → open August |

| **Web-P6d** Odometer + closure | `GET /api/lease/{id}/deposit` (returns `null` when none was taken — that is not a W-56 gap) · `ReadOdometerSheet` · `CloseLeaseScreen`, an in-screen stepper on one route. **Back is removed once step 0 succeeds** — the lease is really `closing`, a posted fact, not a draft | 60 / 206; api 20/20 |
| **Web-P7** Trips | `tripResponseSchema` fixed — `closingDate`/`cancelReason`/`advanceDisposition` were fetched but never projected · `GET /api/trip/{id}/expense` · `BookTripScreen`, `TripDetailScreen`, `CloseTripSheet` (**INV-17 blocks with a `Dialog`**, its first real caller), `CancelTripSheet` (never blocks; a 409 reveals a Refund/Keep-as-fee chooser inline) · calendar tap-through, B/C half | 64 / 226; api 26/26 |
| **Web-P8a** Incidents | Three wire gaps closed: recoveries + claim now reach `GET /api/incident/{id}`; `GET /api/vehicle/{id}/incident`; `GET /api/incident/{id}/expense` · `IncidentScreen` as a **container, not a wizard** — every action stays available regardless of `status` · five single-purpose sheets · `VehicleOverviewScreen`'s one app-bar action became a menu | 71 / 254; api 18/18, 27/27 |
| **Web-P8b** Costs + quick-add | **`GET /api/expense`** — every filter optional, newest first, voided rows included; no 404 for a filter naming another business's row, since the `businessId` AND already excludes it · `RecordExpenseSheet` + `FuelFillSheet` — **`BorneByPaidBy`'s first real caller** · `QuickAddSheet` on `AppShell`'s `＋`, shipping 3 of M-4's 5 actions — **that shortfall carried no gap id until GAP-82 (8 Aug), which is why nothing ever scheduled the other two**. **`borne_by` is never recomputed client-side** — omitted from the request unless explicitly overridden to "Us", so the server's §6.7 matrix always wins. **No `ExpenseListScreen`**, correcting the plan's own guess: §3.3's route map has no business-wide costs route, and `docs/` outranks the plan | 74 / 264; api 20/20 |

**Web-P8b's own open gaps** — GAP-29 (`GET /api/advance` skipped for want of a caller), GAP-30 (`expense.odometer_reading_id` never wired), GAP-31 (no business-member list, so paid-by has one option), GAP-32 (borne-by overrides only to "Us"), GAP-33 (`GET /api/expense` has no caller), GAP-34 (fuel fill's U-3 prefill degrades to the first vehicle).

---

## 4. Every open gap

One table, because scattering these is what let GAP-22 sit unowned through four phases. **Track** resolves in [Plan.md](Plan.md); `—` means nothing schedules it yet.

**The Track column was repointed at the waves on 14 August 2026, and the reason it needed repointing is worth keeping.** Twelve rows still named the pre-wave vocabulary — seven said `B9`, one said `19a`, three said "needs a Plan.md slot", one said "rides with A7" — all of it written before the 12 Aug replan, which retired that scheduling and put every one of these into a wave. Plan.md had them sequenced the whole time; this column did not say so, while its own header promised the two resolve against each other. **Found by being asked a question this table should have answered on its own** — "where is the UI work?" — and the honest answer was that about a third of it was scheduled somewhere this table would not lead you to. That is the failure mode the header's own GAP-22 sentence is about, so the lesson is recorded here rather than only fixed. Nothing moved wave as a result; twelve rows started naming the wave they were already in.

**What is actually open — added 16 August 2026, because this section could no longer answer its own question.** [Full accounts](#full-accounts--open-and-closed) below holds both open and closed rows, while [Closed](#closed) compacts many closed gaps to one line each. The rows were never wrong; they were never relocated either, because a compact closed line and a full account serve different jobs. So the subsection named for what is *scheduled* had become mostly finished work, and "what is left?" took a row-by-row read to answer. **It does not any more, and the subsection has since been renamed to say what it actually holds** — the note below it conceded the name was wrong and left it standing; renaming costs nothing and removes the last reason to read the table row by row:

| Genuinely open | What it is | Track |
|---|---|---|
| **GAP-135** | `effective_due_on` is never derived from `settlement_rhythm` — **no longer latent: the write path refuses instead of misreporting** (17 Aug 2026). Still unbuilt as a feature | **phase 2** — reclassified 17 Aug 2026 from verification debt to the feature it always was |
| **GAP-136** | UC-99's **PDF half**, split out of GAP-18 on 17 Aug 2026 — no PDF renderer has a home in this runtime (`TS §8`) | **phase 2** — deferred by decision, not blocked |
| **GAP-65** | the signed share link (A14) — the first route outside the login, carrying a full financial position. **Its 22 Aug "false premise" flag is resolved, not still open**: GAP-170 shipped 27 Aug 2026 (PR #143), so an unlinked driver or customer again has a route to their own figures, which is the fact this deferral was originally reasoned on | **phase 2** — deferred out of phase 1 by decision, 17 Aug 2026, **re-review concluded 27 Aug 2026: deferral is sound again** |
| ~~**GAP-174**~~ | **Withdrawn 23 Aug 2026, the same day it was filed — B1 and B9 are both non-bugs, and three verification passes missed it.** `insurance_claim` has **no `belongs_to_period_id` column and never has**: its `0001` DDL carries a deliberate two-period design (`posted_period_id` + `received_period_id`) with its reason in the comment, while `incident_recovery` carries all three. So `submitInsuranceClaim` setting the field on the recovery and not on the claim is **correct code following the schema**, not an asymmetry — and `received_period_id` *is* written, by `settleInsuranceClaim`. B9 likewise: `opening-balance.ts:104` and `:295` both already call `resolvePeriodLinkage` and spread `belongsToPeriodId`; its `PeriodClosedError` fires only when a business has **no open period at all**. **The external report asserted the column exists, the follow-up "verified" B1 by confirming the spread was missing — true and irrelevant — and the plan repeated it.** Every check was real and each confirmed the symptom without testing the premise: the same failure GAP-173's own first verdict was overturned for, committed twice more on the way to fixing it | **closed — filed in error**, no work; Step 1 is GAP-173 alone |
| **GAP-176** | **`new Date(\`${date}T00:00:00\`)` without the trailing `Z` parses at device-local midnight — 15 files / 18 sites in production plus 2 in `HomeScreen.test.tsx`.** Off by one calendar day for five and a half hours a day in Colombo. `formatShortDate.ts:16` carries the correct pattern. Grep by pattern, not by file — `VehicleCalendarScreen.tsx` already uses the correct form once alongside two bare ones (evaluation B3) | **phase 1** — Step 3 |
| **GAP-177** | **Zod money-input schemas accept zero on `expense.amountMinor`, `arrangement.rentAmountMinor` and `incident.agreedAmountMinor`**, so a goodwill charter can be recorded as `0` instead of at full price with a waiver — bypassing the UC-77 "goodwill given" total the feature exists to produce. **DB `CHECK` stays `>= 0`**: accumulator columns legitimately start at zero (evaluation B21) | **phase 1** — Step 4 |
| **GAP-178** | **Eight concurrency and tenancy defects, and the tenancy half is not a race at all: `updateObligationSettled` (`obligation.ts:392`), `applyAdjustmentToObligation` (`:246`) and `reverseAdjustmentOnObligation` (`:376`) all `UPDATE … WHERE id = ?` with no `business_id` predicate**, so a wrong id mutates another tenant's books silently. Plus deposit double-draw (no predicate locking under `READ COMMITTED` — the lock must be on the parent `deposit`), write-off void TOCTOU, party-archive TOCTOU, linkage resolved outside its transaction across five sites, and no unique constraint on `incident_recovery (incident_id, source)` so concurrent claim submits silently duplicate. **The go-live blocker of this set** (evaluation B5, B10–B15, B17–B20) | **phase 1** — Step 5, carries migration `0031` |
| **GAP-184** | **The `docs/` change promoting vehicle loans out of UC §9.1 phase Third, and adding the distributable-cash report** — including UC-03's owners-only capital row being stated as covering **partner-level** facts, so a business liability and a cash figure sit with a manager's working set, with its reason recorded. **The critical path for every feature below it**, and it requires no code | **phase 1** — Step 11, the critical path for GAP-185/GAP-186; no code |
| **GAP-185** | **Vehicle loans — new `vehicle_loan` and `loan_payment` tables, `'finance'` expense category, `'loan_on_behalf'` payout kind, `manageVehicleLoans` capability.** Flat-only amortisation, proportional split, final-payment true-up, settle-and-close, cumulative arrears. **`loan_payment` must set `belongs_to_period_id` itself** — it does not inherit the late-fact flag for free. Design in `PENDING-FEATURES-DESIGN-2026-08-23.md`, corrections in the combined plan | closed 25 Aug 2026 — **Step 12**: migration `0032` (drift-checked clean — both `loan_payment_period_open`/`loan_payment_audit` triggers written explicitly), `domain/vehicle-loan.ts`, `POST /api/vehicle-loan` + `.../payment` + `.../settle` + `.../payment/{id}/void` + `GET /api/vehicle/{id}/loan`. **One correction to the design's own DDL, found implementing it**: `loan_payment` needed `expense_id`/`partner_payout_id` link columns for void-cascade (INV-43) — absent from `data-model.md` §4.4's table, not caught until a void needed to find what to void with it. **One real bug caught in self-review before it shipped**: a named `liability_owner`'s payment first wrote only the finance-split share as the `loan_on_behalf` payout, when the design is explicit the *whole* payment is the drawing — fixed, and the test that would have passed either way was corrected too. **Two more real findings from Gitar's review of PR #130**: (1) `recordLoanPayment`/`settleVehicleLoan`'s overpayment check had no row lock — two concurrent payments against the same loan could each read the same stale remaining-to-pay and together overpay it, the identical shape GAP-178's deposit double-draw fix already closed elsewhere; fixed with `findVehicleLoanForBusiness(tx, …, true)` locking the loan row (`FOR UPDATE OF vehicle_loan`, not the joined `vehicle` row) inside the transaction, proven with a two-real-connections concurrency test run against the unfixed code first and observed failing, the same discipline `gap-178-concurrency.test.ts` set. (2) `voidLoanPaymentHandler` ignored its own `id` (loan) path param, voiding a payment by `paymentId` alone — a payment that exists under a *different* loan is now checked and refused as 404, the same "cross-tenant is 404" reasoning one level down from `business_id` to `loan_id`. A third Gitar finding (settlement's `waivedMinor` reusing the split ratio for a `liability_owner` loan) was verified against the design and is **not a bug** — the ratio is a property of the loan agreement, independent of who pays it down — recorded as a comment rather than changed. **A fourth real finding, from Copilot's own review of the same PR**: `settleVehicleLoanRequestSchema` allows `settlementAmountMinor: 0` (a full-forgiveness settlement — the lender walks away from the whole remaining balance, F-12.3's own "settlement < principal outstanding" case taken to its limit), but `loan_payment.amount_minor CHECK (amount_minor > 0)` rejected the closing row outright — a real settlement crashed with a 500 instead of closing the loan, confirmed live (reverted the constraint, watched the same test go 201→500, restored it). Widened to `CHECK (amount_minor > 0 OR is_settlement = true)` — an ordinary payment of nothing still isn't a payment (GAP-177's own reasoning), only a settlement may be zero. Backend only, matching this row's own scope — no client screen; API responses proven with 13 integration tests (11 in `vehicle-loan.test.ts`, 1 dedicated concurrency test, plus the access-boundary suite) and the golden fixtures (39/39, 134,000/15,000/7,500 unmoved) |
| **GAP-186** | **Distributable cash — `cash − deposits held − loan instalments due`, reaching overdue plus the next falling due.** Everything else in UC-67 distribution already exists and needs verifying, not building. Visible to `viewReports`, manager included; recording a `partner_payout` stays owners-only. **Degrades to "not available", never zero** — the most expensive wrong number in the plan, because someone acts on it by moving money | closed 25 Aug 2026 — **Step 13**: `domain/reports.ts`'s `getDistributableCashReport` composes the existing cash-position sum, `sumDepositsHeld` and GAP-185's own `sumInstalmentsDueForBusiness` (never re-derived); `GET /api/reports/distributable-cash`, `viewReports` (manager included, W-70). **`loanInstalmentsDueMinor`/`distributableMinor` degrade together to `null`** — a judgement call recorded here since the design left the exact trigger unspecified: any open loan with no monthly instalment figure makes the whole report's headline unsafe to show, not just that one loan's contribution, since W-56 calls a partial read here "the single most expensive wrong number... because someone acts on it by moving money." Client: `DistributableCashReportScreen`, a twelfth catalogue card ("What can we safely take out"), `NotAvailable` rendered per-tile on the degraded case rather than a fabricated 0. 3 new integration tests + 3 new component tests; full suite 49 files/837 tests and 148 files/894 tests; golden fixtures 39/39 unmoved. **One real bug from PR #131's own review (Claude)**: `cashOnHandMinor` reused `getCashPositionReport`'s payment-only total, which never includes deposit cash (deposits write only `deposit`/`deposit_movement` rows, never `payment` — data-model.md §15), so subtracting `depositsHeldMinor` from it netted a liability the figure never held as an asset — understating `distributableMinor` by the full deposit balance whenever one was held (the PR's own test: business holds 58,000 cash, owes 8,000 back, should read 50,000 distributable; read 42,000). Fixed by folding `depositsHeldMinor` into `cashOnHandMinor` before subtracting it back out — a no-op for `distributableMinor`'s arithmetic, but it corrects the "Cash on hand and in bank" tile itself, which UC-109 names as the true physical total and which the screen shows on its own. Two more from Gitar/Copilot's own review: `sumInstalmentsDueForBusiness` looped `await` sequentially (N+1 round-trips) and always queried a loan's payments even when its raw `monthlyPaymentMinor` was already known `null` (the exact degrade trigger) — fixed by short-circuiting on that pre-check before any query, then parallelising the rest with `Promise.all` |
| **GAP-187** | **`deposit` and `opening_balance_entry` name a party but carry no `posted_period_id`, so migration `0031`'s archive guard does not reach them.** For `deposit` that is a real if narrow hole: the parent row is a container and the money lives in `deposit_movement`, which names the deposit rather than the party — so taking a **new** deposit from an archived driver is still possible. W-60 makes it unlikely (a party cannot be archived while holding one) but not impossible. **Filed rather than widened**: dropping the `posted_period_id` half of the membership test pulls in `lease`, `daily_lease`, `driver_link_invite` and `message`, which are arrangements, identity and correspondence rather than money and want a different answer. **`expense` was in this row until Gitar's review of PR #117** — it carries `posted_period_id` and names its party `borne_by_driver_id`, and the guard's first draft matched four hard-coded column names rather than the foreign keys, so it missed the column and the drift check called it clean. The membership test now derives party columns from FKs to `driver`/`customer`, and asserts per column rather than per table | **phase 2** — found while building GAP-178 PR A, 24 Aug 2026; not a Wave 9 blocker |
| **GAP-188** | **F-8.1's aggregate half of the late-fact flag: a report for the open month still blends a late prior-period item into its total with no flag.** GAP-173 closed the record-level half — the export carries a "Belongs to" column, and `IncidentScreen`/`DriverActivitySections` render the period inline (INV-10). **This half is not the same shape and is deliberately not a threading job**: `vehicleMonthRowSchema` returns per-vehicle **sums** (`earnedMinor`/`costsMinor`/`profitMinor`) with no line items, so there is no affected line to flag — passing `belongsToPeriodId` into the query changes nothing a reader can see. **Design settled 26 Aug 2026** (owner decision, `doc-change`): the month report grows real lines — a late `obligation`/`trip`/`expense` row (`belongs_to_period_id` set) is returned as its own dated, labelled item beside the existing sums, which stay unchanged. The alternative (a single *of which, belongs elsewhere* subtotal) was not taken: F-8.1's Accept clause already promises the item itself shows inline, not just an aggregate hint that something moved, and this keeps the aggregate report consistent with how every other late-fact surface (the CSV export, `IncidentScreen`, `DriverActivitySections`) already shows it. Scoped to UC-70 alone — UC-73's year window has no single "this period" for lateness to be measured against, so it is out of scope rather than silently widened. `user-flows.md` v1.1.18, `data-model.md` v1.1.15 | **phase 1** — Step 16, unblocked for code 26 Aug 2026 |
| **GAP-191** | **Voiding an incident's repair-cost expense doesn't refresh the incident's own bottom line — "Total repairs"/"Net cost to you" stay at the pre-void amount for the rest of the session, until the page is reloaded.** Found 28 Aug 2026 live-verifying GAP-172 (`docs/qa/live-test-plan.md` LT-15, `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-28.md`): recorded a real Rs 500 "Repairs" expense via the incident's own new "Record repair cost" action, both bottom-line figures correctly moved from Rs 0 to Rs 500 (`POST /api/expense` 201), then voided it — the expense list correctly struck the row through with its reason, but "Total repairs"/"Net cost to you" held at Rs 500 in the same client session; a hard reload (fresh `GET /api/incident/:id`) then read Rs 0 correctly, proving the underlying data was always right and this is a stale-client-cache bug, not a server miscalculation. **Root cause, confirmed by source**: `IncidentScreen.tsx:392`'s repair-cost void action passes `invalidateKeys={[["incident", incidentId, "expense"]]}` only — the incident *detail* query (`["incident", incidentId]`, which carries the server-computed `bottomLine.totalRepairCostMinor`/`netCostMinor`) is invalidated elsewhere in this screen only by the close-incident mutation (`IncidentScreen.tsx:159`), never by voiding a cost. `TripDetailScreen`'s equivalent "Costs so far" doesn't share this bug, but not because of a parallel fix — it's computed client-side by summing the trip's own expense list (`TripDetailScreen.tsx:138`), which the expense-list invalidation already reaches; live-confirmed correct (reverted to Rs 0 immediately on void, no reload needed) the same session. **Why this was invisible until today**: GAP-172 (25 Aug 2026) is the first time a user could ever attach a real cost to an incident from the client at all, so a void of one had no visible bottom-line surface to go stale on before it shipped. A confident, plausible, wrong "Net cost to you" is exactly the W-56 class of defect | **phase 1** — found live-verifying GAP-172, unscheduled |
| **GAP-189** | **A live Neon Postgres password sat hardcoded in `docs/engineering/fixtures/golden.py`/`reports.py` since this repo's early history, on both `main` and `develop`.** Found 25 Aug 2026 evaluating why PR #129 (`develop`→`main`, the release PR) fails SonarCloud's Security/Reliability Rating on New Code — none of it from recent feature PRs, all pre-existing code landing on `main` for the first time. **Confirmed to be the production credential, not a disposable one**: it matches `DATABASE_URL`'s own password (the `main` branch), checked by comparing parsed connection-string passwords rather than by connecting to production with the leaked one. **Root cause is the guard's own blind spot**: `check-forbidden.mjs`'s `NEVER_SCAN` exempts all of `docs/` wholesale, written for spec prose that legitimately quotes forbidden patterns as examples — but these fixture scripts are real executable Python, so the leak was invisible to the repo's own security tooling the whole time, and a second gap sat beside it: the existing `secret/connection-string` rule only matches a `postgres://user:pass@host` literal, never `password="..."` passed as pg8000's own separate keyword argument. Both fixed: secret rules now run against `docs/` regardless of the exemption (every other rule stays scoped, so the spec corpus's own example fragments don't become false positives), and a new `secret/bare-password-literal` rule catches the exact shape that leaked — verified by temporarily reintroducing the original literal, confirming it was caught, then reverting. Both scripts now read the password from `PGPASSWORD` (the standard libpq env var); the SSL context is also built explicitly (`PROTOCOL_TLS_CLIENT`, pinned `minimum_version`, explicit cert verification) so Sonar's S4423/S4830 pass on more than an inferred default. **The leaked password itself is not rotated by this fix** — that is a live-infrastructure action needing coordination with the Cloudflare Worker's `DATABASE_URL` secret (production and QA) so nothing breaks mid-rotation, left for the owner to schedule. One smaller CRITICAL finding from the same Sonar sweep fixed alongside: `StartDailyLeaseScreen.tsx`'s `patternWeekdays.sort()` used lexicographic string order instead of numeric (currently benign — weekday indices are always single digits — fixed to `(a, b) => a - b` anyway). **The other CRITICAL finding — two `bigint`-inference false positives in `accounting-period.test.ts`, already verified and documented 24 Aug 2026 — was not touched, and a first attempt to mark them `NOSONAR` was reverted**: the marker did not suppress the issue (Sonar likely disables inline suppression at the project level, a defensible governance choice for a repo that already asks every other check for a visible reason rather than a silent one) and, worse, touching those two previously-untouched lines pulled them into PR #133's own diff, re-triggering the identical known false positive against *this* PR instead of leaving it exactly where it already lived on `main`. Left alone once that was understood. **PR #133's own SonarCloud run then found a real issue of its own**: the two fixture scripts' near-identical new connection setup measured 54% duplication (>3% threshold) — factored into a shared `docs/engineering/fixtures/_connect.py`, both scripts re-verified against the live disposable branch afterward (39/39 golden fixtures unmoved) | PR #133 → `develop`, closed the code-level half 25 Aug 2026. **Password rotation closed 27 Aug 2026**: `neondb_owner` rotated on both Neon branches (`main` and `qa`) via `ALTER ROLE … PASSWORD`, the Cloudflare Worker `DATABASE_URL` secret updated for both `fleetsettle-api` and `fleetsettle-api-qa`, and the matching GitHub Actions environment secrets (`production`, `qa`) updated the same way — `qa` rotated too though only `main`'s password was ever confirmed to match the leaked one, since `qa` was branched from `main` on 5 Aug, after the leak was already in place, and so plausibly inherited it. Both `/api/health` and `/api/ready` verified 200 on `fleetsettle.com` and `qa.fleetsettle.com` immediately after, confirming no gap between rotation and secret update. The old leaked password is dead everywhere it could have reached |

| **GAP-196** | **A trip detail read can mistake its driver-fee payable for the customer receivable and offer to collect it from the customer.** Confirmed live 28 Aug 2026 with synthetic trip `01a0474c-8e4f-760d-8d52-f3b351d4f17b`: the trip raised two live obligations with the same `(source_type, source_id)` — `trip_fare`, `owed_to_us`, Rs 123.45 and `driver_fee`, `owed_by_us`, Rs 25. After the fare was fully paid but before the fee was paid, Trip detail rendered `Due Rs 25` in its Received row and opened `Collect payment`. The ledger itself was correct; the read chose the wrong row. Root cause was `findObligationBySource` filtering source only and returning one arbitrary row, while `getTripHandler` labelled that row `receivable` without constraining direction/kind | **closed 28 Aug 2026** — PR #150: `findObligationBySource` (`api/src/queries/obligation.ts`) now requires `kind`/`direction` as arguments, not optional narrowing — every one of its 8 call sites (`confirmDay.ts` ×3, `mileage.ts`, `lease-closure.ts`, `incident.ts`, `billing-period.ts`, the `day-record`/`trip` handlers) had to name what it actually wants, the same "compiler enumerates every call site" discipline GAP-178 used for `businessId`. New regression test (`trip.test.ts`) proven against the unfixed code first (fails, returns the pending `driver_fee` row instead of the paid `trip_fare` one) before the fix. **Two things this PR's own CI found that local testing didn't**: `incident.test.ts`'s "credit_days" test 404'd because its synthetic fixture created a `billing_period` obligation without `kind: "rent"`, silently inheriting the factory's generic `"other"` default — a real billing-period obligation is always `rent`, so the new constraint correctly refused an unrealistic fixture shape; fixed the fixture, not the check. SonarCloud flagged 23% new-code duplication from repeating the same `(kind, direction)` pair across the `day_record`/`billing_period` call sites — genuine, not just a metric, so `findDayRecordObligation`/`findBillingPeriodRentObligation` (PR #150's follow-up commit) named each source's fixed shape once rather than seven repeats of it |
| **GAP-197** | **Driver statement days have no guaranteed chronological order.** Confirmed live 28 Aug 2026 on Sunil's 29 Jul–28 Aug statement: 24–28 Aug rendered first, then 10–21 Aug, then 23 Aug before 22 Aug. `listDayRecordsForDriver` (`api/src/queries/day-record.ts`) had no `ORDER BY business_date`; `DriverActivitySections` mapped the wire order unchanged | **closed 28 Aug 2026** — PR #151: `.orderBy(desc(dayRecord.businessDate))` added to `listDayRecordsForDriver`, newest-first, matching the advances/deposits/offsets sections rendered beside it in the same component and the printed statement (`DriverStatementScreen`) that reads the identical query. Fixed once, server-side, rather than sorted again on the client — "two implementations of one rule diverge." New integration test inserts three days out of date order on purpose and asserts the response comes back newest-first |
| **GAP-198** | **Utilisation's revenue-per-available-day truncates money instead of half-up rounding it.** Confirmed live 28 Aug 2026 for `NC-1234`, 30 May–28 Aug: Rs 33,000 earned / 90 available days displayed Rs 366.66; the required half-up result is Rs 366.67. `api/src/domain/reports.ts` used plain BigInt `/`, which truncates the repeating minor-unit quotient | **closed 28 Aug 2026** — PR #151, same commit as GAP-197: new `divideHalfUp(dividend: bigint, divisor: bigint)` in `packages/shared/src/money.ts` — a division, not a split (`split()` already answers a different question, "how do N weighted shares add back to a whole exactly," via largest-remainder). Symmetric rounding, plain `bigint` in/out rather than the wire-facing `Minor` brand, since this domain-layer figure was never threaded through it. `reports.ts:694` now calls it; new test picks 100,000/7 = 14,285.71… specifically because it's unambiguous under truncation vs half-up, unlike the suite's existing 35,000/14 = 2,500-exact case, which would pass either way |
| **GAP-199** | **GAP-171's closed sticky-action guarantee regresses on a tall mobile viewport: ordinary content is still painted beneath the CTA before any scroll.** Confirmed live 28 Aug 2026 at 384×824 on customer detail: `Show all (5)` measured y 723–767 and `Collect payment` y 680–736; their 13px visual intersection is enough that hit-testing at the top and centre of `Show all` resolves to `Collect payment`, only its lower sample reaches the intended button. The `pb-[88px]` added by GAP-171 in `Screen.tsx` does not prevent the sticky node from clamping over a child that initially lands in that zone; scrolling remains the workaround. **The same mechanism was independently observed the same day on GAP-170's statement screen and dismissed there as ordinary sticky-footer behaviour — that dismissal did not run `elementFromPoint` hit-testing and should be treated as too quick, not as a second confirmation the pattern is safe** | **closed 28 Aug 2026** — PR #153, filed and fixed the same day. **M-39** (`ui-ux-guidelines.md` v1.10): the primary action stops being `position: sticky` inside the scroll region and becomes an ordinary flex sibling below it (`shrink-0`, scroll region `flex-1`) — the root cause both GAP-171 and GAP-199 share is a sticky element sharing the scroll region's own coordinate system at all, so no amount of padding tuning closes it for every content shape. Supersedes, not patches: `pb-[88px]`/`scroll-pb-[88px]` both become unnecessary and are removed rather than kept alongside the new layout. `Screen.tsx` updated, `docs/README.md` and `web/CLAUDE.md` both carry the new rule. Renumbered from this row's own original filing as GAP-194 (see the note above this table) |
| **GAP-200** | **Opening balance's icon-only Remove action shrinks below the project's 44×44 floor when a party label is long.** Confirmed live 28 Aug 2026 at 360×640: `Remove QA2 Driver…` measured 35×44. `OpeningBalanceScreen.tsx` placed a nominally 44px icon `Button` in a flex row without `shrink-0`, so its sibling text consumed nine pixels of the target | **closed 28 Aug 2026** — PR #152: `shrink-0` added to the icon `Button`. New component test renders a deliberately long party label and asserts the button's own computed size never drops below 44×44 |
| **GAP-201** | **UC-109's distributable cash is unmoved by the exact action it exists to gate.** Found 30 Aug 2026 by two independent reviewers reading the same query from different directions: `listPartnerCashPositions` (`api/src/queries/reports.ts`) summed only `payment` rows with `direction = 'received'`, never `'paid'` (UC-50 "pay the driver" — cash a partner hands straight to a driver read as though he still held it), and `getDistributableCashReport` (`api/src/domain/reports.ts`) never referenced `partner_payout` at all — an owner could take a payout and be shown the same "safe to take out" figure immediately after. A third, smaller defect in the same query: it filtered `payment.status = 'active'`, so a `'corrected'` payment (§10.2 — `amount_minor` already holds the true remaining figure) dropped out entirely instead of counting at what's left | **closed 30 Aug 2026** — PR #156: `listPartnerCashPositions` gains a `direction = 'paid'` subtrahend and reads `status <> 'reversed'` instead of `= 'active'`; `domain/credit-forward.ts`'s own candidate query gets the identical status fix, so a corrected payment's surplus can still settle a later due. A new `sumPartnerPayoutsForBusiness` (all three `partner_payout` kinds — `payout`, `partner_settlement`, `loan_on_behalf` — are each real cash leaving the business for a partner) is subtracted in `getDistributableCashReport`'s `cashOnHandMinor`, **not** in `listPartnerCashPositions`'s own `heldMinor` — UC-67 states "Holding" and "Taken out" as two separate lines, and `partner-summary.test.ts`'s existing `holdingMinor: "20000"` assertion (unmoved by a payout + settlement recorded in the same test) already locks that in; confirmed passing unchanged rather than updated to match. `docs/engineering/data-model.md` §15's UC-75 SQL carries both new terms. 3 new integration tests; all pre-existing distributable-cash/cash-position/partner-summary assertions (including the golden fixture, 134,000 unmoved) pass unchanged, confirming the fix adds a term rather than moving one |
| **GAP-202** | **A correction to an incident recovery's received amount could reverse an unrelated payment, and a void raced against a receipt could discard money already received.** Two defects in `domain/incident.ts`, found 30 Aug 2026 building out the rest of the backend accuracy review's fix plan. First: `recordRecoveryReceived`'s reverse-then-repost correction (GAP-12/W-61) walked every live `payment_allocation` on the obligation and called `markPaymentReversed` on **each allocation's parent payment** — but a customer carrying unapplied credit-forward (GAP-5b) can have this obligation's allocation drawn from an older, otherwise-unrelated surplus payment that is still funding a live allocation against a *different* obligation entirely; reversing the whole payment marked it `'reversed'` everywhere, not just here, and (after GAP-201/PR-1's fix) silently excluded it from every future credit-forward draw. Second (NM-4): `voidIncidentRecovery` read the recovery row and checked `receivedAmountMinor > 0` **before** opening its transaction — a concurrent `recordRecoveryReceived` landing in the gap could commit a real receipt against a recovery the void then discarded anyway, based on a stale "nothing received yet" snapshot, the identical class `write-off.ts`'s `voidWriteOff` (GAP-190/B12) and `obligation.ts`'s `voidObligation` (GAP-178/B12) were already fixed for | **closed 30 Aug 2026** — PR #157: the correction loop now voids or reduces the allocation only, mirroring `adjustment.ts`'s `unwindObligationAllocations` shape, and never touches the parent payment's own status. `voidIncidentRecovery`'s read and both its checks now happen inside the transaction, against a `findIncidentRecoveryForBusiness(tx, …, true)` `FOR UPDATE` row, the same "lock the parent" shape `voidWriteOff` uses. **Completing H-4**: migration `0035` adds a nullable `incident_recovery.payment_id`, mirroring `write_off_recovery.payment_id` — `recordRecoveryReceived` now records the specific payment it itself mints for provenance (null for an insurer-sourced row, or a receipt settled entirely by credit-forward), though the correction's own unwind still sweeps every live allocation on the obligation regardless of which payment minted it, since a shared credit-forward payment funding it must be unwound too. `docs/engineering/data-model.md` §15's `incident_recovery` DDL carries the new column — **folded into the same version-bump entry is GAP-201/PR #156's own DM §15 SQL change, which shipped 30 Aug 2026 without the version bump `docs.md`'s own convention requires**, caught revisiting the same section. New HTTP-level regression test (`incident.test.ts`) proves the credit-forward isolation by consequence — a second, unrelated contribution still settles from the older payment's remaining credit after the correction, which only holds if that payment was never wrongly reversed — and a new two-real-connections concurrency test (`gap-202-incident-recovery-concurrency.test.ts`, the same discipline `gap-178-concurrency.test.ts` set) races the void against the receipt and asserts the recovery is never left both voided and carrying a real receipt. **One test-factory gap found and fixed alongside**: `trackCreatedPayment`'s teardown could trip the new FK if `incident_recovery.payment_id` still named the payment being deleted — fixed once, in the shared helper, rather than left for every future test combining the two to rediscover. Full incident/reports/concurrency suites pass unchanged, golden fixture (G-2, 95,000/80,000/15,000) unmoved |
| **GAP-203** | **A partial write-off silently discarded its own remainder, and a second write-off against the same obligation was accepted on top of it.** `recordWriteOff` (`api/src/domain/write-off.ts`) flipped `obligation.status` straight to `'written_off'` unconditionally, regardless of the amount given — a manager entering 30,000 against a 70,000 receivable "cleared" the whole thing, with no row anywhere recording that 40,000 was never actually forgiven. The client's own `WriteOffObligationSheet` already lets the amount be edited below the full outstanding figure, so this was live-reachable, not theoretical. A second, smaller gap in the same handler: it checked that `obligationId`/`partyCustomerId`/`partyDriverId` each exist in this business, never that they name the *same* party — an id typo could write off a different customer's balance | **closed 30 Aug 2026** — PR #158, D2 (decided the same day: partial write-off is a real business act, "he'll never pay the last bit"). Migration `0036` adds `obligation.written_off_minor`, mirroring `waived_minor` exactly — a running total across possibly several write-offs, never a delta, never a flag — and widens the sum `CHECK` to `settled_minor + waived_minor + written_off_minor <= amount_minor`. `computeObligationStatus` (`domain/obligation-status.ts`) takes it as a fourth, default-0 argument; reaching the full amount reads as `'written_off'` whenever a write-off contributed, the same precedence the old unconditional flip already gave it. **The real scope of this fix turned out to be every place that computes "what remains outstanding"**, not just the write-off path itself — a written-off portion is never collectible, so it had to be subtracted everywhere `waived_minor` already is: both `findOutstandingObligationsForDriver`/`ForParty` (`queries/obligation.ts`, which `payment.ts` and `offset.ts` both allocate against), `findObligationForDepositApply` (`deposit.ts`), `sumOutstandingByDirectionForDriver`, `queries/reports.ts`'s receivables and ageing (§15, both the Drizzle query and the raw-SQL bucket query), `domain/party-archive.ts`'s open-money check, and `domain/lease-closure.ts`'s unpaid summary and closing deposit-application sweep — 7 arithmetic sites the compiler could not catch, found by grep rather than by type error, since TypeScript has no way to know an object's extra field went unused. Every `computeObligationStatus` call site across the codebase was re-audited the same way; the ones left at 3 arguments (`credit-forward.ts`, `mileage.ts`, `confirmDay.ts`, `offset.ts`'s `deductFromDriverFeeTx`, `trip.ts`'s own fare posting) all settle a **freshly-created** obligation that cannot yet carry a write-off, confirmed by tracing each one rather than assumed. `voidWriteOff`'s own obligation-restore had the identical latent bug once write-offs could be partial: it recomputed status from the unchanged `written_off_minor` instead of subtracting this specific write-off's own share back out, which would have left the column permanently stuck after any partial write-off was voided — fixed alongside, before it could ship as its own defect. The party-mismatch validation reuses `findObligationForDepositApply` (already carries all four party columns) rather than adding a new query. **Left out of scope, recorded rather than silently skipped**: `voidObligation`'s direct-void path (`domain/obligation.ts`) still does not check for a live write-off before voiding the obligation underneath it — pre-existing since `write_off.obligation_id` was added, not made worse by this change, and outside the fix plan's stated scope for this PR. `docs/product/use-cases.md` UC-90 and `user-flows.md` F-8.3 both state the decision; `data-model.md` §10.1's DDL and §15's three verbatim queries (UC-74, UC-78, UC-56) carry the new term. 4 new integration tests (`write-off.test.ts`) — partial write-off leaves the remainder genuinely collectible (proved by a subsequent payment landing at exactly the true remaining figure, not the pre-write-off amount), two write-offs accumulate and can complete an obligation, the party-mismatch 400, and voiding a partial write-off restores only its own share. One test's own initial assertion was itself wrong and corrected before landing: a fully-cash-covered remainder after a partial write-off reads `'written_off'`, not `'paid'` — the same precedence a waiver already had, now confirmed to extend correctly rather than assumed. Full regression sweep — adjustment, payment, payment-correction, customer, driver-view, party-archive, lease-closure, post-closure-charge, reports (60/60, including receivables/ageing) — passes unchanged |
| **GAP-204** | **`recordOffRoad` had no guard against being called twice on the same incident.** `incident.rent_treatment` was written every time F-3.4 step 2 ran but never read back, so a second `credit_days` submission silently applied a **second** pro-rata adjustment against the same billing period's rent obligation, and a second `extend` pushed the lease's end date out **again** on top of the first — each re-submission (a double-click, a confused retry, a manager revising a figure without realising the first one had already landed) compounding rather than replacing | **closed 30 Aug 2026** — PR #159, D3 (decided the same day: refuse, require the prior treatment be voided first — W-61's "undo what you did, in the order you did it," and a refusal is far cheaper to get right than a reversal-and-reapply cascade across an adjustment and a lease extension). `recordOffRoad` now refuses with a new `OffRoadTreatmentAlreadyRecordedError` (409) whenever `existing.rentTreatment` is already `'credit_days'` or `'extend'` — `'continue'` sets nothing live and never blocks a real choice afterward, so only the two treatments with an actual financial or lease-date effect guard. **The message differs by treatment, because the undo path does**: `credit_days` points the manager at the existing `voidAdjustment` (F-2.4); `extend` cannot, because `lease_extension` carries no `voided_at` trio at all — checked directly against the schema rather than assumed, confirming GAP-190's W-50 void-cascade sweep never reached this table. Building that void path is out of scope here; refusing a second `extend` is still strictly better than one nobody can see was ever applied twice. `docs/product/user-flows.md` F-3.4 step 2 records the decision. 2 new integration tests (`incident.test.ts`) — repeated `credit_days` refused with the obligation credited once, not twice; repeated `extend` refused with the lease end date pushed once, not twice. **One SonarCloud finding from this same session's own earlier PR (#157) fixed in passing**: a `10.8%` new-code duplication gate failure from a fourth near-identical copy of the `outcome<T>` two-real-connections test helper (already duplicated in `gap-178-concurrency.test.ts` and `void-cascade.test.ts`) — extracted to `tests/support/concurrency.ts`; the two pre-existing copies elsewhere were left alone as out of scope. A second, smaller duplication (the `credit_days`/`extend` off-road fixture setup, repeated between each happy-path test and its new repeat-refusal sibling) was caught the same way before it could trip the gate a second time, factored into `setupCreditDaysIncident`/`setupExtendIncident` |
| **GAP-205** | **A late mileage reading refused every reading that came after it, even though the later reading never touched the period the first one closed.** `findBillingPeriodsInRange` (`api/src/queries/billing-period.ts`) required a period to be *fully contained* in `[previous.readOn, readOn]` — `period_start >= previous.readOn AND period_end <= readOn`. A reading landing even a day after its own period's end (an ordinary two-days-late submission, not a missed one) leaves `previous.readOn` sitting *inside* that period's range, so the *next* period's `period_start` — which precedes that late date — fails `period_start >= previous.readOn`, even though the next period itself was never assessed by anything. `periods.length === 0` then read as "no billing period covers this range yet" and refused a legitimate, on-time reading for a period nobody had touched | **closed 30 Aug 2026** — PR #160: the predicate is now an overlap, not full containment — `period_start <= readOn AND period_end > previous.readOn`. The lower bound is deliberately **strict** (`>`, not `>=`): billing periods are strictly adjacent with no gaps (`period_end` is always the day before the next `period_start`, migration-generated), and `previous.readOn` is always either the handover reading or exactly the `to_reading_id` date of whichever assessment closed everything up to it — so a period whose own end lands exactly on `previous.readOn` was the one that reading already closed, and a strict `>` is what keeps it from being swept into a *second* assessment. **A design question resolved by deriving it, not by guessing**: the original finding proposed also excluding periods "a live assessment already closed out" via a join against `mileage_assessment`/`mileage_assessment_split` — worked through by hand against the adjacency invariant above, and proven unnecessary: the strict lower bound alone provably excludes every already-closed period, so no second query was added. New test (`mileage-assessment.test.ts`) reproduces the exact failure — a two-days-late reading closing period 1 alone, then an on-time reading for period 2 that the old predicate refused outright — and separately proves period 1 is never re-swept into a third reading's own assessment (`combinedAllowanceKm` stays at period 3's own 3,100, not 2,800 + 3,100). G-3, the golden mileage fixture (7,500 combined-excess split 152/148), reproduces unmoved — none of its readings are late, so the bug this closes was never reachable through it. **Filed as a gap, not fixed here, per the fix plan's own scope**: F-2.3's "prior provisional assessments are reconciled, not rewritten" is unimplemented — `assessMileage` writes `status: "final"` unconditionally and nothing in `api/src` ever writes `"provisional"` or `"superseded"`; the enum makes it look built when it is not |
| **GAP-206** | **A vehicle re-split — an owner buying in, an existing owner's share reduced rather than removed — reached the generic handler as a raw 500 instead of the 400 UC-02 already promises for a bad split.** `setOwnershipShares` (`api/src/domain/partner.ts`) inserted the new set's rows without ever closing the prior open ones first. `queries/partner.ts`'s own `listOwnershipShares` docstring already stated why this was structural, not incidental: every row's range is `[effective_from, ∞)`, so a continuing owner's new row always shares `(vehicle_id, user_id)` with their own still-open old row, and `ownership_share_vehicle_id_user_id_daterange_excl` always fires. INV-16's own worked example — "last year's split changes when someone buys in" — was therefore never actually reachable without a 500, and the existing test coverage only ever exercised a fully disjoint owner set (never a repeated `user_id`), so nothing caught it | **closed 30 Aug 2026** — PR #161: `setOwnershipShares` now closes every currently-open share for the vehicle first — the `changeDailyLeaseRate`/`changeVehicleArrangement` shape every other date-ranged table in this schema already uses (`closeOpenOwnershipShares`, `queries/partner.ts`) — before inserting the new set. `effectiveFrom` must land strictly after whatever was open: closing at a date *before* the open row's own start would write an inverted range (`effective_to < effective_from`) the exclusion constraint has no opinion on, so it is checked explicitly and mapped to a clean 400 rather than left to write a nonsensical row. **The guard's own first draft was wrong, caught by its own new test**: it closed the prior row(s) first and checked `effectiveFrom` second, but `daterange(effective_from, effective_to, '[]')` throws its own raw Postgres error the moment the `UPDATE` itself would write `effective_to < effective_from` — the write fails before the application-level check ever runs, so the guard test got a 500 instead of the 400 it asserted. Fixed by reading the currently-open dates first (`findOpenOwnershipShareEffectiveFroms`) and only closing once the check has already passed. The exclusion violation itself — now reachable only as a genuine concurrent-write race, not the routine buy-in case — gets its own `OwnershipSharesOverlapError` (409, `OWNERSHIP_SHARES_OVERLAP`), the same precedent `DailyLeaseOverlapsError`/`VehicleUnavailabilityOverlapsError` already set for every other EXCLUDE-backed table. 2 new integration tests (`partner.test.ts`): a three-way re-split where two of three owners repeat with reduced shares (the case the old code could never survive), asserting the prior two rows close exactly the day before the new split starts and the new three rows land open; and the `effectiveFrom` guard's own 400. Full `partner.test.ts` suite passes unchanged |
| **GAP-207** | **Two integrity holes in one handler: `paidByUserId` was trusted from the request body outright, and every expense's audit trail recorded the wrong person as who entered it.** `handlers/expense.ts`'s `createExpenseHandler` never checked `body.paidByUserId` names an actual member of this business — the same class GAP-93 already closed for a payment's own partner party, never reaching this handler. Separately, `domain/expense.ts`'s `createExpense` wrote `created_by: input.paidByUserId` — so the routine, correct act of a manager entering "the owner covered this out of pocket" (W-48's `paid_by` half, a fact about whose cash it was) silently rewrote F-8.6's own audit fact ("who entered this record") to name the owner instead, even though the owner never touched the request | **closed 30 Aug 2026** — PR #162: `createExpenseHandler` now checks `body.paidByUserId` against `findBusinessMemberUserId` whenever it is given, the identical shape `recordPaymentHandler`'s own GAP-93 fix already uses, 404 for anyone not an active member of this business. `CreateExpenseInput` gains a separate `actorUserId` field — the real authenticated user, always `requireUserId(c)` — and `createExpense` now writes `created_by: input.actorUserId`, never `paidByUserId`; the two facts (who paid, who typed it in) are independent and were only ever collapsed by an accident of one field being reused for both. 2 new integration tests (`expense.test.ts`): an owner entering an expense with `paidByUserId` naming a different partner asserts the response's `paidByUserId` still names that partner while the DB row's `created_by` names the owner who actually submitted it; and the 404 for a `paidByUserId` naming someone outside this business. Full `expense.test.ts` suite passes unchanged |
| **GAP-208** | **`adjustment` and `deposit` both name a party and both move real money, but migration 0031's own archive guard structurally cannot see either.** `archive_guarded_party_column`'s membership test is two catalogue questions — carries `posted_period_id`, and has a direct foreign key to `driver`/`customer` — and each table fails one half for a different reason: `adjustment` reaches its party only through `obligation_id`, no direct FK at all; `deposit` carries `party_customer_id`/`party_driver_id` directly but is a container row with no `posted_period_id` of its own (the money lives in `deposit_movement`). Migration 0031's own trailing comment already named both gaps honestly rather than leaving them to be discovered, and filed the second as GAP-187 | **closed 30 Aug 2026** — PR #163, migration 0037. `adjustment` gets a dedicated `assert_adjustment_party_not_archived()` trigger that resolves the party through the `obligation_id` join instead of reading columns straight off `NEW` (which is all the existing generic function can do) — same archived check, same `replaces_id` exemption (W-50's replace half) every other guarded table already gets. `deposit` gets the *existing* `assert_party_not_archived()` attached by hand — it already does the right check for a table with direct party columns, it just needed the trigger the catalogue-driven view will never generate for it. **Deliberately outside `assert-no-archive-guard-drift.sql`'s own coverage**: both triggers guard tables the view structurally cannot see, for two different reasons; widening the drift check to assert these two by name would be exactly the hand-maintained list DM §13's own history (three tables silently missing from `assert_period_open()`) already showed goes stale. Verified instead by a direct integration test against each trigger, in the same file GAP-178/B13's own two archive-guard tests already live. **One capability bug found by the new adjustment test itself**: a manual waiver above the auto-waive threshold (0 by default, OQ-3) escalates to `writeOffOrWaiveAboveThreshold` (owners only) — the test's first draft used a `manager` token (matching this file's existing expense tests) and got a 403 instead of the archive guard's own 409, fixed by switching to an owner token. Regression: `adjustment.test.ts` (13/13) and `driver-money.test.ts` (26/26) both pass unchanged — every existing write through either table still succeeds against a live party |
| **GAP-209** | **A manager's "earned: management fee" figure disagreed with the vehicle-side cost it is supposed to mirror.** `sumManagementFeeAsOfDate`/`sumAllTimeEarnedForUser` (`api/src/queries/partner.ts`, `api/src/domain/partner.ts`) both replayed `management_fee_agreement.monthly_amount_minor` in memory, effective-dated as of a period's own **end** — but `generateManagementFeeObligationsTx` (`domain/management-fee.ts`, A10a), the only thing that ever writes a real `management_fee` obligation, decides which agreements count as of a period's **start**, and `sumVehicleCostsForPeriod` (`queries/reports.ts`, the vehicle-side cost this fee is supposed to be the mirror image of) reads only those real obligations. A manager added or removed mid-period could see a fee the vehicle side never subtracted at all — the fee was *earned* on the partner's own summary without ever being *costed* anywhere — and an obligation voided outright, or an agreement corrected after the period's obligation was already posted at the old amount, had no way to reach the replayed figure either way. `queries/partner.ts`'s own comment on the function had already named this "a real question for whoever revisits it," left open pending D1 (net-vs-gross basis) | **closed 31 Aug 2026** — PR #164, after D1. `sumManagementFeeAsOfDate` and the agreement-history replay inside `sumAllTimeEarnedForUser`'s loop are both gone, replaced by `sumManagementFeeObligationForPeriod`/`sumManagementFeeObligationsForPeriodsBulk` — a direct read of `obligation WHERE kind = 'management_fee' AND party_user_id = … AND posted_period_id = … AND voided_at IS NULL`, the identical record `sumVehicleCostsForPeriod` already trusts. The bulk variant follows `sumVehicleEarnedForPeriodsBulk`'s own "one query across every period, not one per period" shape (GAP-145). No migration: this is a read-side fix only, the write side (`generateManagementFeeObligationsTx`) was already correct. **One existing test exposed the real behaviour change**: `partner-summary.test.ts`'s happy-path test granted an agreement via the real endpoint and expected `managementFeeMinor: "15000"` in the same, already-open period — which the old replay showed immediately but the real obligation never will, since granting an agreement does not itself raise the period's obligation (only the cron or a period-close does). Fixed by having the test generate the obligation explicitly (`generateManagementFeeObligationsTx`), the same fact any real business would need a cron cycle or period close to produce before the fee is genuinely earned. Regression: `scheduled.test.ts`, `reports.test.ts`, `subrequest-ceiling.test.ts`, `partner.test.ts` (126/126) pass unchanged, including the Workers-Free subrequest-ceiling test on this exact endpoint |
| **GAP-210** | **Three read paths summed a deposit's own movements one query per deposit, in a loop — the exact N+1 shape GAP-145 already found and fixed elsewhere, at 79 subrequests against Workers Free's 50-subrequest ceiling.** `sumDepositsHeld` (`queries/reports.ts`, feeding both `getCashPositionReport` and `getDistributableCashReport`), `listDepositsDueForRelease` (`queries/home.ts`), and `listOpenMoneyForParty` (`domain/party-archive.ts`) each called `sumDepositMovements` once per open deposit rather than once per business — bounded today (one open deposit per driver/customer, per the comments each site carried), but a fan-out that grows with deposit count rather than staying flat, on exactly the shape that already broke a different endpoint once | **closed 31 Aug 2026** — PR #165. `sumDepositMovements` gains a bulk sibling, `sumDepositMovementsBulk` (`queries/driver-money.ts`) — one grouped query across every deposit id given, seeded with `0n` for each so a deposit with no live movements yet reads as a real zero (W-56), not a missing figure. All three call sites now call it once instead of looping; `sumDepositsHeld` and `listDepositsDueForRelease` fold the sign logic once returned, `listOpenMoneyForParty`'s own `Promise.all` fan-out (already parallel, but still one query per deposit) collapses to the same single call. No migration, no behaviour change — same totals, fewer round trips. Regression: `reports.test.ts`, `home.test.ts`, `party-archive.test.ts` (88/88) pass unchanged |
| **GAP-211** | **UC-70's vehicle profit report and UC-99's own year-end export were computed from different fact sets and could never reconcile.** `sumVehicleEarned*` read `obligation.amount_minor` gross, ignoring `waived_minor` entirely, and neither `sumVehicleEarned*` nor `sumVehicleCosts*` read `customer_contribution`, an insurer settlement, or a `write_off` — all three of which `listTransactionsForDateRange` (the export) already included. A written-off receivable looked fully collected in the month's own report and abandoned only in the year's CSV; a waived rent charge inflated the report's earned figure by exactly the amount the business chose never to collect | **closed 31 Aug 2026** — PR-12, after D1 (decided the same day: net, not gross — FL §5.73's own "the month shows the 340 charged and the 340 waived" already states the intent, and UC-99's export was already closer to it than the report was). `sumVehicleEarnedForPeriodBulk`/`ForPeriodsBulk`/`ForDateRange`/`ForDateRangeBulk` now read `SUM(amount_minor - waived_minor)` and gain two new sources — `customer_contribution` (folded into a new shared `EARNED_OBLIGATION_KINDS` constant) and the insurer settlement, read from `insurance_claim` joined through `incident` (which carries no `vehicle_id` of its own), recognised on `received_on`/`received_period_id` never on the date claimed (W-11). `sumVehicleCostsForPeriodBulk`/`ForPeriodsBulk`/`ForDateRangeBulk` gain a `write_off` source, dated by its own `written_off_on`/`posted_period_id` — never netted retroactively into the month the original charge was raised, the same "separate line" treatment the export already gave it. `listTransactionsForDateRange` now reads the identical `EARNED_OBLIGATION_KINDS`/`COST_OBLIGATION_KINDS` constants the report reads, so the two families cannot drift again on which kinds count as which. **The waiver half of the fix was redesigned once, before landing**: the first draft netted a waiver straight into the obligation's own export line (`amount_minor - waived_minor`, mirroring the report's own SQL) — reverted on rereading F-2.4's own acceptance criterion, "the month shows the 340 charged and the 340 waived," which requires *both* facts visible, not one silently reduced. A waiver now reaches the export as its own line (`listWaiverTransactionsForDateRange`, reading `adjustment` joined through `obligation` for `vehicle_id`, filtered to `adjustment_type IN ('waiver','auto_waiver')`, dated by the waiver's own `occurred_on`) — the identical "separate line, own date" shape `write_off` already had; the report's own `SUM(amount_minor - waived_minor)` and the export's "gross line plus a separate waiver line" net to the same total by construction, so nothing had to choose between the two facts. `sumVehicleEarnedForPeriodsBulk`/`sumVehicleCostsForPeriodsBulk` (the two-period variants feeding `sumAllTimeEarnedForUser`, partner.ts) got the identical fix in the same pass — leaving them gross while the single-period variants moved to net would have reintroduced the exact disagreement M-1 exists to close, one level removed. `sumVehicleEarnedForPeriod`/`sumVehicleCostsForPeriod`/`sumVehicleCostsForDateRange` (the singular, non-`Bulk` variants) were deliberately left untouched — confirmed to have zero call sites (GAP-179/B26's own finding, unchanged), the same "converting code that never runs buys nothing" reasoning that already kept them as documentation rather than optimising them for uniformity. `docs/engineering/data-model.md` §15 gains UC-70's earned query in full for the first time (never previously given its own SQL block) alongside the costs query's new `write_off` term; `docs/product/use-cases.md` UC-70 records D1. **Golden fixtures verified unmoved before writing a line of code, not after**: G-1 (`134,000`), G-2 (`95,000`/`80,000`/`15,000`) and G-3 (`7,500` split `152`/`148`) each traced by hand — G-1 carries no waiver and no incident; G-2/G-3 never call the vehicle-month/year report endpoint at all, only their own incident/mileage arithmetic. 2 new integration tests (`reports.test.ts`): a waiver reducing `earnedMinor` net while the export still shows both the charge and the waiver as separate lines; a customer contribution, an insurer settlement and a write-off all reaching the vehicle-month report the way they already reached the export. **One deliberate, documented threshold move, not a silent one**: GAP-145's own subrequest-ceiling regression guard (`subrequest-ceiling.test.ts`) rose from 37 to 40 — the insurer-settlement and `write_off` sources each cost one genuinely new round trip in both `getVehicleMonthReport`'s own period and `sumAllTimeEarnedForUser`'s all-time loop, four total in that test's six-period fixture. Chasing them out via a hand-rolled `UNION ALL` was considered and declined: 40 is still 10 short of the real Workers Free ceiling (50), and a raw-SQL join replacing four typed query-builder calls would trade four subrequests for a correctness risk not worth taking at that margin. Regression: `reports.test.ts`, `accounting-period.test.ts` (G-1), `incident.test.ts` (G-2), `mileage-assessment.test.ts` (G-3), `partner-summary.test.ts` all pass unchanged; `subrequest-ceiling.test.ts` passes at the new threshold |
| **GAP-212** | **Three same-day-tie-break gaps and three tenancy-defence-in-depth gaps, both classes matching a fix already made once elsewhere in this schema and left incomplete for its own siblings.** `findOutstandingObligationsForDriver`/`ForParty`/`findObligationsForLease` (M-6, `queries/obligation.ts`) ordered by `due_on` alone — a rent and a mileage excess both landing on the 12th, or several `daily_amount` rows from one confirm-all, could return in whatever order Postgres happened to pick, so *which* of a day's dues a partial payment cleared could disagree between the allocation preview and the write, or between two replays of the same figures. `queries/vehicle-loan.ts`'s two loan-payment listings (NL-3) had the identical gap, plus `listLoanPaymentsForLoan` (NL-2) was missing the `businessId` filter its own sibling three lines above already carries. `updateLeaseTerms`/`setVehicleServiceInterval`/`findCurrentVehicleArrangementRow`+`endVehicleArrangementRow`/`archiveDriverRow`+`unarchiveDriverRow`/`archiveCustomerRow`+`unarchiveCustomerRow` (L-2 residual) all wrote or read with no `business_id` predicate of their own — the exact shape PR #144 (Copilot review) already fixed for `setVehicleLifecycle`, left in place on every sibling that PR's own scope didn't reach | **closed 31 Aug 2026** — PR-13. `queries/obligation.ts`'s three finders and `queries/vehicle-loan.ts`'s two loan-payment listings now `.orderBy(dueOn/paidOn, id)` — `id` is UUIDv7, so this is insertion order, the identical precedent `findOwnershipSharesAsOfBulk` (queries/reports.ts, GAP-145) already set and stated the reasoning for by name: *"an unordered SELECT leaves that to whatever the query planner happens to pick."* Every write/read named above gains its own `businessId` parameter and `WHERE`/`JOIN` predicate — all six confirmed, not assumed, to be defence-in-depth only: every caller already resolves tenancy first (`findVehicleForBusiness`, `findLeaseForBusiness`, `findDriverForBusiness`/`findCustomerForBusiness` under a `FOR UPDATE` lock), so none of these six was a reachable cross-tenant gap. **`vehicle_arrangement` carries no `business_id` column of its own** — `findCurrentVehicleArrangementRow` scopes through an `innerJoin` to `vehicle` (a plain `SELECT` supports it); `endVehicleArrangementRow` scopes through a subquery against `vehicle` instead, since Postgres `UPDATE` has no native join clause. `changeVehicleArrangement`'s own `ChangeVehicleArrangementInput` gains `businessId` to thread it through — the input carried no tenancy fact of its own before this, not merely an unused one. No migration, no behaviour change — every one of the nine functions returns identically for every existing legitimate caller; only a cross-tenant id, never reachable through any existing route, now fails closed at the query layer too rather than only at the handler that already checked first. Regression: `party-archive.test.ts`, `vehicle.test.ts`, `vehicle-loan.test.ts`, `lease.test.ts`, `payment.test.ts`, `offset.test.ts`, `driver.test.ts`, `customer.test.ts` pass unchanged |

**Renamed from `Scheduled`, 17 August 2026.** The name was already conceded as wrong by the note that sat under it — *"the section named for what is scheduled had become [86%] finished work"* — and by the summary table above, both of which existed to work around it. Only **7 of these 49 rows are not closed**, so a reader taking the old heading at face value got the wrong answer about 42 of them. Renaming is the fix the two workarounds were substituting for; both stay, because the summary table is still the fastest way to answer "what is left" and this table is still the place a closed row's full account lives.

**Holds both open and closed rows — read the Track column, or [the summary table above](#4-every-open-gap) for what is genuinely open.** A closed row stays here rather than moving to [Closed](#closed) when its account is worth more in full than compacted to a line — that is this subsection's whole purpose, and it is why the rows were never relocated.
