| **GAP-126** | **`HomeScreen` can render "Nothing needs you today" during its own initial pending window, before any of its six queries has resolved — the exact class its own comment claims was already closed.** [:140](web/src/features/home/HomeScreen.tsx#L140)'s comment names the fix precisely: "`?? []` alone can't tell 'nothing yet' from 'never coming', which is exactly how this screen used to render 'Nothing needs you today' while the Worker was down." That sentence is about the *failed*-read case, and `anySectionErrored` (kind `=== "error"`) does cover it — but `anySectionHasContent` is built from the same six `?? []` reads with no `kind !== "ready"` gate anywhere, so a read that is merely *still in flight*, no different in observable shape from one that failed, produces the identical false-empty render. **The most visible instance of the whole class** — Home is the first screen every user sees, on every cold load, and GAP-125's own S0-1 confirmed the fetch-flight window is real and widens under throttling, not a jsdom artefact. Found triaging S0-3's guard-rule candidates, 14 Aug 2026, by reading the file rather than trusting either heuristic tried. **Built 15 Aug 2026, riding in early with the GAP-124b pass below** (this route was already being touched for that item, and the two fixes share the exact same six-query gate): `HomeScreen` now computes `allSectionsReady` (all six `useQueryState` reads `=== "ready"`) and gates "Nothing needs you today" on `!anySectionHasContent && !anySectionErrored && allSectionsReady`; the pending window in between renders a neutral loading line instead. The real-empty state also now carries the business date (`EmptyState`'s new `detail` prop), matching UI §7.1/§7.2's own "with the date" wording, which the pre-fix render had never actually done | closed 15 Aug 2026 — **Wave 7 · Client remainder** |
| **GAP-127** | **`VehicleCalendarScreen` renders every day as free — startable, bookable — during its own initial pending window, on the one screen whose job is stopping a double-booking.** [:131](web/src/features/vehicles/VehicleCalendarScreen.tsx#L131)'s own comment claims INV-1 coverage ("a failed calendar read must not render as every day free"), and the `daysState.kind === "error"` branch does deliver that — but `byDate` is built from `days ?? []` with no pending gate, so `byDate.get(cell.date)` returns `undefined` for every date until the fetch resolves, which is indistinguishable in this code from a date genuinely having no occupancy. `daysQuery` is not `enabled`-gated — it fires on mount, so the window is the page's own initial network round trip, present on every real load, not only a slow one. **The most severe of this pass's findings**: a tap during that window reads as a real "start a rental"/"book a trip" affordance over a day that may not actually be free. **Rides with 5A-1** (GAP-7), which already touches this exact file and this exact query — one visit, not two. Found triaging S0-3, 14 Aug 2026. **Closed 14 Aug 2026, alongside 5A-1**: `byDate` now gates on `daysState.kind === "ready"` (the same `useQueryState` primitive its own error branch already used) rather than building from `days ?? []` regardless of fetch state — a "Loading…" fallback renders instead of the grid until the read settles. Six pre-existing tests had themselves depended on the bug (they read cells the instant the month label appeared, before the grid painted) and needed `getByTestId` → `findByTestId`; a seventh, new test reproduces the live finding directly with a deferred fetch | **Wave 5 · Track 5A** — closed, rode with item 5A-1 |
| **GAP-128** | **`PeopleListScreen` can show "Drivers · 0" / "Customers · 0" during its own initial pending window.** [:46](web/src/features/people/PeopleListScreen.tsx#L46) checks only `driversState.kind === "error"`; the else branch renders `Section` unconditionally with `count={drivers?.length ?? 0}` and `items={(drivers ?? []).map(...)}` — `Section` ([Section.tsx:31](web/src/design/primitives/Section.tsx#L31)) renders the count as plain heading text, `"Drivers · 0"`, with no distinction from a business that genuinely has none. The file's own comment two lines above the bug ("a failed list must not read as 'you have no drivers'") states the exact guarantee this misses for the non-failure case. Found triaging S0-3, 14 Aug 2026. **Built 16 Aug 2026** (Wave 7 step 1, full account in §0): `PeopleListScreen`'s driver/customer counts now wait for `useQueryState`'s own "ready" before rendering | closed 16 Aug 2026 — **Wave 7** |
| **GAP-129** | **`AddOpeningBalanceEntrySheet`'s four party/vehicle pickers can open with zero options while their own `enabled: open`-gated queries are still in flight — the identical shape GAP-125 was, one screen over.** [:223](web/src/features/opening-balance/AddOpeningBalanceEntrySheet.tsx#L223)/[:290](web/src/features/opening-balance/AddOpeningBalanceEntrySheet.tsx#L290) check only `kind === "error"` before handing `EntityPicker` an `options` array built from `.data ?? []`; [`EntityPicker`](web/src/components/EntityPicker.tsx#L29) — unlike the now-fixed `ReasonPicker` — has no `pending` prop at all, and no plan to add one inside S0-2's own scope, which touched only `ReasonPicker`. Narrower window than GAP-125's own worst case (all four queries fire on the *sheet's* open, giving a head start before the user reaches the nested picker's own sheet), but the same shape, on the same standing `enabled`-gated-picker pattern this codebase clearly keeps reaching for. Found triaging S0-3, 14 Aug 2026. **Built 16 Aug 2026** (Wave 7 step 1, full account in §0): `EntityPicker` gains a `pending` prop mirroring `ReasonPicker`'s own GAP-125 fix | closed 16 Aug 2026 — **Wave 7** |
| **GAP-130** | **Two lower-severity instances of the same shape, grouped because neither renders a confident wrong claim — only a momentarily missing secondary affordance.** `ExpenseCostRow.tsx`'s receipt-count link ([:94](web/src/features/costs/ExpenseCostRow.tsx#L94): `attachmentsState.kind === "error" ? … : receiptCount > 0 ? <button/> : null`) and `CorrectPaymentSheet.tsx`'s audit `Timeline` ([:187](web/src/features/period/CorrectPaymentSheet.tsx#L187): `historyFailure !== null ? … : timelineEntries.length > 0 ? <Timeline/> : null`) both render nothing, not a zero, while their read is pending — the same missing branch as GAP-126/127/128/129, but the consequence is a secondary link or history section that appears a moment late, not a headline number or a booking-safety grid. Recorded rather than silently accepted, same reasoning as GAP-115's own "no functional defect, unverified-branch risk" rows. Found triaging S0-3, 14 Aug 2026. **Both fixed 16 Aug 2026**, riding with PR #60's review response: each gained the missing `pending`/`idle` branch (`ExpenseCostRow` → "Checking receipts…", `CorrectPaymentSheet` → "Loading…" under the History label), the same shape GAP-126/127/128/129 already used. Two new tests, both confirmed against a never-resolving fetch (`new Promise<never>(() => {})`) to keep the query pending for the whole assertion window | closed 16 Aug 2026 — **Wave 8** |
| **GAP-125** | **Mobile Quick Add → New trip can stack two sheets and hide the vehicle picker.** Confirmed live on QA 14 Aug 2026 at 390 x 844 with touch/coarse-pointer emulation (`docs/qa/findings/2026-08-14.md`): an immediate `Add → New trip` tap left the original `Add` sheet visible while a second `New trip — choose a vehicle` sheet mounted almost entirely below the viewport (`y=837` in an 844px viewport) with no visible vehicle buttons. The route stayed on `/`, no write happened, and `/api/vehicle` was healthy in the same flow family, so this is not missing data. Source check matches the failure class: `ActionSheet` closes and opens the target in the same handler, while `QuickAddSheet` renders `ActionSheet` and `ReasonPicker` as sibling `Sheet`s. **Re-diagnosed against source 14 Aug 2026, and it is not the gap it was filed as — it is a GAP-101 miss, not a GAP-104 regression.** The history manager is behaving correctly: a history bug in `useMobileHistoryDismiss` manifests as *the wrong sheet closing*, never as an empty list, and its same-commit handoff provably nets to zero before any `window.history` call. The actual defect is one character sequence — [QuickAddSheet.tsx:192](web/src/features/quick-add/QuickAddSheet.tsx#L192) feeds `ReasonPicker` from `(vehiclesQuery.data ?? [])` over a query gated `enabled: tripVehiclePickerOpen`, so **the query only begins fetching when the picker opens, and renders an empty list for the whole flight** — the exact `?? []`-turns-an-unresolved-read-into-a-confident-empty-answer failure mode GAP-101 was created to eliminate. [`ReasonPicker`](web/src/components/ReasonPicker.tsx#L35) has precisely two branches, `error` and the list, **and no pending state at all** — its own doc comment names the hazard ("in place of the *otherwise indistinguishable* empty list") and closes only the error half of it. **The geometry in the report is an animation frame, not the defect**: 105px is a sheet containing a title and zero rows, and both `y` values are mid-transition (`Add` 22px into its close, the picker still sliding up). The report's own Passes section is the confirming evidence — "in a slower run the picker did load" is what a completed fetch looks like, and is not something broken layout would fix. **Blast radius is all three of `QuickAddSheet`'s pickers**, not just the trip one: `receivedPartyReasons` and `paidPartyReasons` are built the same way ([:90](web/src/features/quick-add/QuickAddSheet.tsx#L90), [:99](web/src/features/quick-add/QuickAddSheet.tsx#L99)). The other three `ReasonPicker` call sites are static lists and are unaffected — checked, not assumed. **And the guard cannot catch it**: `checkQueryErrorHandling` skips a whole file if `useQueryState` appears anywhere in it ([check-forbidden.mjs:297](scripts/check-forbidden.mjs#L297)), so three `useQueryState` calls wired to `error` only exempt every unhandled pending state in the same file — the identical file-wide-scope weakness Wave 2 already worked around in the *sibling* rule without fixing it here. Fixing the guard is part of this item, or the next picker repeats it. **Reproduced live 14 Aug 2026 (S0-1)** — see this tracker's own dated entry below for the cold-vs-warm-cache instrumentation; confirms the diagnosis exactly. **Fixed the same day (S0-2)**: `ReasonPicker` gained a `pending?: boolean` prop, checked after `error` and before the reasons list; `QuickAddSheet` wires it at all three call sites via a local `inFlight` helper collapsing `idle`/`pending` (deliberately, and only here — the query is gated on the picker's own open state, so `idle` only means "sheet still closed"). Two new tests, both confirmed to fail red against the pre-fix code first — a bare empty `<ul>`, the exact live symptom. **Hardened further, 15 Aug 2026, riding with the GAP-124b pass below**: `QuickAddSheet`'s own source `ActionSheet` is now gated `open={open && !childSheetOpen}` (true whenever any of its six child sheets/pickers is open), and the received/paid-party `ReasonPicker`s close once the payment sheet opens — belt-and-suspenders against the parent staying visually stacked behind a child during the mobile slide transition, on top of (not instead of) this row's own diagnosed fix. New assertion in `QuickAddSheet.test.tsx` pins that "Add" is gone the instant "New trip" is tapped. **S0-3 built 15 Aug 2026, closing this row and Wave 5 in full — superseding Plan.md's own 14 Aug "no new rule" decision, not repeating it.** That decision was correct about what it tested: two heuristics ("file never says the word 'pending'"; "a raw `.data ?? []` with no gate anywhere in the file") both chase the *harder* question of whether an exhaustive branch over a query's resolved `kind` exists before render, which is genuinely a control-flow problem no text-level check can answer reliably (12-of-15 false positives on the first, blind to `.data`-then-destructure indirection on the second). This build asks a narrower, purely structural question instead — never attempted on 14 Aug — and that narrower question is enough: `checkQueryErrorHandling` ([check-forbidden.mjs:284](scripts/check-forbidden.mjs#L284)) is now call-site-level, checking whether every `const X = useQuery({…})` binding's own `X` reaches `useQueryState(X)` or `<QueryState query={X}>` anywhere in the file — not "does this file mention the primitive anywhere," the exact hole this row's own bug slipped through, and not "is every read of it properly gated," which stays open exactly where GAP-126 through GAP-130 already track it (probed directly: 82 cases across the client read a wrapped query's raw `.data` alongside its own `useQueryState` call, almost all legitimate pre-gate plumbing or post-gate convenience, confirming that harder question is correctly still deferred to real AST tooling). A bare `const { data } = useQuery(...)` is flagged unconditionally, since destructuring only `data` discards `isError`/`isPending` before there is anything left to wrap. **Nine previously-hidden call sites surfaced immediately** across `CloseLeaseScreen`, `LeaseHubScreen`, `CorrectPaymentSheet`, `BookTripScreen`, `TripDetailScreen` (×2) and `VehicleCalendarScreen` — each checked individually rather than blanket-suppressed: all eight of the title-label/name-badge/fail-closed-gate cases got a scoped `// allow: <reason>` documenting exactly why that read degrades safely (never a wrong money figure, never a false booking affordance); the ninth, `ReviewThisMonthScreen`'s `warningsQuery`, was a real instance of this row's own failure class — a failed paperwork-warnings fetch silently read as "no vehicle has an expiring document" — and was fixed properly (wrapped with `useQueryState`, gated on `ready`, an inline `QueryStateFailure` added alongside the screen's existing overheads/partner treatment) rather than allow-commented away. `npm run guard` clean; the touched screens' own test files (69 tests across 7 files) pass unchanged. LT-13 remains open for the wider mobile interaction sweep, tracked separately | closed 15 Aug 2026 — **Wave 5 · Step 0 (shared prelude)** — S0-1/S0-2/S0-3 all done, Wave 5 closed in full |

| **GAP-12** | **Void-and-replace exists for `expense` only.** The other twelve W-50 tables carry the `voided_*` trio structurally, so the mechanism is proven — not each instance of it. A record created by mistake in any of the twelve cannot currently be reversed. **The reference pattern to replicate, not reinvent, is [`voidExpense`](api/src/domain/expense.ts#L170) + [`voidExpenseRow`](api/src/queries/expense.ts#L71)**: existence check → already-voided check → `writer.transaction` (`withActor` only attributes inside a real one) → catch `isPeriodClosedViolation` → `PeriodClosedError`, with `voidedReason` a parameter and never hardcoded. Widening `check-forbidden.mjs`'s `money/void-table-unfiltered` rule to the tables this puts a live void behind is part of the item — Wave 2 scoped it to four precisely because the rest had no void endpoint yet. **Scope decided 14 Aug 2026: all twelve remaining tables, uniformly**, not only those a screen reaches today — so this closes as filed rather than returning as a partial. **First three done, 14 Aug 2026: `capital_contribution`, `banking_event`, `partner_payout`** — the group with no child rows to unwind, so each is `voidExpense`'s shape exactly. Landed one shared `voidRequestSchema`/`voidedResponseSchema` pair (`schemas/common.ts`) rather than three near-identical bespoke schemas; kept one `XAlreadyVoidedError` class per table (matching Expense/Attachment/DayRecord's own precedent). `listBankingEvents`/`listPartnerPayouts` now select `voidedAt` — their own prior comments named this as the open question "when A9 adds a void endpoint," and this is that point — but the wire response still omits it; teaching the list and the client to render the struck-through row (W-50) is GAP-81's own shape, a separate item. 8 new integration tests, `npm run check` clean. **PR #44 opened against `wave5/plan-2026-08-14`** for this item's progress so far (Step 0 + GAP-36 both halves + these three); Gitar's review found two real issues in the driver/customer/vehicle archive endpoints, both fixed the same sitting — a race between the already-archived check and the write (archive UPDATEs now guard `WHERE voided_at IS NULL`, the `voidAdvanceById` shape, so a losing race is a no-op rather than a clobber; new concurrency regression test) and inconsistent transaction wrapping (fixed for consistency, though its stated reason — `audit_log` attribution — turned out not to apply: `driver`/`customer`/`vehicle` carry no `posted_period_id`, so migration 0002's trigger never covers them; two regression tests built on that wrong premise were removed rather than left green for the wrong reason). **`advance_settlement` and `write_off_recovery` turned out not to belong in the "no cascade" group** — checked before building, not after: voiding a settlement must recompute the parent `advance.status` (currently only written forward, at settlement time) or a voided settlement leaves a trip's INV-17 reconciliation silently wrong, and voiding a `write_off_recovery` must also address its own `payment` row or that payment reads as ordinary unallocated customer credit. **PR #42 (develop-bound, carries #44's commits forward) re-run through Gitar: one of its five findings was real and un-caught by the #44 fix above.** The archive TOCTOU finding was stale (already closed), the redundant-lookup (GAP-131) and N+1-deposit-sum (GAP-132) findings are minor and left open, and the `disposed`-lifecycle guard (GAP-133) is correctly deferred as unreachable — but the `WHERE voided_at IS NULL` guard landed on `archiveDriverRow`/`archiveCustomerRow` had not been carried to this item's own three void rows: `voidCapitalContributionRow`/`voidBankingEventRow`/`voidPartnerPayoutRow` still did a plain `WHERE eq(id, …)` UPDATE, so two concurrent voids on the same row could both succeed and the second silently clobber the first's `voidedReason`/`voidedBy`. Fixed 14 Aug 2026, same sitting, same shape: all three UPDATEs now guard the WHERE, the three domain functions map a losing race (`undefined`) to their own `XAlreadyVoidedError` instead of returning it, and one new concurrency regression test (`capital_contribution`) proves one request resolves 200 and the other 409. `npm run check` clean. **The remaining nine tables' cascade behaviour was asked and answered 14 Aug 2026, then re-validated against practical use before any code — two answers changed on that second pass** (build log below has the full reasoning). **The principle the nine decisions settled on:** *cascade into rows minted by the same call; refuse when separately-entered user actions sit beneath.* Per table: **`adjustment`** reverses its own effect, and where that pushes `amount_minor` below `settled_minor` it unwinds `payment_allocation` newest-first so the surplus becomes unallocated credit (DM §10.2) — `payment.status` stays `active`, cash never moves · **`offset_record`** unwinds both directions' allocations and reverses `settled_minor`+status, **needing one migration** to give `offset_allocation` the W-58 void trio it never got · **`deposit_movement`** gets a real void **and** `sumDepositMovements` gains the `voided_at IS NULL` filter in the same change, with `deposit.status` recomputed (newest live terminal movement, else `hold_window` when `hold_release_date` is set, else `held`) so INV-35's archive guard stays honest · **`advance_settlement`** recomputes `advance.status` and filters `sumSettledForAdvance`, restoring INV-17's trip-close block · **`advance`** refuses while live settlements exist · **`write_off_recovery`** cascades to `markPaymentReversed` its own payment (minted by the same call; leaving it active turns recovered bad debt into spendable credit, against INV-15) · **`write_off`** restores the obligation's status via `computeObligationStatus` (exact, since a write-off never touches `settled`/`waived`) and refuses while live recoveries exist · **`incident_recovery`** refuses while `received_amount_minor > 0`, and **`recordRecoveryReceived` is fixed to be a real correction** (reverse the prior payment and void its allocation before posting the new pair) since re-recording is the natural path and currently double-posts cash · **`obligation`** gets a direct void only for directly-raised `source_type`s (`post_closure_charge` today), refusing derived ones so a number never detaches from its cause, plus the standing guard against live allocations/adjustments. **The doc-change landed 14 Aug 2026, before any of the nine — `use-cases.md` W-61, `user-flows.md` INV-36 and F-8.5**, mechanising exactly the table above; cherry-picked onto `wave5a` too so Track A isn't building against stale docs. **All nine endpoints, done 14 Aug 2026, same wave** — migration `0024`'s `offset_allocation` void trio, the two read-side filters, one shared `VoidBlockedError`, nine `*AlreadyVoidedError` classes, `check-forbidden.mjs` widened, 23 new integration tests (full reasoning and the two bugs found building it — `adjustmentId` missing from the create response, `recordRecoveryReceived`'s double-post — in the build log above). **GAP-12 closes as filed.** Still open, deliberately deferred to its own item: GAP-60's `replaces_id` writes on the twelve create paths | closed 14 Aug 2026 — **Wave 5 · Track 5B**, item 5B-1 (A9b), with GAP-36 and GAP-60. **Track corrected 16 Aug 2026**: this cell still read as scheduled while [§0](#0-build-log) recorded twice that 5B-1 closed as filed, and GAP-60's own row had said so since 14 Aug — the wave closed, two of its three rows did not follow |

Found by Gitar's review of PR #42, 14 Aug 2026, confirmed against source. **Built 16 Aug 2026 exactly as scoped**: `archiveDriver`/`unarchiveDriver`/`archiveCustomer`/`unarchiveCustomer` now return `DriverRow`/`CustomerRow` (the pre-fetched row merged with post-write `voidedAt`) instead of `{ voidedAt }` or `void`; all four handlers dropped their own upfront `find*ForBusiness` call and its 404 check, relying on the domain function's own `NotFoundError` — the same pattern already used throughout `domain/`. `party-archive.test.ts` (21/21) unchanged and green, including every 404/409 case, so the handler-level check was provably redundant, not load-bearing | closed 16 Aug 2026 — **Wave 8** |
| **GAP-132** | **`findOpenMoneyForParty` sums a party's open deposits one query at a time.** [domain/party-archive.ts:72-74](api/src/domain/party-archive.ts#L72-L74) loops the party's open deposits and `await`s `sumDepositMovements` sequentially inside the loop — an N+1 read on the archive-eligibility check. A party normally holds few deposits, so the cost is small today; the fix is one aggregate query grouped by deposit, or `Promise.all` over the awaits. Found by Gitar's review of PR #42, 14 Aug 2026, confirmed against source. **Built 16 Aug 2026, the `Promise.all` option**, not the aggregate query — `sumDepositMovements` has eleven other call sites across `reports.ts`, `home.ts`, `driver-view.ts`, `lease-closure.ts`, `deposit.ts` and `handlers/lease.ts`, all single-deposit; rewriting its contract to fix one caller would have been scope creep past what this row named. `reports.ts:586`'s `sumDepositsHeld` has the identical sequential-loop shape and was checked, not touched — its own doc comment already reasons the count is business-wide-bounded ("one per driver/customer with an open deposit") and calls it "a small, real read, not the per-row bulk-write loop IG §2 warns against," a deliberate, recorded call rather than an oversight matching GAP-132's. Left as documented. `party-archive.test.ts` (21/21) green, including the deposit-held 409 case that exercises this path | closed 16 Aug 2026 — **Wave 8** |
| **GAP-133** | **Nothing refuses an archive/unarchive transition out of `vehicle.lifecycle`'s terminal `disposed` state.** `setVehicleLifecycle` ([queries/vehicle.ts:117](api/src/queries/vehicle.ts#L117)) and both handlers write `active`/`archived` unconditionally, with no guard against the third state the column's own type already carries. Not reachable today — no endpoint ever sets `disposed` — so this is latent, the same standing GAP-59 held before A11 made it live. Becomes real the moment a disposal endpoint exists; the fix is a 409 on any transition attempted from `disposed`. Found by Gitar's review of PR #42, 14 Aug 2026, confirmed against source | **flagged, not tracked** — nothing to build until a disposal endpoint exists to make the race reachable; revisit then, not before |
| **GAP-134** | **Any `ActionSheet`/`Sheet` selection that closes itself and triggers a router `navigate()` in the same handler silently reverts the navigation, on any real touch device — not the narrower "blank picker" LT-13 was filed to describe.** Reproduced live against `qa.fleetsettle.com`, 16 Aug 2026, via Chrome DevTools MCP with genuine CDP mobile/touch emulation (`390×844×3,mobile,touch`, which is what actually flips `window.matchMedia("(pointer: coarse)")` — the exact gate [`useMobileHistoryDismiss.ts:154`](web/src/lib/useMobileHistoryDismiss.ts#L154) checks, and why this never shows up under a plain desktop resize, matching the user's own report that web/responsive testing looked clean). Three for three, across two different sheets and three different destinations: Home → Add → New trip → pick a vehicle (expected `/vehicles/:id/trip/new`, landed back on `/`, twice, with two different vehicles); Vehicle → Vehicle actions → Book trip (expected `/vehicles/:id/trip/new`, landed back on `/vehicles/:id`); Vehicle → Vehicle actions → View calendar (expected `/vehicles/:id/calendar`, landed back on `/vehicles/:id`). No console or page error in any case — the tap just silently does nothing, the same "confident wrong outcome, nothing announced" shape W-56/M-28 exist to prevent, except here it's navigation rather than a number. **Mechanism, captured directly rather than inferred**: an instrumented `window.history` (wrapping `pushState`/`back`, listening for `popstate`) shows the router's own `pushState` for the new route landing first, then [`SheetHistoryStack#flush`](web/src/lib/useMobileHistoryDismiss.ts#L74)'s `history.back()` — meant to unwind the closing sheet's own pushed entry — firing while `location.href` is already the new route, consuming that entry instead; a `popstate` ~15-20ms later lands back on the previous screen. `SheetHistoryStack`'s own design ([its class doc, :8-36](web/src/lib/useMobileHistoryDismiss.ts#L8-L36)) assumes it is the only thing that ever touches `window.history`; a route `navigate()` firing in the same handler as a sheet close breaks that assumption, and nothing in the stack is aware a navigation happened. [`ReasonPicker`](web/src/components/ReasonPicker.tsx#L48-L51)'s own `onClick` (`onOpenChange(false); onSelect(reason);`) and `router.tsx`'s [`onBookTrip`](web/src/app/router.tsx#L720-L727) (`setQuickAddOpen(false); navigate(...)`) are both completely ordinary, unremarkable call sites — the race lives entirely in the shared primitive, which is why it reproduces identically from a plain `ActionSheet` with no nested picker at all (Vehicle actions → Book trip/View calendar), not only from `QuickAddSheet`'s deeper stack. **Corrects LT-13's own filed scope** (`docs/qa/live-test-plan.md`), rather than extending it: LT-13 was opened 14 Aug naming one symptom — "Quick Add → New trip → choose vehicle" rendering no vehicle choices — and that specific case is already closed as **GAP-125** (an unrelated `?? []`-during-fetch bug, fixed 15 Aug; reconfirmed still fixed here — the picker showed real vehicles every time). What LT-13's own "continue the wider sweep" instruction turned up is not a second instance of GAP-125's failure mode — it is a different, more severe defect one step later in the same flow, and it is not Quick-Add-specific at all: it is systemic to every `ActionSheet`/`Sheet` action anywhere in the client that navigates rather than opening another sheet. **Existing regression coverage does not catch it**: [`e2e/mobile-sheet-history.touch.spec.ts`](web/e2e/mobile-sheet-history.touch.spec.ts) — GAP-104's own touch-emulation regression file — only exercises a sheet closing and opening *another sheet* (Quick Add → Fuel/Expense); it has no case for a sheet closing and *navigating to a route*, the exact shape that fails here, which is why this shipped invisibly through every prior gate run. Full reproduction, raw `window.history` logs and CDP-emulation caveats in `docs/qa/findings/2026-08-16.md`. **Fixed 16 Aug 2026, not by patching the race but by removing the mechanism that could race at all**: `SheetHistoryStack` — the whole `pushState`/`.back()` scheme this section's own mechanism paragraph describes — is deleted outright, replaced by `useCloseWatcherDismiss` (`useMobileHistoryDismiss.ts` renamed), which answers a close request via the platform `CloseWatcher` API and never touches `window.history` at all, so there is nothing left for a `navigate()` to race, structurally rather than by timing. No fallback on iOS Safari (no stable `CloseWatcher` support as of 16 Aug 2026) — a deliberate, owner-confirmed call, since §3.3's rule is about the hardware back button, which iOS has none of. Full account, including the browser-support research behind the "remove the mechanism, don't patch it" call, in this tracker's own 16 Aug 2026 "later still" build-log entry above | closed 16 Aug 2026 — **Wave 8** |
| **GAP-135** | **DM D-5's `obligation.effective_due_on` maintenance was never built — `driver.settlement_rhythm` is written by no endpoint and read nowhere in the codebase.** Found investigating an automated PR #60 review comment (gitar-bot) claiming `web/src/lib/ageingBucket.ts`'s `computeAgeingBucket` could disagree with the server's `listAgeingBuckets` (api/src/queries/reports.ts) because the two bucketed off different columns (`dueOn` vs. `effectiveDueOn`). Traced every obligation-insert path (`billing-period.ts`, `trip.ts`, `management-fee.ts`, `post-closure-charge.ts`, `opening-balance.ts`, `confirmDay.ts`): every one of them sets `effectiveDueOn` equal to `dueOn` at creation, so the bot's claim was correct about the *mechanism* but not currently reachable — the two columns cannot diverge today, since nothing ever writes them differently. **That "cannot diverge" is itself the gap, one layer down**: DM D-5 licenses this divergence specifically for `driver.settlement_rhythm = 'weekly'` ("a driver who settles every Friday is not overdue on Thursday"), and its own row already flagged the maintenance mechanism as "worth confirming" — [`confirmDay.ts:236`](api/src/domain/confirmDay.ts#L236) sets `effectiveDueOn: input.businessDate` unconditionally, never reading `driver.settlementRhythm` at all, and grepping the whole client and API for `settlementRhythm` turns up only the schema column definition ([db/schema.ts:153](api/src/db/schema.ts#L153), `default('daily')`) — no create/update endpoint ever sets it away from the default, no read path ever consults it. **Harmless today** (every real driver is implicitly `'daily'`, where `effectiveDueOn === dueOn` is correct), **silently wrong the day anyone becomes `'weekly'`**: that driver's day-fee obligation would read as overdue on any day the fixed date lands before Friday, in both `listAgeingBuckets` and the driver-money screens, with no error and no way to notice short of an ageing report someone happens to compare against the settlement calendar. The class this belongs to is GAP-134's own (W-56/M-28's "confident wrong number, nothing announced"), latent rather than live for the same reason GAP-59 was before A11 — no write path currently reaches the diverging case. Fix shape not designed: needs at minimum a `settlement_rhythm`-aware `effectiveDueOn` computation inside `confirmDay.ts` (or a shared helper it and any future driver-obligation write path call), plus deciding what "not overdue until Friday" means for a rhythm change mid-cycle, which D-5's own row already flags as a second open question ("if the rhythm changes, existing rows keep their original value — deliberate, but worth confirming"). **Guarded 17 Aug 2026, and the option first recommended for it was wrong — worth recording, because the reasoning failed in a way this project has a rule against.** The proposal was a `CHECK` pinning `settlement_rhythm` to `'daily'`, on the argument that it made the wrong state unrepresentable and "forces the design when a real weekly driver exists." **Reading the owning documents before writing it showed weekly drivers already exist**: UC-31 lists "or pays weekly" as a way `received` differs, UC-37 calls it "the ordinary case for some drivers rather than an exception", F-4.5 carries the acceptance criterion outright, and FL A-26 has a dedicated edge case for a weekly driver checked on a Thursday. So the constraint was not a schema tidy-up at all — it was a silent product decision to drop a described phase-1 capability, exactly what CLAUDE.md's "the owning document decides" exists to catch, and it would have been made in `data-model.md`, one level below where such a decision belongs. **Built instead**: `confirmDay`/`confirmDaysBulk` refuse a non-`'daily'` driver with `SETTLEMENT_RHYTHM_UNSUPPORTED` (409) rather than writing an `effective_due_on` known to read as overdue — W-56's "admit the gap, never a confident wrong number" applied to a write instead of a report. One read per call, not per day: `confirmDaysBulk` carries a single `driverId` for its whole batch, so checking inside `confirmDayInTx` would have reintroduced the exact N+1 GAP-132 had just removed. Two integration tests, both confirmed failing red (201/200 instead of 409) against the guard disabled before being trusted. Docs moved with it: `data-model.md` D-5 v1.1.9, `user-flows.md` F-4.5 v1.1.14 — the criterion is recorded as **unmet, not withdrawn** | **phase 2** — reclassified 17 Aug 2026. The guard makes it safe, not done: this was never verification debt, it is an unbuilt feature F-4.5 has always specified, and it is scheduled with the other phase-2 work rather than left implying a small fix |
| **GAP-136** | **UC-99's statement-to-PDF half — no PDF renderer has a home in this runtime.** Split out of GAP-18 on 17 Aug 2026, when the decision it was waiting on was finally taken, rather than left making its parent row look blocked when only half of it ever was. `user-flows.md` F-9.3 asks for "a year of transactions to CSV, a statement to PDF"; the CSV half is an ordinary data dump and stays in GAP-18, built as specified. The PDF half has no obvious home: `api/CLAUDE.md` and `TS §8` rule out Node globals, a filesystem and unbounded CPU per invocation, so the two available answers are a rendering dependency running inside the same runtime that performs every money write, or an external service that would receive every figure on the statement — names, amounts, balances. **Deferred rather than decided, deliberately**: neither answer is needed for what UC-99 is actually for. Getting the numbers out is what the CSV does, and a statement can be printed from the screen that already renders it, so nothing is unreachable while this waits. **What would settle it is evidence this project does not have**: a stated need for a document rather than a page — an accountant who refuses CSV, or a filing that requires PDF. Decided 17 Aug 2026; docs moved with it (`use-cases.md` UC-99 v1.2.13, `user-flows.md` F-9.3 v1.1.14, both recording the split rather than weakening the criterion) | **phase 2** — deferred by decision, not blocked by anything |
| **GAP-44** | **INV-1 (double-booked vehicle-day) has no `Dialog` anywhere in the client.** `Dialog.tsx`'s own doc comment reserves it to three call sites — INV-1, INV-17, M-10 — and `grep`ing for real `<Dialog` usage (not the doc comments referencing it) finds exactly one: `CloseTripSheet.tsx`, for INV-17. Nothing renders it for INV-1. A double-booking conflict today surfaces as a bare line of `mutation.error.message` in both `StartLeaseScreen.tsx` ([:448](web/src/features/vehicles/StartLeaseScreen.tsx#L448)) and `BookTripScreen.tsx` ([:307](web/src/features/trips/BookTripScreen.tsx#L307)) — confirmed, `VEHICLE_DOUBLE_BOOKED` ([app-error.ts:97](api/src/errors/app-error.ts#L97)) is never referenced anywhere under `web/src`. Not merely a component swap — §9.3 requires "the fix offered inline," and that copy doesn't exist yet either. Found in `UI-UX-REVIEW.md` §2.1, confirmed against source 6 Aug 2026. **Decided 10 Aug 2026, not yet built**: the fix enriches the backend error with real conflict data (the conflicting dates and the entity holding them) rather than a generic name-and-dismiss — the one existing precedent, INV-17's own `Dialog` ([CloseTripSheet.tsx:165-175](web/src/features/trips/CloseTripSheet.tsx#L165-L175)), still only names its block and dismisses; GAP-100 later added a staff settlement caller, but this dialog still does not deep-link to it. Chosen over the cheaper "name it, link to the calendar" option because a precise conflict statement is worth the extra surface. **Bigger than a client-only fix as a result**: `VehicleDoubleBookedError` needs a structured payload, every catch site of the exclusion-constraint violation needs to supply it (`domain/trip.ts` today; check `lease.ts`/`dailyLease.ts` for the same shape), and the wire schema changes to carry it. **Built 16 Aug 2026** (full account in §0): `VehicleDoubleBookedError` carries a structured `VehicleDoubleBookedDetails` (conflicting dates, resolved holder name/destination, next-fit date); auditing `lease.ts`/`dailyLease.ts` as this row asked found neither ever throws this error, so `bookTrip` is the only reachable source | closed 16 Aug 2026 — **Wave 7** — sequenced after Wave 2, which is what made D-12's "replaced, not refused" true |
| **GAP-59** | **Related ids on one request are each checked against the business, never against each other — so an expense can name a vehicle and a trip that have nothing to do with one another.** [handlers/expense.ts:38-46](api/src/handlers/expense.ts#L38-L46) fetches the trip and the incident purely to prove they belong to this business, **then discards both rows** — `trip.vehicleId` is never compared to `body.vehicleId`, and neither is `incident.vehicleId`. Two same-business ids that describe different events are accepted as one coherent fact. The row then lists under `GET /api/trip/{id}/expense` (filtered by `trip_id` alone, [queries/expense.ts:309](api/src/queries/expense.ts#L309)) *and* under the other vehicle's costs, so one cost is attributed to a trip on one vehicle and to the profit of another. **Not reachable from the client today** — no screen sends both ids (the fuel-fill trip link is GAP-30, unbuilt) — which is what keeps this latent rather than live, the same standing as GAP-1 before A11. **`post-closure-charge.ts` has the same shape** (source, party and vehicle each validated independently). Worth deciding deliberately whether the fix is request-level or a DB constraint; CLAUDE.md's "validate at boundaries" argues for the handler, and DM's preference for constraints-over-code argues for the schema. Found by `MUST-FIX-FINDINGS.md` (backend edition) §6, confirmed against source 7 Aug 2026. **The `expense` half closed in Wave 1** — migration `0016`'s composite FKs plus two `CHECK`s (D-14/INV-33) make the mismatch unrepresentable, so what remains under this id is `post_closure_charge` alone, which writes an `obligation` and cannot take the same treatment. **Sized 13 Aug 2026 into Wave 4 Step 2, where it pairs with GAP-123 below** — the same three lines carry both. **Built 14 Aug 2026, closing with the rest of Wave 4**: `findVehicleForBusiness` on `body.vehicleId` → 404, matching its four siblings, then `vehicleId` cross-checked against the resolved `lease.vehicleId`/`trip.vehicleId` → 400, handler-level (this writes an `obligation`, not a table `0016`'s composite-FK treatment could reach) | closed 14 Aug 2026 — **Wave 4**, Step 2, paired with GAP-123 |
| **GAP-123** | **`POST /api/post-closure-charge` writes an entirely unvalidated `vehicleId` into a money row.** [handlers/post-closure-charge.ts:44](api/src/handlers/post-closure-charge.ts#L44) spreads `body.vehicleId` into the obligation having never looked it up; the four sibling ids on the same handler (`sourceId` via lease/trip, `partyCustomerId`, `partyDriverId`) are each proven against the business first. `obligation.vehicle_id` is a plain `uuid REFERENCES vehicle(id)` ([0001:500](api/migrations/0001_initial_schema.sql#L500)) with no business-consistency constraint behind it, so **another business's vehicle id satisfies the FK and is accepted**, attributing this business's charge to a vehicle it does not own. **Not a leak, and the distinction is worth keeping straight**: the handler never branches on a lookup it does not perform, so every request returns 201 either way and there is no existence oracle — this is a foreign key written into a money table, not a cross-tenant read. Distinct from GAP-59, which is the *consistency* of two ids that both belong here; this is one id that may not belong here at all. Found 13 Aug 2026 sizing Wave 4, by reading the handler rather than from any review. **Built 14 Aug 2026 alongside GAP-59** — the same handler-level fix (find-then-cross-check) closes both | closed 14 Aug 2026 — **Wave 4**, Step 2, paired with GAP-59 |
| **GAP-65** | **F-6.6 — the printed slip's "not optional" mechanism doesn't exist**: a signed, expiring, no-login share link, per the spec's own words. B5's "statement link" checklist item is the driver's own authenticated view, a different requirement it does not satisfy. Found by the flow-inventory audit (AUDIT-5). **Expiry decided 11 Aug 2026: 30 days. Mechanism decided 14 Aug 2026: a stored, revocable link row** — chosen against the stateless-token recommendation, and it deliberately exceeds F-6.6, which asks only for "a signed link that expires… not a guessable URL". What the table buys: revocation the moment a phone is lost, and a record of who shared what with whom. Both decisions still stand for whenever this is built. **The "Wave 5's only migration, `0024`" claim recorded with decision 4 (§0) is stale, corrected 15 Aug 2026**: `0024` went to GAP-12/5B-1's `offset_allocation` void trio instead, decided and built the same sitting as this row's own decision — Wave 5 shipped five migrations in the end (`0024`–`0028`), not one, and A14's own number is whatever is next free when it is actually built. **W-49 is the acceptance criterion, not a nice-to-have**: a share link is a route that bypasses the JWT entirely, so it takes the linked-driver isolation test class by construction, and expiry and revocation each need their own test rather than being assumed from the column existing. **Deferred out of Wave 5, 15 Aug 2026 — checked, not assumed, while sizing 5B-5's own backend-only build.** The issue/revoke/public-read endpoints this item was scoped as have no reachable consumer without the actual unauthenticated page a driver opens the link on, unlike 5B-2/3's endpoints, which had real future callers already implied by existing flows. That page is real, undiscussed scope: every route in `web/` currently sits behind `AuthGate` (`main.tsx` wraps the whole router in it on the non-stub path) — there is no unauthenticated path in this client at all today, so A14 is not "a migration plus three endpoints" the way it was sized, it is that plus a first exception to the auth boundary. Resized and picked up whole (backend, the `AuthGate` exception, and the share button) in a future wave rather than split across two. **Deferred out of phase 1 entirely, by owner decision, 17 Aug 2026 — and this amends a standing premise rather than merely rescheduling an item.** The premise set 12 Aug was that nothing goes live until every phase-1 gap is fixed and tested; this is the first gap deliberately left unfixed at release, so the premise now reads "every phase-1 gap except those explicitly deferred with their reason recorded," and this row is the first such reason. **What made it worth the amendment**: the item is not a migration plus three endpoints, it is the first route in this client outside `AuthGate`, carrying someone's whole financial position, and it takes W-49's linked-driver isolation class plus its own expiry and revocation tests by construction. **What it does not cost**: nothing becomes unreachable — a linked driver already reads these exact figures through F-6.8/UC-59, and an unlinked one is handed the printed slip, which is what UC-57 describes first. ~~**Corrected 22 Aug 2026 — this premise is false.**~~ The printed slip was never built (GAP-170, filed the same day): `grep` across `web/src` and `api/src` finds no print/slip/statement mechanism at all, live-confirmed against `qa.fleetsettle.com` the same session. So as the build stands today, an unlinked driver or customer **does** lose reachability by this deferral — there is no route at all, logged in or not, for them to see their financial position. This does not by itself mean GAP-65 should move back to phase 1 — that is the owner's call, not a conclusion this correction draws — but the reasoning that made deferring it feel free no longer holds, and the row should not be read as still-sound until re-reviewed. Docs moved with the decision (`use-cases.md` UC-57 v1.2.13, `user-flows.md` F-6.6 v1.1.14): **F-6.6's "Not optional" clause is untouched and the acceptance criterion is recorded as unmet rather than withdrawn**, because print satisfies what that clause actually demands. **Re-review closed 27 Aug 2026**: GAP-170 shipped the same day (PR #143), so the "an unlinked one is handed the printed slip" premise this paragraph flagged as false on 22 Aug is true again — nothing further changes about this row's own phase-2 placement, that question was never GAP-170's to answer and stays the owner's call | **phase 2** — deferred by decision 17 Aug 2026, to be picked up whole (backend, the `AuthGate` exception, the share button) |
| **GAP-171** | **The sticky "Next"/primary-action CTA `Screen` renders can overlap a form's own last field before any scroll happens, and a plain tap on the covered area hits the CTA instead of the field.** Found live 23 Aug 2026 testing "register a bus, put it on daily lease, book a multi-day charter alongside it" end to end: tapping `BookTripScreen` Step 1's "Agreed amount" (the last field, `MoneyField`) three separate times, with a fresh accessibility snapshot immediately before each click, advanced straight to Step 2 every time — no amount sheet ever opened. `Screen.tsx`'s own comment names the intended mechanism precisely ("`position: sticky`, so `scroll-padding-bottom` ... keeps a focused field from landing behind it") and UI §4.2/§17 cite it as the fix for WCAG 2.4.11 — but `scroll-pb-*` only offsets the browser's *scroll-into-view* calculation, used when something is already focused and gets programmatically or keyboard-scrolled into view. It reserves no static padding in the unscrolled layout, so a **plain pointer tap**, which hit-tests before any focus/scroll event fires, lands wherever the sticky bar happens to already be sitting. Proven geometrically, not just reproduced: `document.elementFromPoint` at "Agreed amount"'s own visual centre resolved to the "Next" button's DOM node; the two elements' bounding rects overlapped by 23px (`agreedRect` top 529/bottom 573, `nextRect` top 496/bottom 552); the scroll container's own `scrollHeight` (572) genuinely exceeded its `clientHeight` (483), so this was real overflow, not a false read. **Workaround used to complete testing, not a fix**: scrolling the container to its end via `scrollContainer.scrollTop = scrollContainer.scrollHeight` before clicking let the sheet open normally — confirming the click-hit-testing theory directly, since nothing else changed. **Blast radius untested beyond this one screen** — `Screen`'s own docstring says "every later screen builds on this one," so any form whose content genuinely overflows a real viewport, with its last field close enough to the CTA's height (56px + safe-area inset), is a candidate; this was found on a stock 1280×800 desktop session, not a cramped phone, which suggests it is not rare. **Not fixed this session** — filed for scheduling; the likely correct fix is real `padding-bottom` on the scroll container sized to the CTA's rendered height (not just `scroll-padding-bottom`), but that wants checking against every existing `Screen` consumer before landing, not decided in passing here | closed 25 Aug 2026 — **Step 14**: real, unscrolled `padding-bottom` (`pb-[88px]`/`pb-[76px]`, matching the existing `scroll-pb-*` sizes) now wraps `Screen`'s `{children}` ahead of the sticky CTA, so the last field is never laid out inside the zone the CTA can paint over, checked against every existing `Screen` consumer via the full 147-file web suite (887→891 passing) rather than assumed safe |
| **GAP-172** | **No client screen can set `expense.trip_id` or `expense.incident_id`, though both exist and are fully handled by the API.** Found the same session, testing "an incident during a trip, its repair cost borne by us, not the customer": `api/src/handlers/expense.ts` accepts and stores both ids (validates the trip/incident belongs to the business, round-trips them on read, filters by them on list) — but `RecordExpenseSheet.tsx` (`grep -c "incident"` → 0, no `tripId` reference either) never offers either field, `TripDetailScreen`'s only actions are "Record advance" and "Close/Cancel trip," the incident's own "Incident actions" menu offers only "Record off-road days," "Customer contribution" and "Submit insurance claim," and `router.tsx` wires no trip- or incident-scoped expense route at all. **Confirmed live, not just by source**: booked a real 5-day trip, recorded a real Rs 8,000 "Repairs" expense on a date inside it (`borne_by` correctly auto-resolved to `us`, per UC §6.7's matrix — that half of the mechanism is right) — the trip's own "Costs so far" stayed **Rs 0** and a separately-created incident's "Total repairs" stayed **Rs 0**, both permanently, since the expense row that should count toward each has `trip_id`/`incident_id` both `null` with no way to set them after the fact either. **Consequence beyond this one figure**: F-5.4's whole trip-close promise ("costs by type, profit, distance, km/l, profit per km") and UC-12's incident bottom line ("total repair cost, total recovered, still expected, net cost to you") are both structurally unreachable — every trip and every incident in this business will show costs of zero forever, regardless of what is actually spent, which is exactly the "confident wrong number" class W-56 exists to prevent, except here the number is confidently *absent* rather than wrong. **Not fixed this session** — filed for scheduling; likely the smallest correct fix is a picker on `RecordExpenseSheet` (optional, defaulting to none) plus matching actions on the trip and incident screens that pre-fill it, mirroring how `MoneyField`'s existing entity-picker pattern already works elsewhere in this client | closed 25 Aug 2026 — **Step 15**: `RecordExpenseSheet` gained optional `tripId`/`incidentId` props (sent alongside `vehicleId`, never exclusive with it, so `resolveBorneByDefault` still runs); `TripDetailScreen` gained a "Record cost" action beside "Costs so far", `IncidentScreen` gained "Record repair cost" in its actions menu — both pre-fill the vehicle from the trip/incident so the picker never shows |
| **GAP-173** | **⏳ Everything from here to "Closed 24 Aug 2026" below is the account *as filed on 23 August 2026*, kept for the reasoning rather than the status — every grep and present-tense claim in it describes the code at filing time, not now. Group by `adjustment_type` (a real enum) rather than the sparse free-text `reason`. Also unenforced: nothing in the DDL ties `adjustment.sign` to `adjustment_type`, and the sum ignores `sign`. **Distinct from GAP-72**, which is the comparison being wrong for the date it already uses. | **GAP-60** | **A void and its replacement are not linked, so "what corrected this?" has no answer in the data.** `expense` carries the `voided_*` trio and W-50's void-and-replace is the prescribed correction path, but **there is no `replaces_id`/`corrects_id` column anywhere in the schema** (`grep`ped `api/src`, `api/migrations`, `packages/shared` — the only hit is [queries/expense.ts:67](api/src/queries/expense.ts#L67)'s own comment saying the column does not exist). So the corrected expense and its correction are two unrelated rows that happen to be near each other in time. UC-97/F-8.6 ("who changed what, readable from the record itself") is satisfiable for a *payment* — `payment_correction` is a real table — and not for the twelve other money tables W-50 covers. **Distinct from GAP-12**, which is about void endpoints not existing; this is about the linkage being absent even where voiding works. Relevant to A9b, which will build ~15 void paths and would otherwise build all fifteen without it. Found by `MUST-FIX-FINDINGS.md` (backend edition) §11, confirmed 7 Aug 2026. **Shape decided 9 Aug 2026, not yet built**: a nullable, self-referencing `replaces_id uuid` column **on each of the 12 tables**, not one shared polymorphic correction table — typed, indexable, joinable without a polymorphic lookup, and Postgres can enforce it as a real per-table FK the way a polymorphic reference can't be. Lands with A9b, as already planned. **The "no column anywhere" claim is stale — corrected 14 Aug 2026 planning Wave 5**: migration `0021` landed it in Wave 1, on **thirteen** tables (the doc's "twelve" undercounted; `payment` stays out, it has `payment_correction`). Nine `replacesId` declarations sit in `db/schema.ts` with **zero writes** anywhere in `api/src`. The rule to settle once rather than fifteen times: **the replacement writes `replaces_id`, not the void**. **Built 14 Aug 2026, closing 5B-1 for good.** Every one of the thirteen tables' create paths now takes an optional `replacesId`, validated before the insert (in the same transaction the period check already opened): the named row must belong to this business (else 404, the ordinary cross-tenant shape) and must already be voided (else the new, shared `ReplacesTargetNotVoidedError`, 409) — void first, then replace, never the other order. **Migration `0025`** adds the constraint side: a partial unique index (`WHERE replaces_id IS NOT NULL`) per table, so two concurrent replies at the same voided row can't both land — the loser gets the new shared `ReplacesTargetAlreadyReplacedError` (409), the identical "constraint, not code" shape the void race already uses. `capital_contribution`/`banking_event`/`partner_payout` (`domain/partner.ts`) and `expense` were the reference build; the remaining nine followed the identical shape: `adjustment`, `offset_record`, `deposit_movement`, `advance` and `advance_settlement` (two create paths, one file), `write_off` and `write_off_recovery`, `incident_recovery` (**two** create paths — `recordCustomerContribution` and `submitInsuranceClaim`, both wired, since either can be the one a manager got wrong), and `obligation` (only through `post-closure-charge`, the one kind INV-36 ever lets void directly — the others correct at their source, so `replacesId` on this table only ever names another post-closure charge). **Scope held deliberately narrow**: the wire response echoes `replacesId` on the record's own create response and on the general list-for-business read where one already exists (both cheap, and core to F-8.6 — "what corrected this?" has to be answerable from the record a manager is already looking at); it was **not** threaded into every narrower per-context projection (a vehicle's cost tab, a trip's own cost breakdown), the same restraint GAP-81 already models for `voidedAt`. 5B-1 (GAP-12 + GAP-36 + GAP-60 together) closes as filed. 26 new integration tests across the thirteen tables (happy path, not-voided-yet, already-replaced, cross-tenant 404 — not all four for every table, spot-checked where the mechanism repeats verbatim), `npm run check` clean, migration `0025` verified against a disposable Neon branch (drift assertion clean, golden fixtures unmoved) | **A9b** — **Wave 5 · Track 5B**, item 5B-1, alongside GAP-12/GAP-36 — **closed 14 Aug 2026** |

Neither fails a test today, which is exactly why neither has been fixed. Trivial, and a ride-along for whichever item next touches `web/src/test/` rather than a sitting of its own. F-4.6's bulk week-confirm landed alongside it rather than separately, per Plan.md's own finding that acceptance scenario A-2 needs both together: `POST /api/day-record/{dailyLeaseId}/confirm-week` confirms every open day before today on one lease in one transaction, sharing `confirmDay`'s own per-day write via a new `confirmDayInTx` savepoint helper ([domain/confirmDay.ts:93](api/src/domain/confirmDay.ts#L93)) — already-adjusted days and `did_not_run` are never touched. | **GAP-5** | `recordPayment`'s surplus comes back as `unallocatedMinor` but is never applied forward against a future due. **Re-raised 10 Aug** by `PENDING-REVIEW-ITEMS-2026-08-10.md`; evaluated the same day, nothing about size changed — a durable credit ledger, its own allocation-on-read path, retry-safety, not a fix. **Split 13 Aug 2026 sizing Wave 4, because this row had been carrying two things under one id.** **GAP-5a — a live money defect nobody had filed**: [domain/payment.ts:113](api/src/domain/payment.ts#L113) reads a party's outstanding obligations with no row lock and then loops allocating, so two concurrent payments can both settle the same obligation past its own `amount_minor`. Small, and it sequences ahead of items sized under it. **GAP-5b — the feature this row always described**, the forward application. **No new table for either, checked rather than assumed**: `payment_allocation` has no `posted_period_id`, is deliberately outside `assert_period_open()`'s array, carries `UNIQUE (payment_id, obligation_id)` for idempotency, and its own DDL comment says `allocated_on` "may be later than payment.occurred_on" ([0001:549-556](api/migrations/0001_initial_schema.sql#L549-L556)). **Built 14 Aug 2026**: **5a**, `findOutstandingObligationsForParty` gained `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` at both its callers (`payment.ts`, `offset.ts`), Step 0's own reproduction now the committed regression test. **5b landed on-write** (the lower-risk of Step 1's two options), one shared `domain/credit-forward.ts` helper, seven call sites — surfacing two pre-existing bugs neither this row nor Plan.md's own anticipated: `bookTrip` and `recordPostClosureCharge` had each hardcoded their response's `status`/`settledMinor`, true before credit-forward existed and false after; both now report the real post-credit state | closed 14 Aug 2026 — **Wave 4**, 5a at Step 5, 5b at Step 6 |
| **GAP-6** | F-8.4's deposit-apply — reusing `allocateAgainstOldest` verbatim would wrongly create a `payment` row for money already held. **Needs no migration, checked 14 Aug 2026 rather than assumed**: `deposit_movement` has carried `movement_type = 'applied'` and `obligation_id uuid REFERENCES obligation(id) -- when applied against what is owed` since `0001` ([:670-675](api/migrations/0001_initial_schema.sql#L670-L675)). The gap is entirely in the domain layer — `recordDepositMovement` never takes the `obligationId` the column exists for, and nothing moves the obligation's `settled_minor` alongside the movement. **Read Wave 4's `credit-forward.ts` first**: it settles an obligation from money held elsewhere, and a second implementation of that arithmetic is the divergence trap. **Built 14 Aug 2026, closing 5B-3's first half.** Two layers, both real gaps the row's own text pointed at: (1) `recordDepositMovement` (F-6.7/UC-58, the driver's own deposit) now takes `obligationId`, required exactly when `movementType` is `'applied'` — validates the named obligation belongs to this business, is not voided, is `owed_to_us` (a deposit can only offset what its own party owes *us*), belongs to the *same party* as the deposit, and that the amount does not exceed what remains outstanding on it, then moves `settled_minor`/`status` directly via `updateObligationSettled` in the same transaction as the movement insert — never `recordPayment`'s `allocateAgainstOldest`, so no `payment` row is ever minted for money already held. (2) `settleLeaseDeposit` (F-2.6 step 6, the customer's own lease-closure flow) gains the fourth action its own docstring had explicitly deferred ("apply against what is owed... recorded in TRACKER.md rather than half-built here") — `action: 'apply'` sweeps the held balance against the customer's outstanding `owed_to_us` obligations oldest-due-first (the identical discipline `getLeaseClosureSummary`'s own INV-18 list already uses), one `recordDepositMovementTx` call per obligation touched inside a single transaction with the obligations locked `forUpdate`, since each `deposit_movement` row names exactly one obligation. Any balance left once every due is cleared stays `held` (deliberately not transitioned to a terminal status — out of this item's own scope, recorded rather than silently assumed), available for a follow-up refund call. Found and fixed the same sitting: `trackCreatedLease`'s own test-harness teardown deleted `billing_period`-sourced obligations before clearing `deposit_movement.obligation_id` references to them — unreachable before this item, since nothing had ever populated that column; now nulls the reference first. 15 new integration tests (7 for the driver-side primitive, 2 for the lease-closure sweep, split by the two call sites) plus 3 new shared-schema unit tests, `npm run check` clean | closed 14 Aug 2026 — **Wave 5 · Track 5B**, item 5B-3, paired with GAP-15 |
| **GAP-7** | Trip `hold` (ST-5) and `in_progress` are unreachable — `bookTrip` writes `booked` outright. `VehicleCalendarScreen`'s hold-outline cell and `T?` glyph render and are tested but cannot occur. **Decided 11 Aug 2026: build `hold`/`in_progress` end to end** — the calendar UI stays as specified, it does not get simplified away. **One sub-question the decision surfaced, not yet answered**: ST-5 (`user-flows.md:201`) says a hold "expires or is confirmed" with no duration or mechanism specified anywhere — no default window, no cron, no column to hold one. That is a `doc-change` to settle first, on the same rule that sent GAP-70/71 and GAP-10 through `user-flows.md` before any code moved: the document is silent, not wrong, and the build should not invent a number nobody decided. **The `doc-change` landed 12 Aug 2026 and this row's blocker text is stale — corrected 14 Aug while planning Wave 5.** `user-flows.md` v1.1.8 settled ST-5's mechanism (INV-32/33) and migration `0020` shipped `trip.hold_expires_on` plus a `business_settings.hold_expiry_days` default of 7 the next day. **Startable, and has been since Wave 1.** What is actually missing: `bookTrip` writes `'booked'` outright and **no file under `api/src/domain` mentions `hold` at all** — though `trip.status` has defaulted to `'hold'` in its own CHECK since `0001` ([:361](api/migrations/0001_initial_schema.sql#L361)). Recorded here instead of edited there, per CLAUDE.md's own forward-only rule. | closed 14 Aug 2026 — **Wave 5 · Track 5A**, item 5A-1. **Track cell added 16 Aug 2026**, same defect as GAP-124's row: no third field, so no status rendered |

**Built end to end, 14 Aug 2026.** All three decided mechanics as specified: `hold_expires_on` the only truth; `bookTrip`/`startDailyLease`/`startLease` each call the new `releaseExpiredHolds` (queries/trip.ts) for their own vehicle before touching an allocation, and a new `release-expired-holds` nightly unit (`scheduled.ts`) catches only what nobody booked around; `deriveTripStatus()` (domain/trip.ts) derives `in_progress` at every read, never written. `bookTrip` gains `asHold` — when true it still reserves the calendar (`vehicle_day_allocation.is_hold: true`) so INV-1 still fires on a real conflict, but skips pausing day records and raises no `trip_fare` obligation, both deferred to the new `POST /api/trip/{id}/confirm` (`confirmTripHold`), per F-5.1's own Accept clause ("reserves the calendar but does not suppress daily cards"). **One correctness gap found and closed in the same pass, not filed separately**: `closeTrip` had no guard against a still-`hold` trip, which would have closed without ever pausing its days or raising its receivable — now refuses with `VALIDATION_ERROR` until confirmed. GAP-127 rode along as its own row said it would (same file, same query). Full account, including a client hold/confirm UI on `BookTripScreen`/`TripDetailScreen`, in Plan.md's own 5A-1 entry. **Verification gap, stated plainly, and closed 15 Aug 2026**: the original build had no `TEST_DATABASE_URL`/Neon MCP access, so `trip.test.ts`/`scheduled.test.ts` were written and self-consistent but never run against real Postgres. Run for the first time 15 Aug 2026, alongside evaluating PR #46/#47's Gitar review — five real bugs surfaced in total, two of them the same ones Gitar's own review had already named from source (expired-hold release left a daily-lease calendar hole, fixed by factoring `cancelTrip`'s GAP-119 restore into a shared `restoreDailyLeaseOccupancy` every `releaseExpiredHolds` caller now runs; a stale pre-transaction read let a concurrent confirm double-post a receivable or resurrect a cancelled hold, fixed with a `forUpdate` re-read inside `confirmTripHold`'s own transaction) and three the test run itself found, none of them touched by either review: **`releaseAllExpiredHolds`'s cron path** voids with `voided_by` left `undefined` (by design — the nightly sweep has no actor) but `vehicle_day_allocation_void_check` (migration `0022`) required it `NOT NULL` whenever `voided_at` is set, so the cron's own release always 500'd the moment it found a real expired hold to release — migration `0026` drops that requirement for this table only, matching what the code already intended; **`cancelTripRow`** never cleared `hold_expires_on` when cancelling a still-`hold` trip, violating `trip_hold_expires_on_check` (migration `0020`) the moment a manager cancelled a hold directly instead of letting it expire; and **two `trip.test.ts` fixture bugs** — a vehicle arrangement never set before booking a trip/starting a daily lease on it, and a booked trip never registered with `ctx.trackCreatedTrip` so its own allocation row blocked the vehicle's teardown delete. Verified clean end to end on a fresh disposable Neon branch (migrations `0001`–`0026` applied from scratch): the full `api` integration suite, 37 files/657 tests, green throughout, alongside `guard`/`typecheck`/`lint`/`lint:css`/`format`/unit tests across all three workspaces | **Wave 5 · Track 5A** — item 5A-1, **done 14 Aug 2026, verified 15 Aug 2026** |

AND status = 'open' RETURNING`, and under READ COMMITTED a second concurrent close blocks on that row lock, re-evaluates against the committed version, matches nothing and raises `NotFoundError` — a real guard, not a status check. ### Closed

**Compacted from full accounts to one line each, starting 9 August 2026** — the full accounts are in git history and in each item's section in [Plan.md](Plan.md). Kept as one line each because a closed gap's remaining value is "this id means this thing, and it is done"; where a closure taught something that still binds, the lesson lives in [§5](#5-the-traps) and the row points at it. **The row count is deliberately not stated here** — it is mechanically computable from the table itself, and a hand-copied count in prose is exactly the kind of number this project has already watched drift (CLAUDE.md's own migration-count correction, and this table's earlier "38 gaps" claim, stale since well past 9 Aug before this correction).

| id | Closed | What it was, and what closed it |
