Full account and GAP-65's restored deferral reasoning above |
| **GAP-182** | 24 Aug | **`data-model.md`'s DDL blocks disagreed with the migrations in four places, and one of the four was a code bug wearing a docs costume.** `insurance_claim`/`incident_recovery` gained `business_id` in migration `0004`, `expense` gained `voided_by` in `0007` (whose own header already recorded that this document wrote only two of the void trio), `offset_allocation` gained the void trio in `0024` — all three added to the doc. The fourth ran the other way: `business_creation_request.status` was documented with `DEFAULT 'pending'` **which the database never had**, migration `0030` stating the omission is deliberate, so the doc was the wrong one of the three sources and the `DEFAULT` was removed from it rather than added to the migration. **The code half is the part worth keeping**: `api/src/db/schema.ts` declared `.default("pending")` on that same column — inert only because the single writer (`insertBusinessCreationRequest`) hard-codes the value on every insert, and a runtime failure on a `NOT NULL` column the first time any insert path trusted the declared default. Dropped. `obligation.status` kept its own `.default("pending")`, checked against migration `0001` rather than pattern-matched — that column really does carry one. **All five facts verified against the live schema through `information_schema`, not against the migration files alone.** Drift row 5 was correctly *not* treated as a docs item — it is B20, already shipped in migration `0031`. **The plan's ordering constraint for this step was inverted by merge order** ("must land before Step 11", but GAP-184/PR #113 merged first), so the `expense` block was reconciled against what GAP-184 had already written rather than against a clean base. `data-model.md` → v1.1.14, `docs/README.md` status table updated; `admin.test.ts` 15/15 against live Postgres exercises the real insert path — **Wave 8c, Step 9** |
| **GAP-183** | 24 Aug | **Three of the bell's summary rows had a real destination and no way to reach it** — worse than having none, since the sheet reads as a menu whose entries do nothing. `NeedsAttentionRow` gains an optional `onGo`: a row with a destination renders as a real `<button>` at M-1's 44px floor (`min-h-tap` — the old `py-3` row was under it), a row without one stays a plain `<div>` so nothing announces an action it cannot perform. **Rent due** → **corrected after review**: it first pointed at the receivables report per Q-4, but `rentDue` is filtered to `partyType === 'customer'` (driver arrears show elsewhere on Home) while `ReceivablesReportScreen` lists customers, drivers *and* partners — so the row's own figure could not match the screen it opened (1 customer at Rs 1,500 landing on a 2-row report totalling Rs 2,000). Newly introduced by wiring the row, since it had no destination before. The owner chose to send it to the Home section it actually summarises instead, where the two agree by construction — the same treatment "Earlier days" already had, and the same property that made the deposits row safe. `onOpenReceivables` was removed rather than left as dead surface. **Earlier days** → closes the sheet and scrolls to the Home section already rendering it, which is genuinely interactive (each card confirms a day) and so is a better destination than any new screen. **Deposits to release** → a new `/deposits-to-release` screen reading the same `GET /api/home/deposit-releases` Home uses, so the two can never disagree. **Q-4's premise for that third row was stale and the owner was asked rather than routed around**: it prescribed "one new minimal list screen" without noting that Home *already* renders a Deposits-to-release section with the identical fields — the owner chose **both**, so the screen exists *and* Home's own cards became tappable, and chose the same for rent due. **The compiler caught a real defect in the first draft**: `onSelectParty` was typed `"customer" \| "driver"`, but a receivable's `partyType` can be `partner`, which lives at `/partners/$userId` rather than under `/people` — widened, and the router's `useSelectParty` now routes all three. A deposit release is only ever a customer or a driver, a narrower set that stays assignable. Five new tests, each confirmed failing against the un-wired or pre-correction code before being trusted. **Two more review findings fixed the same pass, both introduced by this PR**: the new screen formatted `holdReleaseDate` with a year where Home's identical line omits it, on a screen whose own comment says it shows nothing Home does not; and M-1's *"≥ 8px apart"* was unmet between the newly-interactive bell rows — `min-h-tap` gave each its 44px height but separation was a 1px border with no `gap`. **The first draft argued a divided list was exempt from that half of M-1; neither `CLAUDE.md` nor `web/CLAUDE.md` carries such a carve-out, so it was retracted rather than defended** — inventing one in a code comment is exactly the "route around it silently" the root document forbids. **A `git checkout` intended to revert only that mutation reverted the whole file and destroyed the session's `HomeScreen.tsx` work** — rebuilt from the record and re-verified 17/17; recorded because the lesson is that `git checkout <path>` is not an undo for a one-line experiment, `git stash push -- <path>` is — **Wave 8c, Step 10** |
| **GAP-179** | 24 Aug | **Three performance defects (evaluation B26, B27, B29), and the row's own previous wording named a *fourth* thing that was never a defect.** It read *"including `day-card-generation`'s cron scan having no `business_id` filter at all"* — but `api/src/queries/scheduled.ts:18-25` answers that in advance: *"Every function here is deliberately **unscoped by `business_id`** … a scheduled job is not a request … A Cron Trigger fires once for the whole Worker and **must see every business to do its job at all**."* Adding that filter would have made `generate-day-cards` serve exactly one business and silently stop generating cards for every other one. **Second instance in this evaluation of a deliberate design read as an oversight**, after B28. **Built**: `B26` — `sumOverheadsForPeriod`, `sumOverheadsForDateRange` and `sumVehicleEarnedForDateRange` now sum in Postgres instead of fetching rows and reducing in the Worker (TS §7). `COALESCE(SUM(…), 0)` is load-bearing: `SUM()` over zero rows is NULL where the JS `reduce` returned `0n`, and a business with no overheads is a real Rs 0. The four call sites go through a new `singleAggregateTotal`, which **throws** on a missing row rather than `?? 0` — a bare aggregate always returns exactly one row, so its absence is a broken query, not missing data, and the repo's own W-56 lint rule caught the first draft writing `?? 0` and was right to. **B26's other three cited functions (`sumVehicleEarnedForPeriod`, `sumVehicleCostsForPeriod`, `sumVehicleCostsForDateRange`) have zero call sites** — checked, not assumed; GAP-145 replaced them with `…Bulk` variants and left them behind. Deliberately neither converted (optimising code that never runs buys nothing) nor deleted (each `…Bulk` function's doc comment explains itself *by reference to them*), with that reasoning written into the file. `B27` — `listAgeingBuckets` bucketed and summed in SQL; `asOfDate` stays a bound parameter cast `::date`, never `CURRENT_DATE`. `B29` — `generateDayCards` called `listAllocatedDatesForVehicle` once per active lease; new `listAllocatedDatesForVehicles` collapses that to one `vehicle_id IN (…)` read over the widest window, with each lease still reading only its own range. **B28 re-confirmed as the documented deliberate trade-off, untouched.** Three new ageing tests pin every bucket edge from both sides; teeth proved by mutation (shifting one `<=` by a day fails them with a readable message) rather than by red-then-green, since a behaviour-preserving refactor cannot fail against its own pre-image — **Wave 8c, Step 6** |
| **GAP-180** | 24 Aug | **Five hygiene defects plus one copy asymmetry** (evaluation B2, B4, B6, B7, B24). `B2` — `countAllocatedDaysForVehicle` counted voided allocations, overstating UC-79 utilisation; `voided_at IS NULL` added, the same D-11/W-58 filter its sibling ran/lost read already had under GAP-118. A day count, not money — no golden fixture moves. `B4` — **resolved by B27 rather than separately**: the `Date.parse` pair it named lived inside `listAgeingBuckets`, which Step 6 moved into SQL wholesale, so the fragility is gone with the code. `B6` — `profitPerKm`'s `Number(profitMinor)` was unguarded past `MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`; now degrades to `null`, **not** thrown the way `chartAxis.ts` does for the same overflow, because this field is already `number \| null` and already renders as "not available" (W-56/P11), so the honest answer has a home here and a mis-scaled chart bar has none. `B7` — the `WIRE` regex rejects `-0` (the one wire string that could not round-trip: `parse("-0")` is `0n`, `toWire(0n)` is `"0"`) **and nothing else**; leading zeros stay legal and `fromInput` stays deliberately more permissive, since wire format and human-typed decimals are different grammars on purpose. Two new `money.test.ts` cases, one pinning the rejection and one pinning every form still accepted. `B24` — the cited routes that genuinely can 400 now say so. **The plan said "the 4 valid citations only"; there are five.** `listVehiclesRoute` (no request schema) and `archiveVehicle`'s 409 (a bare idempotent `UPDATE`) are correctly dismissed, but `createMileagePackageRoute` from B24b has a real request body and can 400 — judged on its merits rather than dropped to match the count. A uniform sweep of *every* route-def declaring a request schema is deliberately **not** done here: it is a larger job than this row, and it leaves a known inconsistency (`getVehicle` documents its 400, `archiveVehicle` still does not). **Copy fix** — `SettleInsuranceClaimSheet`'s "Amount received" became "Received so far", matching `RecoveryReceivedSheet`'s identical field: `settleInsuranceClaim` **overwrites** `received_amount_minor` rather than adding to it, so the old label invited a second part-settlement to be typed as the instalment and would have replaced the running total with it. `CollectPaymentSheet`'s own "Amount received" is genuinely incremental and was left alone — **Wave 8c, Step 7** |
| **GAP-181** | 24 Aug | **No golden fixture exercised a voided row anywhere**, so W-50's void-and-replace — a core money rule — had no regression coverage against the three figures FL §9.1 calls breaking to move. **The deeper problem was not the missing row.** G-1's test *re-derives* its own totals with a hand-written query that had no `voided_at IS NULL` filter, and never touched `sumVehicleEarnedForPeriodBulk`/`sumVehicleCostsForPeriodBulk` — the production queries where such a bug actually lives. So seeding a voided row alone would have failed G-1 for the *test's* arithmetic rather than for anything the product did. **Fixed in both halves**: G-1 seeds a voided duplicate 12,000 repair and now also asserts the golden figure through `GET /api/reports/vehicle-month`, the real report; G-2 seeds a voided mistyped 700,000 body-work quote and keeps its 95,000/80,000/15,000 bottom line. **Both guards proven by mutation, not assumed**: dropping `isNull(expense.voidedAt)` from `sumVehicleCostsForPeriodBulk` moves G-1's reported costs 46,000 → 58,000 and fails loudly; dropping it from `sumIncidentCostMinor` moves G-2's repairs 95,000 → 795,000. Both mutations were reverted and `api/src/` confirmed byte-identical to `develop` — this gap is **test-only**. **G-3 deliberately gets none, for a structural reason rather than a judgment call**: `mileage_assessment` and `odometer_reading` carry no void trio at all (checked, not assumed), so no voided row can exist in that flow — a mileage figure is corrected by a later reading, not by voiding. Two timeouts raised (G-1 90s → 150s, G-2 default 20s → 60s) rather than trimming round trips: writing into a closed period has no shortcut, which is exactly what makes these fixtures worth having — **Wave 8c, Step 8** |
| **GAP-175** | 24 Aug | **The CSV export omits insurance claims, incident recoveries and write-offs entirely.** `listTransactionsForDateRange` (`api/src/queries/reports.ts:1608-1628`) pulls four sources only. A year with a Rs 250,000 insurer settlement and a Rs 40,000 write-off shows the accountant neither. Payments/adjustments/offsets deliberately stay out (they would double-count); deposits and advances are a separate judgment call. **Built 24 Aug 2026, and the plan's own three-source wording is one of the three that would have double-counted.** "Add insurance claims, incident recoveries and write-offs" reads as three new queries; implementing it that way reports **every insurer settlement twice**, because `fileInsuranceClaim` (`domain/incident.ts:620-640`) writes an `insurance_claim` *and* an `incident_recovery` with `source: 'insurer'` in one transaction, and `settleInsuranceClaim` then writes the identical `received_amount_minor` to both — the Rs 250,000 in this row's own motivating example. `source` is `CHECK (source IN ('customer','insurer'))`, exactly two values, and **both are already represented elsewhere**: the customer half raises an `obligation` of kind `customer_contribution`, the insurer half is the claim. So the fix adds `customer_contribution` to the existing `owed_to_us` kind list (which also buys the date, vehicle, void filter and W-35 period join for free — `incident_recovery` carries **no date column at all**, only period ids), plus two genuinely new sources: `insurance_claim` recognised on `received_on` per W-11 (expected money is not earned money) and `write_off` on `written_off_on`, void-filtered. **`incident_recovery` is deliberately never read as its own source** — recorded here rather than left for the next reader to "fix". A write-off is deliberately *not* netted against the obligation that earned it: rent in, write-off out, which is what nets to the truth over a year. Two integration tests against real Postgres, both confirmed failing red against the pre-fix code first; one asserts the settlement appears **exactly once** |

| **GAP-104** | 11 Aug (F4) | `useMobileHistoryDismiss` rebuilt as one module-level stack manager (`Sheet.tsx`'s sole caller, so every sheet in the client inherits it unchanged) instead of each `Sheet` independently pushing/popping `window.history` and listening for its own `popstate`. New coverage: `useMobileHistoryDismiss.test.tsx` (manager-level, incl. the handoff-nets-to-zero and topmost-only-closes invariants), `Sheet.test.tsx` (two real nested `Sheet`s, touch-emulated — this is the one that actually reproduced the nested-closes-parent bug pre-fix; the jsdom-level handoff check did not, confirmed, and says so in its own comment), `QuickAddSheet.test.tsx` (switched from a no-op `onOpenChange` that could never have caught this to a real `useState`-backed one), and a new Playwright project (`mobile-360x640-touch`, `hasTouch`/`isMobile`) plus `e2e/mobile-sheet-history.touch.spec.ts` — the existing e2e project never carried touch emulation, so this bug could not have reproduced there either. **Not yet closed**: a real iOS Safari / Android Chrome pass (F4's own acceptance checklist item), which no automated run in this environment can substitute for. **Superseded 16 Aug 2026** — GAP-134 found that this same `useMobileHistoryDismiss`/`SheetHistoryStack` mechanism, sound as it was for sheet-vs-sheet, still let a route `navigate()` race it; the whole mechanism (not just its bookkeeping) was replaced by the platform `CloseWatcher` API, which structurally cannot race anything because it never touches `window.history`. | **GAP-112** | 12 Aug, LT-11 | **Reproduced and fixed — the fixture doubt LT-11 raised did not hold.** Reported again in real usage on QA (a real phone photo, not a generated fixture), which already ruled out the "bad PNG fixture" theory the 11 Aug filing led with. Root cause confirmed by pulling the exact two R2 objects behind the report directly (`wrangler r2 object get`, bypassing the app): both were byte-for-byte valid, complete JPEGs — storage and the upload path were never at fault. The actual gap was on read: `ReceiptThumbnail` (`web/src/features/costs/ReceiptSheet.tsx`) trusted whatever `res.blob()` resolved with, so a network-truncated download (two ~350KB receipts fetched concurrently the instant the sheet opens is exactly the situation for it) resolved with no thrown error and silently became a broken `<img>` — `naturalWidth: 0`, the exact symptom both the 11 Aug QA session and this report saw. Fixed: `blob.size` is now checked against the `sizeBytes` already known from the attachment list before rendering, with a Retry affordance on mismatch matching the existing `PendingReceiptTile` pattern. Not a `Content-Length`-header check — that would false-positive if a response were ever compressed at the edge, since `Content-Length` reflects the wire size, not the decoded one. New tests: `ReceiptSheet.test.tsx` (new file). PR #28 |
| **GAP-117** | 12 Aug, M-30 | **`PhotoCapture`'s file input dropped `capture="environment"`, restoring the OS's own camera/gallery choice.** Found investigating GAP-112 (unrelated in cause, same component family) and confirmed as a real, previously-undiscussed gap rather than a deliberate decision: `capture` was chosen only for M-18's stated reason (avoiding `getUserMedia`'s iOS PWA permission unreliability), but on many mobile browsers it has a second effect — it skips the OS picker's own "Camera" vs. "Photo Library" choice and jumps straight to the camera, leaving no way to attach a photo that already exists. No product document requires a live-only photo — the evidentiary language in the use cases (W-18, W-30, W-38) is about a photo outweighing a verbal claim, never about how the photo entered the app. `docs/design/ui-ux-guidelines.md` v1.3.1 (M-30). New regression test in `PhotoCapture.test.tsx` pins that neither file input carries `capture`. PR #28 |
| **GAP-118** | 13 Aug (Wave 2) | `releaseDailyLeaseAllocationsAfter` + `voidFutureOpenDayRecordsForLease` (queries/dailyLease.ts, queries/day-record.ts), called from `changeDailyLeaseDriver` and `changeVehicleArrangement` (moving off B) right after the old row closes and before any replacement materialises. Voids the closed lease's future `vehicle_day_allocation` rows and its still-`open` `day_record` cards — deliberately not `paused_for_trip` ones, an adjacent question this fix doesn't answer since `changeDailyLeaseDriver` has no open-trip guard of its own. Reproduced end to end against the real endpoints, not just the read-side prerequisite: a future date inside the old lease's own horizon starts under driver 1, still `open`; after the change it is voided under driver 1 and refilled under driver 2. Surfaced its own prerequisite gap first — see GAP-120 |
| **GAP-119** | 13 Aug (Wave 2) | `releaseDailyLeaseAllocationsForRange` (queries/trip.ts), called from `bookTrip` before `insertAllocationDays` — frees the vehicle's daily-lease occupancy for the trip's own range so the two inserts never collide on `one_arrangement_per_vehicle_day`, D-12's "REPLACED, not refused" now actually true. `cancelTrip` re-materialises whatever the vehicle's *current* daily lease would occupy (looked up fresh, not the one active at booking time — GAP-118 may have superseded it since). Wave 0's own reproduction (`POST /api/trip` over an active daily lease → `409 VEHICLE_DOUBLE_BOOKED`) now returns `201`; the same scenario is the permanent regression test. **GAP-44 still sequences after this** — the enriched double-booked-error dialog is Wave 7's own item |
| **GAP-120** | 13 Aug (Wave 2, found closing GAP-118/119) | **`day_record` gained the W-58 void trio in migration `0022` "ahead of Wave 2" and nothing filtered it** — eight sites (`confirmOpenDayRecord`'s own idempotency guard, the close checklist, all three lost-day reports, the driver/unconfirmed-list reads, `pauseDayRecordsForTrip`) read or wrote the table with no `voided_at` awareness, latent only because nothing had ever voided a row yet. The sharpest of the eight: `confirmOpenDayRecord`'s `WHERE state = 'open'` could confirm a voided card and post real money against a day nobody covers any more, `state` being untouched by voiding. Closed ahead of GAP-118's own write path landing (Step 0 of the same session, not a separate gap discovered later) — the eight reads/writes filtered, plus a new domain check (`DayRecordVoidedError`, `DAY_RECORD_VOIDED`, 409) on the confirm path specifically, since filtering that one query would have broken `confirmDay.ts`'s own unique-violation-as-race-recovery contract. A new `check-forbidden.mjs` rule (`money/void-table-unfiltered`) guards the four tables this wave's own migration named as needing it — `vehicleDayAllocation`, `paymentAllocation`, `dayRecord`, `openingBalanceEntry` — deliberately not all twenty-two void-trio tables, most of which have no live void endpoint yet (A9b/Wave 5) and would have failed the gate on pre-existing, unvalidated reads this session never touched. Four new regression tests (checklist exclusion, confirm rejection, unconfirmed-list exclusion, lost-days exclusion), all run against the real `test` branch |
| **GAP-1** | 13 Aug (Wave 3) | Per-vehicle capability scoping, open since P7. `use-cases.md` **W-59** / `user-flows.md` **INV-34** / `data-model.md` **D-17** name the mechanism: `ownership_share` scopes an owner-manager's `managePartnerCapital` reads, `management_fee_agreement` (bound to period overlap) scopes a manager's UC-70. New `queries/vehicle-scope.ts`; UC-70 and the two capital-read handlers in `partner.ts` scoped; writes deliberately stay flat (a solo owner-manager must still be able to set up a first vehicle's split). The manager-invite guard lifted the same session — `manager` is a real, invitable role again |
| **GAP-121** | 13 Aug (Wave 3, found auditing GAP-1) | Four `managePartnerCapital` handlers in `partner.ts` checked membership, never role, and could let an owner name a `manager` as a partner (or, the mirror, `grantManagementHandler` could refuse an owner-manager legitimately managing a vehicle he doesn't own, UC-64 — the fix's own first draft got this wrong, caught by `partner-summary.test.ts`'s existing fixture failing red). Fixed at all four sites; a Wave 3 prerequisite, the same shape GAP-120 was for Wave 2 |
| **GAP-122** | 13 Aug (Wave 3, found auditing GAP-1) | `getAuditLogHandler`'s free-form `tableName` let a manager reach `capital_contribution`/`partner_payout` audit trails by another route, the two owners-only tables among the seventeen `auditedTableNameSchema` admits. Defence-in-depth (no listing endpoint hands a manager the UUID) — gated with `managePartnerCapital` on those two tables specifically |
| **GAP-137** | 19 Aug (platform-tier Phase 3, found reviewing `main.tsx` for it) | `main.tsx`'s 403 handler (the A11 demoted-user refetch) still invalidated `["me"]`, a key PR #75 had already stopped populating when `useMe()`/`<Can>`/`ReportsCatalogueScreen` moved onto `["session"]` — a silent no-op on `develop` for the ~2.5 hours between #75 and #76 merging. Fixed to invalidate `["session"]`. Never reached `main`. Full account in §0's 18–19 Aug entry |

### Recorded, unowned, and correct to leave

**Emptied as a category 11 Aug 2026.** Its eighteen rows moved into the table then called `Scheduled`, since renamed [Full accounts](#full-accounts--open-and-closed), above — as phase-1 work per the phase model — see that table for each one's own reasoning and what it now waits on. Two of the eighteen (GAP-21, GAP-28) are flagged rather than tracked there, because they are not build gaps: nothing in the codebase can manufacture a second business timezone or an on-call rotation. This heading is kept, empty, so the next pass finds the decision recorded rather than re-discovering the category is gone.

---

## 5. The traps

Things that cost a session each when rediscovered.

**Testing and environment**

- **`npm run check` does not run the api integration suite.** `api`'s `test` is `vitest run --project unit`; `test:integration` is separate and needs a live Neon branch. A green `check` says nothing about endpoint behaviour.
- **`mintUser(db, ctx, businessId, role)` takes the role as an explicit argument everywhere it is called — which means no test in this codebase has ever exercised the role a real signup actually gets.** GAP-42's bug (`createBusiness` granting `owner`, the one role with no screen) sat behind 386 green integration tests for exactly this reason: every one of them minted its `owner`/`manager`/`driver` directly rather than going through `POST /api/business` and reading back what it assigned. `business.test.ts`'s own happy-path test does call the real endpoint — and had asserted the wrong role as correct, so even the one test positioned to catch this had been pinned to the bug (the same shape as A9a's `voidExpense` regression test). Worth a standing question on any new setup/provisioning path: is there a test that reads back what the system decided, or only tests that assume a value and mint it directly?
- **The Neon branch has a connection ceiling.** Every integration file opens its own `Pool` and holds it to `afterAll`, so file-level parallelism opens all of them at once — `vitest.config.ts` sets `fileParallelism: false` for exactly this. On one long-lived interactive branch it still surfaces as `Connection terminated unexpectedly` at a **different, random** test each run. **A failure that moves between runs, in files the change never touched, is the environment.** Re-run the touched file alone before believing a regression. Web-P6d, Web-P7, Web-P8a and Web-P8b each hit it; P8a's session saw 15 failures across 7 untouched files, P8b's saw 2 in `auth.test.ts` that passed 9/9 alone.
- **A dedicated Neon branch plus `TEST_PARALLEL=1` cuts a full local `test:integration` run from ~29 minutes to ~105 seconds** — a ~16x speedup, measured both ways on the *same* branch (`test-parallel`, `br-lingering-violet-afr5li7f`, forked from `main`) so nothing else differs. `api/.dev.vars`'s `TEST_DATABASE_URL` now points at it; the old shared `test` branch is unused, not deleted. The env var sets `fileParallelism: true` with `poolOptions.threads.maxThreads: 4` (`vitest.config.ts`) — bounded well under the ~14-file ceiling above, so it doesn't reintroduce that failure mode. Opt-in and off by default; CI and anyone who hasn't set up a personal branch are unaffected.
- **The connection flakiness above can also surface as a foreign-key violation mid-cleanup, not just a raw timeout.** Found running `TEST_PARALLEL=1` twice in a row: run one failed 6 tests in `mileage-assessment.test.ts` (`billing_period_lease_id_fkey`), run two failed 2 in `day-record.test.ts` (`day_record_daily_lease_id_fkey`) — different file, different constraint, different count, both files 25/25 re-run alone. Same "moves between runs" signature as above, not a cleanup-ordering bug — checked `trackCreatedLease`'s teardown before concluding that, and it's correctly written (re-queries and deletes child rows before the parent, one sequential closure, no bug found). A dropped connection mid-closure can still leave a delete half-done; the fix is the one already established above, not a change to test support.
- **`audit_log` no-ops every `DELETE`** (`DO INSTEAD NOTHING`, migration `0001`). An `app_user` is permanent from the moment anyone acts. Test cleanup must not try — it will compile, run, and silently do nothing. The fix lives in `tests/support/auth.ts`/`factories.ts`; production only ever revokes `business_member`.
- **17 ESLint warnings are expected**, all `react-refresh/only-export-components` in `router.tsx` (15, one per route component — 14 until B10 added `StartDailyLeaseRoute`), `ApiContext.tsx` (1) and `AuthActionsContext.tsx` (1, added in B0 — the same context-with-a-hook shape as `ApiContext.tsx`). A direct consequence of the code-based-router decision plus that shared pattern. **The count rises by one with every new route and that is correct**; 0 *errors* is the bar. Do not "fix" these by splitting the route tree or the contexts.
- **`eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` is deliberately absent** — its published peer range caps at ESLint 8/9 and this repo is on ESLint 10. **axe-core in Playwright is the a11y gate** until it catches up.
- **Two test files sharing a base name with only the extension differing breaks typescript-eslint's `projectService`, reproducibly, with a "not found by the project service" parsing error — not an environment or caching artefact.** Found building B4: five screens each grew a `Screen.test.ts` (pure view-model functions) alongside the existing `Screen.test.tsx` (render tests), and every `.test.tsx` half of a same-stem pair started throwing, across separate process invocations, with `cwd` and the file's own existence both independently confirmed correct first (a red herring this session chased for several tool calls before testing the actual variable — file-stem collision — rather than the environment). `web/tsconfig.json`'s `include` glob covers both files individually; the crash is specific to the pair coexisting. **Fix: merge into the one file this codebase's own convention already uses** (one test file per screen, `StartLeaseScreen.test.tsx`'s own shape — pure-function `describe` blocks and render `test`s in the same file) — not a `tsconfig`/`allowDefaultProject` workaround, since the simpler fix is also the one that matches how every other screen here is already tested.
- **Neon's Free plan rejects an explicit `suspend_timeout` on branch creation outright** — a `412` with no error body surfaced by `create-branch-action`, even though the value passed (300s) matched the plan's own fixed default exactly. Confirmed against Neon's own plans doc: *"Can I disable scale-to-zero? Free: No, it's always enabled (5 min idle timeout)"* — the setting isn't configurable via the API on this plan at any value, matching or not. Cost three failed CI runs before the parameter itself was suspected (the first three failures were a separate, missing-secrets cause). Fixed by omitting it from `integration.yml` entirely; Free already suspends at 5 minutes regardless, so nothing is lost.
- **The free plan caps a project at 10 branches and starts failing branch creation around nine open PRs**, on *someone else's* PR, for a reason that looks unrelated (IG §9.2) — why `integration.yml` is one workflow, not two. Delete a hand-created scratch branch (a `migrate-check-*` from testing a migration manually) once its job is done rather than leaving it as permanent headroom loss.
- **`2822193` shipped with `npm run typecheck` broken** — `api/vitest.config.ts`'s `process.env.TEST_PARALLEL` trips `noPropertyAccessFromIndexSignature` (`tsconfig.base.json`), TS4111, since the index-signature access needs bracket notation (`process.env["TEST_PARALLEL"]`). `TEST_PARALLEL=1` had been run and verified, but plain `npm run check` apparently hadn't, on this one file. One-line fix; found and fixed at the start of A2 before `check` could pass at all.
- **Nothing pushed straight to a feature branch (`build/p0-foundation` included) has ever run through CI.** `checks.yml` and `integration.yml` both trigger only on `pull_request` into `develop`/`main`; a bare `git push` to any other branch triggers neither. Every gate result recorded in this document through B0 was a local run substituting for that, not a CI-confirmed one. **Changed 5 August 2026 (A9a):** stop running the full local integration suite at all — run only the touched file locally, push, and open a PR into `develop` so `integration.yml`'s fresh-ephemeral-branch-per-PR run carries the full-suite cost instead, in parallel with the next item. It is also strictly better at the one thing the shared branch keeps failing at: A9a's own verification, run against a throwaway branch created via the Neon MCP tools rather than `api/.dev.vars`'s long-lived one, came back **378/378 in one pass with zero connection flakiness** — the failure mode above is specifically a contention problem, and a branch nobody else is using has none.
- **The `api` integration count recorded in §1's gate table had drifted to a stale `388`** before A9a touched anything — corrected to the verified **378** (ground-truthed two ways: the full CI-equivalent run above, and a plain `grep -c` across every `tests/integration/*.ts` file). Worth a reminder that a number in this document is only as good as the run that last produced it; re-verify rather than carry one forward on trust when a full run is available anyway.
- **Mocked/stub-mode review cannot see `AuthGate` or `FirstRunGate`'s non-`AppShell` states, because the mock replaces the exact code path in question.** `VITE_AUTH_MODE=stub` — used by every test file and by `UI-UX-REVIEW.md`'s own Playwright pass — skips straight past sign-in. GAP-49 (6 Aug, live testing) lived on precisely that boundary and had already cost one near-miss: B8's own writeup flagged "one real browser round trip still unexercised" as an open risk and left it open. **Standing rule: anything touching `AuthGate`, `FirstRunGate`'s non-`renderOperate` branches, or any component that can render outside `AppShell`, needs one real-browser check, both colour schemes, before being called done.** Not a preference — mocked review is structurally incapable of catching this class.
- **Nobody had been reading the browser console as a standing step of any review, automated or manual.** It surfaced GAP-50 (a real `aria-hidden`-on-focused-ancestor WCAG violation in `Sheet.tsx`, live and reproducible) in under a minute, on the second sheet interaction tried. Cheap enough that there is no excuse to skip it — check console warnings/errors alongside any screenshot-based review from now on.
- **A route-def-by-route-def, screen-by-screen validation pass proves what exists matches spec — it cannot notice a flow that was never wireframed *and* never built**, because there is nothing on either side to diff. That is exactly how GAP-51 (F-1.7, "Set up the daily lease") went unbuilt through every prior pass: it has zero mentions in `ui-ux-guidelines.md` and zero implementation in `web/src`, so no comparison ever flagged a mismatch. None of this project's validation passes to date have enumerated `user-flows.md`'s full phase-1 flow list top-down against both documents at once. That audit hasn't run yet; F-1.7 falling through whole is reason to suspect it isn't the only one.
- **A migration comment can be right about its own object and wrong about the conclusion drawn from it — and it reads as authoritative either way.** `0003`'s header says revoked rows are excluded "so a revoked manager (F-1.4) can be re-granted". True of `0003`'s own index; false of the outcome, because `0001`'s `UNIQUE (business_id, user_id)` one migration above still blocks exactly that (GAP-52). The comment never looked up. **This is the third instance of the pattern §6 already names twice** — a stated reason that was accurate about the thing it examined and wrong about what it concluded. The habit that catches it: when a comment claims a *behaviour* is possible, check the other constraints on the same table, not just the one being added. It survived because nothing ever exercised the path — no endpoint has ever written `revoked_at`, so the claim was never tested against Postgres.
- **An accessibility assertion that watches for browser console warnings is worthless without `Accessibility.enable`, and it fails in the passing direction.** Headless Chromium computes the a11y tree lazily; with no client asking for it, checks like `aria-hidden`-on-focused-element never run and the warning is never emitted. The first version of `e2e/sheet-a11y.spec.ts` passed cleanly against code that was definitely broken. The fix is one CDP line (`await session.send("Accessibility.enable")`), but the habit that matters is the one that caught it: **before trusting a test that asserts the absence of something, make it fail on purpose.** Here that meant firing the exact violation by hand on `about:blank` and confirming the harness saw it — nothing, then two entries once a11y was enabled. Note also that `page.on("console")` alone is fine *once a11y is on*; the missing piece was never the listener.
- **`date AT TIME ZONE tz` and `timestamp AT TIME ZONE tz` are different overloads that produce different, silently-typed results — and "make it fail on purpose" catches a wrong fix, not only a missing one.** GAP-72's own suggested fix — `created_at >= $from::date AT TIME ZONE tz` — looked right and was wrong: Postgres upcasts the bare `date` to `timestamptz` at the **session's** zone first, then reinterprets that instant's wall-clock time in `tz`, landing eleven-plus hours off (`pg_typeof` even reported the result as `timestamp`, not `timestamptz` — a second, independent tell). Casting to a bare `::timestamp` first (genuinely naive, no zone attached) is what makes `AT TIME ZONE tz` read it as *that* wall-clock time *in* `tz`. **Verified directly against Postgres with a throwaway query, not assumed from the docs or from how the fix reads.** The discipline this confirms: running the new test against pre-fix code is necessary but not sufficient — run it against the *first-draft fix* too, before trusting that fix. Here, one of two new tests kept failing against the first draft; only the second run caught it.
- **`npm run typecheck` has now shipped broken twice, both times because the gate wasn't run before pushing.** `2822193` was the first (`api/vitest.config.ts`, TS4111). A0 was the second: `NewBusinessMember.role` was typed as the literal `"owner"`, so changing the one call site to `owner_manager` did not compile — found 7 Aug, a day after A0 shipped, by running `npm run check` for an unrelated reason. **The shape is worth naming: a type narrowed to the single value its only caller happens to pass.** It reads as precision and behaves as a tripwire on the next change; `role` is a set, and typing it as the set (`BusinessMemberRole`) is both more honest and less brittle. Note that no *test* failed either time — `test` and `typecheck` are separate scripts, and a green test run says nothing about whether the branch compiles.
- **A QA branch is only a meaningful live-verification target when it reflects what's actually merged.** `git log` is authoritative here, not the tracker's own "done" rows — A0 (`17da20d`) has been sitting on `build/p0-foundation`, unmerged into `develop`, since it shipped, so QA spent 6 August still running the pre-A0 signup path. Live-testing against a stale environment risks the exact failure it's meant to catch, inverted: a real fix reading as still-broken. Check `git log develop` (or the deployed commit) before trusting a live QA result to mean what it looks like it means.
- **A local branch ref is not the same fact as its remote-tracking ref, and `git fetch` only updates the second one.** Checking "has X been merged to `develop`" by diffing against a local `develop` that hasn't been checked out in days answers a question about a stale copy, not about what's actually on the remote — the exact inversion of the QA-staleness trap directly above, one layer lower in the stack. Diff against `origin/develop`, or fast-forward local `develop` first, before concluding a merge hasn't happened.
- **Before calling a single-writer column a bug, ask what the column is *for*.** A `grep` showing one writer proves one writer; it does not distinguish "everything else forgot to maintain this" from "this is a standing configuration and only one flow was ever meant to change it." GAP-54's first draft got that backwards and then went looking for live data to confirm the wrong reading — finding some, because a vehicle configured for daily lease with no daily lease yet *looks* like a stale figure and is in fact the ordinary case. The one-line answer was sitting in `createVehicle`'s own doc comment the whole time ("F-1.2 is what ever closes it"). **The distinguishing question is whether the column is derived from other rows or declared independently of them** — a derived one that nothing recomputes is a bug; a declared one with a single deliberate writer is a design, and its gap (if any) is the missing *flow*, not the missing writes.
- **An accessibility-tree snapshot taken immediately after a click can render before the async data it depends on has resolved, and the gap reads as real state.** Live-tested 7 Aug: a snapshot taken right after clicking a sheet's close button appeared to show a driver record that hadn't existed a moment before — momentarily indistinguishable from having just created one by accident. The network log settled it: the one `GET /api/driver` request for that page load, timestamped *before* the interaction in question, already carried that row in its response body — the earlier "0" was the query's loading state rendered as a fallback, not its resolved one. **Before concluding a UI action wrote something, check the network log for the actual request, not just the next snapshot** — this is the same discipline TRACKER already keeps for a11y console warnings (§5, `Accessibility.enable`) and it generalises to any assertion made from a point-in-time render rather than the data source itself.
- **A verification script nobody schedules can be wrong for over a week with nothing red anywhere.** `docs/engineering/fixtures/golden.py` — the actual script behind the "39 of 39" claim both `CLAUDE.md` and DM §16.1 make — stopped running on 1 August 2026, the day migration `0004` added `business_id NOT NULL` to `deposit_movement`, `insurance_claim` and `incident_recovery`; its own inserts into those three tables were never updated to match. Found 9 August verifying GAP-70/71's new DM §15 queries, purely because that pass happened to re-run it. **Deliberately not wired into CI** (`docs/README.md` calls it "supporting material"; IG §9.2's branch-count ceiling is part of why), so nothing else would have caught this either. The three protected figures (`134,000` / `15,000` / `7,500`) were never actually at risk — only the ability to prove them was, silently, for eight days. Fixed with the missing column on all three inserts; re-run confirms 39/39 (DM §16.1, DM v1.1.2). **A second, smaller gap found the same pass and left as found**: the script never writes a `business_member` row at all, so DM §15's held-per-partner query has nothing to select against it — worth fixing alongside the next thing that touches this file, not urgent enough to hold this one up.
- **`api` integration test files outside the ones a change directly touches can still depend on that change** — checking impact by filename match (touch `lease.ts` → re-verify `lease.test.ts`) misses siblings that hit the same endpoint under a different name. GAP-84 (F1, 8 Aug) added an arrangement check to `POST /api/lease`; the F1 session re-verified `lease.test.ts` and `dailyLease.test.ts` and called it done, but `lease-closure.test.ts` and `mileage-assessment.test.ts` (G-3's own fixture file) both also call that endpoint through their own local helpers and neither was touched. Invisible locally — nothing runs the full `api` integration suite by default (§1) — and invisible in the F1 session itself for the same reason; only surfaced 9 Aug when `integration.yml` ran for real on the PR and failed 10 assertions, all `lease-closure.test.ts:52`, all `expected 409 to be 201`. **The search that should have run was "every call site of the endpoint", not "every file with a matching name."** `grep -rl` for the route string across `api/tests/` found both missed files in one pass; fixed by giving all 21 affected vehicles the same `setVehicleArrangement(vehicleId, "A")` the two originally-checked files already had.
- **The connection-flakiness trap above (§5, "the Neon branch has a connection ceiling") got materially worse this session, not just recurring** — full-suite runs of `trip.test.ts`, `lease.test.ts` and `business-member.test.ts` hit multi-*minute* stalls (one run: 985,847ms on a 14-test file; another: 3,044s on `trip.test.ts` alone) rather than the instant `Connection terminated unexpectedly` the original trap describes, with a **different** test failing each retry and every failure clearing on an isolated or repeat run. Same root cause, same diagnostic ("moves between runs, in files the change never touched, is the environment" — do not chase it as a regression), but severe enough on this particular branch that **`TEST_PARALLEL=1` (already documented two bullets up) is worth reaching for by default now, not just as a known option**, and a single touched-file-plus-siblings run is cheaper to trust than a full-suite one when the branch is in this state.
- **`TEST_DATABASE_URL`'s own migration bookkeeping can silently fall behind the schema its tests actually need, and the two failure modes look identical from a test's own output.** Found 9 Aug: `business-member.test.ts` showed 4 failing tests (`POST /api/business-member/invite` and `.../change-role` all 500ing) that looked exactly like a pre-existing code bug — same shape as a dozen other "flaky/pre-existing" findings this session. `npm run migrate:status` (with `DATABASE_URL` overridden to `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, never the bare command — see IG §8.3) showed migration `0010` still `PENDING` on this branch despite being merged and deployed weeks ago; `business_member_invite` genuinely did not exist (`to_regclass` returned `null`). Applying it (`npm run migrate`, same override) fixed 3 of the 4 failures instantly, leaving only a genuine connection timeout. **Check `migrate:status` against `TEST_DATABASE_URL` before spending time diagnosing a cluster of same-shaped integration failures as a code regression** — a stale disposable branch (the `.dev.vars` comment on this one records it was created fresh, "test-parallel", specifically to dodge the contention trap two bullets up) is a real, ordinary explanation and costs one query to rule out.

**Writing code**

- **A U-3 prefill that crosses a direction-of-money boundary is the one prefill that must never be written, and this project has now reached for it twice.** U-3 ("sensible defaults, pre-filled") is a good rule that becomes a money bug the moment the source field points the opposite way from the target. The live case: `driverDayFeeMinor` (`packages/shared/src/schemas/driver.ts:23`) is money **you pay him**; `dailyLeaseAmountMinor` is money **he pays you**. Prefilling the second from the first seeds a plausible, wrong, unremarked figure into every record created. **This is not a hypothetical — UC §2's UC-04 note records that v1.1 of the specification itself called both a "per-day rate", and renaming them apart was a deliberate correction.** Plan.md's B10 write-up then re-proposed exactly the merge v1.1 had been corrected for, six days after the vocabulary lock was written down; it was caught by reading the schema rather than by any check. **Standing rule: before wiring any prefill, name the direction of both fields out loud. If they differ, there is no default — leave it empty.** The vocabulary lock (UI §9.6) exists to make this visible in copy; nothing yet makes it visible in code, which is why it needs to be a habit.
- **A bare single-statement write to an audited table records `changed_by` as `NULL`.** `withActor` wraps `.transaction`, so a write that never opens one is invisible to it. P9 fixed nine such functions by wrapping them even where atomicity was not otherwise needed. **Any new write to a `posted_period_id`-carrying table must open a transaction.**
- **`payment.amount_minor` has `CHECK (> 0)`** — a full reversal can never zero it. `status = 'reversed'` carries "nothing here counts"; the wire response reports the true remaining figure separately.
- **`tailwind-merge` does not know this project's scale.** Any new `--text-*` or spacing token must be added to `theme.text`/`theme.spacing` in `web/src/lib/cn.ts` or it is silently dropped next to a real colour class. Regression test in `cn.test.ts`.
- **`ownership_share` can never hold two open (`effective_to IS NULL`) sets for one vehicle — a second `POST /api/ownership-share` for a vehicle that already has a set always 400s.** `assert_shares_total` (migration 0001) sums every row whose date range contains the *new* row's own `effective_from`; a still-open first set's `[effective_from, ∞)` range contains any later date, so the total hits 200% and the trigger rejects it before a second set can ever exist. **This means `effective_to IS NULL` alone is a correct and sufficient "current split" filter** — not a coincidence, the deferred constraint's whole job — and a test that tries to `POST` a second set to prove otherwise needs a direct `db.insert` instead (found writing A2's `listOwnershipShares`, `partner.test.ts`'s own comment records the reasoning).
- **A `GENERATED ALWAYS` column reads back nullable through Drizzle even though Postgres never actually leaves it null.** `banking_event.discrepancy_minor` (`recorded − counted`, migration 0001) is one; a raw `SELECT` of it types as `bigint | null` in Drizzle regardless of the `NOT NULL` guarantee the two source columns give it. Defaulting the gap with `?? 0n` is exactly the W-56 pattern the lint rule exists to catch (and it caught it) — compute the figure from the two real columns instead, the same way `recordBankingEvent` (domain/partner.ts) already does on write, rather than trusting the generated column on read.
- **"A row exists for this idempotency key" is not the same fact as "this action already happened", the moment something *other* than the action itself can create that row (GAP-3).** `confirmDay` was idempotent on `(daily_lease_id, business_date)` since P3, correctly, right up until P13 added a second writer (`generate-day-cards`) that inserts the same row in a placeholder `open` state before the user ever acts. The idempotency check kept matching on existence alone and started silently no-op'ing real taps against real money. The fix — and the pattern for the next table that grows a second writer — is to gate the no-op on the row's own **state**, not its presence: a row in a non-terminal/placeholder state is the write path (an `UPDATE`, guarded on that same state in the `WHERE` clause so a concurrent racer's write affects 0 rows instead of throwing), and only a row already in a terminal state is the genuine no-op. Worth checking any other `find-then-return-if-exists` idempotency guard against whether a cron can now produce that same row ahead of the user — `day_record` was one of at least two tables (`vehicle_day_allocation` is the other, cron-only, no user-facing write competes with it today) P13 turned into a second writer.
- **A new invariant trigger firing on `INSERT` "because the reference implementation does" is a copy of the wrong half of the pattern.** `assert_business_has_owner()`'s (A11/INV-31) first draft matched `assert_shares_total`'s exact shape — `AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE`, deferred — and broke roughly a dozen existing integration-test fixtures that mint a `manager` as a business's sole member, because an `INSERT` can only ever *add* a row and can therefore never by itself take an active-owner count to zero; only an `UPDATE` that sets `revoked_at` can. `assert_shares_total` genuinely needs `INSERT` (a 60/40 split is built from inserts), which is exactly why copying its trigger *shape* without re-deriving which statement types can actually violate the *new* invariant produced a check that rejected valid, unrelated writes for no protective benefit. Ask "which statement type can actually cause the bad state" before choosing `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, or both — never default to "both" because a nearby trigger does.

**Three form bug classes, fixed structurally — every new form inherits the fix**

Found in P2 and fixed once each, at the structural level rather than per-form. A new form that hand-rolls around any of them is reintroducing a solved bug:

1. **Wire/domain mismatch on any zod schema with `.transform()`** (`moneyWireSchema`, `businessDateSchema`) — its `z.infer` output type differs from its `z.input` wire type, but `MoneyField`/`DateField` produce and consume the *domain* type. The fix: a form-local schema built from `z.custom<Minor>`/`z.custom<BusinessDate>` with no transform, keeping form state in domain types throughout, converting to wire via `toWire()` **only inside `mutationFn`**.
2. **`""` vs `undefined` on optional text inputs** — a native `<input>` always yields `""` when untouched, but `z.string().min(1).optional()` only skips validation for `undefined`. Fixed once in `lib/optionalTextField.ts`'s `blankToUndefined`, spread into `register(name, …)`.
3. **A cross-field error hidden inside a collapsed `Disclosure`** — W-55's error attaches to a level-2 field, invisible on a failed submit. Fixed by `Disclosure`'s `forceOpen?: boolean`, driven by `errors.fieldName !== undefined`.

**Migrations** — forward-only and numbered. `0001` DDL · `0002` audit trigger · `0003` single active membership · `0004` `business_id` on seven audited child tables · `0005` odometer `(lease_id, read_on)` partial unique · `0006` **`assert_period_open()` only fires on `INSERT` or an `UPDATE` that actually changes `posted_period_id`** — settling a claim or obligation months later is legal and was being rejected · `0007` `expense.voided_by` · `0008` **narrows `0006`'s exception to exclude a void** (`voided_at` `NULL → NOT NULL`) — GAP-35, A9a · `0009` widens `obligation_kind_check` to admit `trip_fare` — A6 · `0010` **`business_member`'s pair-unique made partial** (GAP-52), an explicit audit trigger (GAP-53), `business_member_invite`, **INV-31** (`assert_business_has_owner()`, `AFTER UPDATE` only — see §5 above for why not `INSERT`) — A11. Run `npm run check:drift` against both databases after any of them.

## 6. What validation corrected

**Compacted 10 August 2026** — nine passes (4–9 August) covering roughly 65 claims across ten external documents became the table below; the full prose, including every rejected claim's own reasoning, is in git history. **The standing lessons these passes taught are what earn a permanent line** (kept in full, below the table) — the pass-by-pass narrative that produced them doesn't need to stay narrative to keep teaching it. **The tenth and eleventh passes stay in full**, further down — 9–10 August, still the most recently actioned material.

| Pass | Date | Validated | New, real | Rejected (why) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1st (unlabelled) | 4 Aug | This plan's own first draft | GAP-33 (`GET /api/expense` no caller, deliberate) | Root `CLAUDE.md`'s "documentation only" claim — stale, not this file's to fix |
| 2nd | 5 Aug | A2/A3/B2/B3 before building | GAP-37 (`/more` the only door to four screens, → B0), GAP-38 (`GET /api/payment` missing, → A3) | — |
| 3rd | 6 Aug | `UI-UX-REVIEW.md` | GAP-44…48 (→ B9) | §7.3's `DialogConfirmFooter` default-text claim (doesn't compile without a value); §7.2's "excess rate per km" (the spec's own term, not a vocabulary-lock violation) |
| 4th | 7 Aug | This session's own GAP-54 draft, an hour after writing it | F-1.2 unbuilt (real) | GAP-54's original claim that `vehicle_arrangement` "never reflects reality" — the column is a standing configuration, not a derived state; the cited live data was the *correct* case (B10's own target) |
| 5th | 7 Aug | `MUST-FIX-FINDINGS.md`, web + backend editions | GAP-56…60 (GAP-56 the one real new money defect either edition produced) | §6's "`/more` only has sign-out" (deliberate, not an omission) |
| 6th | 7 Aug | "0b"'s own premise | — | The premise itself: production `business_member` was never checked, and turned out to be zero rows — nothing to fix |
| 7th | 8 Aug | The 8 Aug QA rerun | GAP-83 (worse than reported — `DateField` itself, not one screen) | "Close month opens with all-zero status" — U-7 (warn, don't block) working as designed |
| 8th | 8 Aug | `QA-COMPREHENSIVE-TEST-FINDINGS-2026-08-08.md` | GAP-84, GAP-85, GAP-86 | Sheet-clickability and keyboard-activation claims — both traced to the reporter's own broken browser-automation transport, not the app |
| 9th | 9 Aug | Four documents at once (BE/RC/QA-refresh/UI-LF, ~40 claims) | GAP-88…98 except GAP-95, plus **GAP-90** (a live 500 chased two documents stopped short of) | GAP-95 (Disclosure "More" labels — UI §6.3's own spec); re-raised keyboard/sheet claims (unchanged since 8th pass); BE-12 (reviewer's own credentials issue, not a repo defect) |

**The standing lessons, kept permanently:**
- **A gap's stated reason is a fact with a date on it.** GAP-13's "indistinguishable from an unscheduled day" expired the moment day-cards existed; GAP-23 and GAP-1 (via A11) are the same shape. Re-check a deferred gap's reason before carrying it forward, not just its existence.
- **A plausible claim stated with the confidence of a checked one is the single most common failure mode across every pass.** GAP-54's withdrawn first draft, "0b"'s unchecked production premise, and three of the ninth pass's re-raises are all this same shape: inference dressed as verification. The fix is always the same — check the thirty-second thing before trusting the inference.
- **`docs/` outranks a review.** Three separate external reviews have each proposed relabelling something the specification names on purpose (UI-UX-REVIEW's "excess rate per km" and `DialogConfirmFooter`, the QA rerun's Disclosure labels) — declined every time, on the record, so the fourth report finds the answer instead of re-litigating it.
- **A review independently rediscovering this tracker's own queue is a real signal, not a coincidence to wave off** — the fifth pass matched an existing gap or "correct to leave" row on all but five of its ~25 claims, from a completely different reading angle.
- **Reading every available document is not the same as reading the right ones.** The ninth pass's four documents found eleven real gaps; two more documents sitting in the tree unread turned out to be near-total duplicates of those four, except for one that changed the axis it looked along entirely (contracts *between* layers, not code *inside* one) — the tenth pass, below, in full.

---

### A tenth pass, 9 August 2026 — `API-CONTRACT-AUDIT-FINDINGS` in full, and it found a defect this session shipped

The ninth pass read `API-CONTRACT-AUDIT-FINDINGS` only far enough to confirm it duplicated the others, pulling one finding out of it (GAP-100). **Read in full it has two more, and the first is the most useful thing any of the six documents produced.** `BACKEND-API-QUERY-EVALUATION` was re-checked and is byte-identical to the copy validated in the ninth pass — BE-01…BE-12 stand as triaged there, no change.

24. **ACA-01 is real, and it is understated.** It reports that nine read screens branch on `query.data === undefined` without checking `query.isError`, so a failed fetch renders as loading. **The true count, verified against source 10 Aug 2026, is 30 of 32 files with a real `useQuery` call, 81 of 83 call sites** — `isError` is checked against a *query* in exactly two files in the entire client (`FirstRunGate`'s `meQuery` and `CloseMonthScreen`'s `checklistQuery`), both hand-rolled. (The "50 of 52" figure first recorded here counted every file importing anything from `react-query`, including 27 that only call `useQueryClient` for post-mutation invalidation and have no read to guard — narrower by file, worse by call site than first thought.) What hid it is that **mutation error handling is genuinely good and everywhere**: those 27 files are almost all of what a naive `grep isError` finds. So the pattern reads as present and is absent exactly where reads happen. Filed as **GAP-101**. **Three things worth stating plainly rather than burying**: this is the structural cause behind GAP-90 — the revoked-member 500 is invisible *because* of it, and fixing GAP-90's client half alone fixes one screen of thirty-two; **eight of the nine screens the audit names are B4 Wave 1's, built in this same session, gate-green, and reviewed twice without anyone noticing there was no error path** — a screen with no error branch passes every test that only ever mocks success, which is what all of them did; and **the eternal spinner turned out to be the least harmful of the three failure modes actually present** — a `?? []`/`?? 0` fallback on a failed read (`HomeScreen` renders "Nothing needs you today" while the Worker is down) and a failed read silently deleting a safety warning (`StartLeaseScreen`/`BookTripScreen`'s expired-paperwork strip, `VehicleCalendarScreen`'s INV-1 grid) are both worse, and neither was named until the 10 Aug implementation plan.
25. **ACA-08's no-caller list is now mostly closed or deliberately phase-2.** The B2 partner/banking callers, the in-app member invite screen, GAP-67's mileage package, GAP-100's advance issue/settle path, and GAP-102's vehicle-paperwork write all landed by 11 Aug. Still deliberately unbuilt from that list: phase-2 write-off and post-closure charge (GAP-15). The remaining lessons are not caller absence: GAP-1 policy scoping, ownership-share replacement after an existing current split, and management-agreement API role hardening.
26. **ACA-06's second half is real but already owned, and the audit is right that the ownership is ambiguous.** `DriverDetailScreen` never calls `GET /api/driver/{id}/view` — A5's staff-facing driver history read has no caller. TRACKER has recorded that as "rendering is B5" since 5 August, but **B5 is the driver's *own* Mine shell over `GET /api/driver-view`, a different endpoint for a different audience**, so the staff history has been sitting inside an item that was never going to build it. Clarified against B5 in `Plan.md` rather than filed as a new gap, since the work is small and genuinely belongs next to B5's own reading of the same data.
27. **Everything else in ACA maps onto an existing row**: ACA-02 = GAP-88 · ACA-03 = GAP-92 (which the audit usefully widens to `vehicleWindowQuery` and the client's own search params, both now named in that row) · ACA-04 = GAP-93 (adding that `route-defs/payment.ts` documents its 404 as "no such customer or driver", omitting partner — the documentation drifted with the validation) · ACA-05 = GAP-100 · ACA-07 = GAP-22/B6 · ACA-09 = GAP-98 · ACA-10 = GAP-89 · ACA-11 = GAP-88's empty-state half · ACA-12 = the same stale-credentials non-finding rejected as BE-12.

**What the tenth pass changes about the ninth's own verdict.** The ninth pass concluded that six documents produced one finding between them that the four did not, and that reading all six was mostly re-reading the same claims. **That was right about the other five and wrong about this one** — `API-CONTRACT-AUDIT-FINDINGS` is the only document of the six that audits *contracts between layers* rather than code in one layer, and that is exactly the seam GAP-100, GAP-101 and GAP-102 all sit in. The lesson is not "read everything" but "a document that changes the axis it looks along is worth reading even when its summary table looks familiar."

---

### An eleventh pass, 10 August 2026 — a live browser session, not a source read, and the first one with a report to check live data against

`LIVE-BROWSER-FINDINGS-2026-08-10.md`, run against `qa.fleetsettle.com` under Chrome DevTools Protocol — real Chromium, both an ordinary desktop context and, for the first time, a touch/coarse-pointer viewport emulation verified against `matchMedia('(pointer: coarse)')` before anything was tested against it, so it faithfully reproduces the condition the mobile-history bug class is actually gated on rather than just resizing a window. **Two things made this pass possible that the 8 August live sessions didn't have**: B4 Wave 2 gave it a reports catalogue to cross-check committed data against, and `MOBILE-SHEET-AND-DATE-PICKER-FINDINGS-2026-08-09.md` existed as a source-only audit nobody had yet stood up a real touch context to confirm or reject.

28. **The opening-balance finding did not come from looking for a bug — it came from checking a number the previous session had already committed live, against a report that didn't exist yet when it was committed.** The 8 August session (`docs/qa/live-test-plan.md`'s LT-5) confirmed a `customer_due` and a `deposit_held` opening-balance entry live and noted, correctly at the time, that verifying they "appear as opening positions and never as income" was "only fully checkable once B4 exists." B4 exists now. Checking is what found **GAP-103**: neither entry appears anywhere, because `commitOpeningBalance` never wrote them into a table anything else reads. **This is the same class of lesson as GAP-13 and GAP-23** (§6 already names it twice) — a gap's standing reason is a fact with a date on it — except here the reason wasn't "not reachable yet," it was "not yet possible to check," and the moment it became possible to check, the answer was the worst one available: not a wrong number, an absent one presented as confirmed.
29. **Three claims from the 9 August mobile-sheet audit were converted from source reads into directly reproduced defects, and one could not be tested at all — for a reason worth recording as its own fact.** MP-02 (Quick Add) and MP-03 (its sibling call sites) reproduced exactly as the source audit predicted, independently, on three structurally distinct screens. MP-04 (nested pickers) reproduced too, and worse than documented — the source audit predicted a parent sheet closing; live, the entire in-progress form state (a party selection and an amount) was discarded with it, no error. **MP-08 (the `Dialog`-in-`Sheet` stacking watch item) could not be tested, and not because of a tooling limit**: reading `AdvanceSheet.tsx` to find a way to seed its precondition found that the client has no way to attach an advance to a trip at all — a gap in its own right, folded into **GAP-100** rather than filed separately, since it's the same functional area found incomplete a second, deeper way.
30. **The cash-position mislabelling is the same shape GAP-75 already taught this queue to watch for, in a screen that shipped ten days after GAP-75 closed.** `CashPositionReportScreen.test.tsx` asserts "held for customers" as the correct rendering of a number the server explicitly, deliberately, does not attribute to any party type — the test encodes the assumption instead of checking it. **Filed as GAP-106.**
31. **Everything else checked live either confirmed a prior fix or found nothing.** LT-7/GAP-3 is now closed in full — the two-visit cron test that had sat open since 6 August finally got its second visit, and the placeholder the cron produced confirmed correctly on the first tap, all three money facts written in one transaction. GAP-84's arrangement gate and GAP-85's blank-vs-zero fix both held under conditions slightly harder than their original tests — direct URL navigation for the former, a fresh trip form for the latter. Dark mode was checked across every screen this pass touched and found genuinely improved since 8 August (the voided-expense treatment now carries a badge and a border, not only strikethrough text) with nothing new wrong.

**What this pass is worth, stated fairly, the way every pass before it has been:** it produced one finding (GAP-103) more severe than anything in this tracker's 106 gap ids, and it produced it by doing the one thing eight prior source-only and live passes had no way to do — committing real data through a real write path and then checking a real report against it, weeks after both existed. The lesson is not "live-test more" in the abstract; it's the same one LT-5's own 8 August note already stated in advance, and was simply too early to act on: **a verification blocked on a screen not existing yet is not the same as a verification that happened.**

### A twelfth pass, 10 August 2026 — a consolidation document, not a review, checked the same way a review gets checked

`PENDING-REVIEW-ITEMS-2026-08-10.md` is a different shape from the first eleven passes' subjects: not a fresh external review finding new defects, but a claimed *consolidation* of the seventeen review documents accumulated since 6 August, each checked against source/tracker and re-filed as one checklist, with the seventeen originals already removed from the working tree by the time this pass opened it. **The consolidation's own claims were checked, not assumed** — the same standing rule this section has run on since the first pass, applied one layer up: a document that says "I already validated these" is itself a claim, not a fact.

32. **The file-removal claim checked out.** All seventeen paths the disposition table names as "deleted after consolidation" were confirmed absent from the working tree and present in `git show HEAD:<path>` — genuinely removed, genuinely recoverable, nothing silently lost. `git status` showed all seventeen as unstaged deletions plus the new file as untracked, meaning none of this had been committed yet; this pass is what gives it a tracker record before that happens.
33. **Nearly every checklist item mapped onto a gap id this file already carries, at more depth than the consolidation's own line.** Spot-checked against source rather than trusted: GAP-45's title-truncation claim, GAP-91's missing `variant`, GAP-89's four-entry tab map, GAP-96's missing aggregate, GAP-97's missing menu action, GAP-55's zero `autoComplete` hits, and GAP-99's leaked "presigned upload storage" copy all confirmed exactly as both this file and the consolidation described them — nothing here was stale.
34. **Five items were re-raised as active work without noting they were already filed `Recorded, unowned, and correct to leave`** (GAP-5, GAP-7, GAP-8, GAP-21, GAP-68) — the same shape GAP-95 already established a precedent for (re-raising a declined item doesn't make it live by itself), except these were never declined so much as sized and parked. Evaluated individually rather than reflexively re-parked: GAP-7's reframing ("stop implying a state exists, if not building it") is a genuine improvement worth keeping for whoever picks it up; the other four stand exactly where they were, with the re-raise now recorded against each row so a fourth report finds the answer instead of re-litigating it (§4).
35. **Nine of B9's rows were small enough, and unambiguous enough about what "done" means, to build the same sitting rather than only record.** GAP-45, GAP-46, GAP-55, GAP-89, GAP-91, GAP-96, GAP-97 and GAP-99 all closed 10 Aug — full accounts in their own §4 rows — plus M-25's vocabulary fix, which rode along with GAP-97 since both touched the same lease-term forms. **What stayed unbuilt and why**: GAP-44 needs a wire-schema change (the error-enrichment decision from the 10 Aug B9 unblock, §0) and GAP-47/GAP-48/GAP-83/GAP-105 each touch a shared primitive or need their own sitting — none of the five are a same-pass extension of what nine straightforward call-site fixes already covered.
36. **One test failure surfaced a real race in the new test, not in the code under test — worth recording as its own trap.** GAP-46's first draft asserted `aria-label` on a calendar cell immediately after `await screen.findByText("July 2026")` — but the month label renders synchronously while the day grid waits on the calendar query, so the assertion sometimes ran before real day data replaced the empty default. Fixed by warming up on the first cell's own glyph first (`findByText`, async), the same pattern the neighbouring "seven day-states" test already used. Filed in §5 as its own trap.

**What this pass is worth:** the consolidation held up — seventeen files' worth of findings really did reduce to one file without losing anything this tracker didn't already have a home for, and the five re-raised items are now harder to lose a second time. The nine builds are the more ordinary kind of progress this tracker mostly records; the validation is what makes them safe to trust without re-reading seventeen deleted files first.

The other sixteen stay correctly retired.

## Not in this tracker

UC §9.1 phase Third and UI §15 phase Third, listed so their absence is a decision: depreciation and disposal · driver retainers and spare-vehicle reassignment · loan and lease schedules · tax, if it applies · offline capture of photos · the desktop analytical dashboard beyond UI §14's three changes.

## Blocked

| Blocked | Waiting on |
|---|---|
| **P14** — messaging | Twelve Meta template approvals (6 messages × 2 languages), minutes to ~2 days each. `dispatch-messages`, the fifth Cron Trigger, waits with it. **17 Aug 2026 — deferred entire by owner decision, and the approvals were deliberately *not* fired.** Both this tracker and [Plan.md](Plan.md) had recommended submitting them regardless on the grounds that they queue and cost nothing; the owner declined, on the same reasoning applied to W-4 and W-2 the same sitting — **decide on evidence rather than pre-commit to a shape that may not survive contact with real use.** That reasoning is stronger here than the "fire them, they're free" argument it overrode, because `use-cases.md` §8 already records W-14 (fully automatic messaging) as one of three decisions it expects a good reviewer to attack, **and assisted sending would need no approved templates at all** — so firing twelve approvals now would have quietly locked in the very decision §8 says to keep questioning. **Nothing external is therefore in flight, and phase 2 starts from a genuinely open question, not a half-committed one** |

Nothing else on either track waits on anyone outside this repository.

**Read this before writing "blocked externally" against anything again.** Real Asgardeo sat in this table for months described as "~10 minutes; blocks nothing else." The console change *was* ten minutes. The client half — SDK, PKCE flow, callback, sign-in screen, a real token getter — was unbuilt, unsized, and blocked **everything**: nobody could log in to a deployed build at all. The label described the blocker's cost and said nothing about the work behind it, and it kept the item ranked last for exactly that reason. P14 carries the same label today; its Meta approvals are genuinely external, but `dispatch-messages` and six message templates are not, and no one has sized them either.

## Standing questions this tracker has asked and had answered

**Six open questions put to the owner and answered, 17 August 2026 — the first sitting in this project where the answer to most of them was "not yet".** Five of the six deferred to evidence rather than deciding now, and the consistency is the point: production holds zero rows, so any judgement about how the business actually behaves would have been a guess dressed as a decision. **GAP-65** (the share link) → deferred out of phase 1, amending the "no phase-1 gap left unfixed" premise, the first time that premise has been bent and it is recorded as such in the gap's own row. **GAP-18/UC-99** (export) → CSV ships, PDF splits out as **GAP-136** and defers. **GAP-135** (settlement rhythm) → neither built nor left latent: the write path now refuses, so it cannot misreport while it waits. **P14** → deferred entire, **approvals not fired**, against this tracker's own standing recommendation (see [Blocked](#blocked) for why the owner's reasoning beat it). **The release gate** → all four live-test items must pass, so the last thing standing between this build and production is evidence rather than code. **W-4 and W-2** → parked, but with real conditions attached rather than "after real use":

| Decision | Revisit when | Why this trigger |
|---|---|---|
| **W-4** — no run means no charge | the first **three closed months containing at least one `did_not_run` day** | The argument turns on one number nobody has yet: how many idle days there really are. **This one has a deadline the other does not** — it is a term in the driver's agreement, not a display rule, so changing it after a second driver signs means renegotiating with him |
| **W-2** — the two driver balances never netted | the first **closed month in which an `offset_record` was actually used** | That is the moment someone has felt the friction and chosen to net two balances deliberately. If a month closes with no offset ever recorded, the friction is theoretical and the rule costs nothing. Presentation only, safe to change any time |

**Conditions, not dates, and deliberately so** — `use-cases.md` §8 carries the same two triggers (v1.2.13), because §8 owns those decisions and this tracker only schedules them. **This section exists because "worth revisiting later" is how the Asgardeo row sat for months** ([Blocked](#blocked) tells that story); a trigger someone can actually observe is the difference between a parked decision and a forgotten one.

**Incidents' phase — resolved 11 Aug 2026.** UC §9.1's phase lists named incidents nowhere — not First, Second or Third — while **G-2, "one accident", is a golden fixture that must reproduce**, and this tracker had been assuming phase one on exactly that basis. Asked directly rather than left open: **confirmed phase one**. `use-cases.md` v1.2.6 §9.1 now names incidents (UC-12) in the First row; `user-flows.md`'s F-3.4 never disagreed (`*Phase:* 1` throughout) — the omission was §9.1's alone, now corrected.

## Adding an entry

A finished item becomes **one row** in §2 or §3 — what it delivers, its proof with real counts, its gap ids — not a section. Anything it did not build becomes a row in §4 **with a track**. Anything that will cost a future session an hour to rediscover goes in §5. The narrative belongs in the commit message; this file is the index.
